Refutes only removal of the initialization condition, not the base-bounded family screen or a general finite-character/profile claim.", "evidence": "# An actual SAME-H_m cap return outside the screened initialization range\n\nStatus: **UNVERIFIED joint counterexample**, within the one selected binary\ncharacter-pyramid mechanism. This tests a scope extension, not a second\nallocated policy or a claim of favorable terminal performance. No trajectory,\narithmetic evaluation or recurrence was executed; all formulas are textual.\n\nThe false extension is: for the full capped binary-character pyramid, the\ncap can never reactivate after the first cone query on H_m, even if the\ninitial transverse coordinates are outside their base intervals. The\nbase-bounded hypothesis of the main joint screen is essential.\n\n## Full static function, word and real geometry\n\nUse the SAME original H_m, m>=1536, tau=1/4, with\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), e=1/(4m), beta=1-e,\n    c=lambda(1+e), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.\n\nEvery child retains these enclosing parameters. The full physical horizon\nis n=2^m-1 and the original denominator is B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 with every\noriginal split retained. No performance comparison to B_n is asserted here.\n\nChoose q=1, b_1=3, identity metric on R^2, and the full character orbit of\nZ/2Z with its two weights1/2 and real slopes\n\n    v_+=(1,3), v_-=(1,-3), both offsets0,\n    D=conv{0,v_+,v_-},\n    F(x)=max_(g in D){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.\n\nPut l=1+a, k_bridge=1+beta, T=2l+k_bridge, and start at\n\n    x_0=(T,3(1+T)).\n\nThis initial point is fixed from the known full word. Its transverse\ncoordinate exceeds the base width3, so it is outside the declared screen.\nIts carrier is positive and at least1. The function is everywhere finite,\nconvex differentiable and1-smooth: its gradient is the unique Euclidean\nprojection on D, whose two normal inequalities imply nonexpansiveness.\nComparing the attained maxima proves differentiability. D has interior,\nso its least smoothness is1. The cap is the physical\nstep1+e, and zeta^2-zeta=2A gives 2e^2+e=A, so0<e<sqrt A.\n\nFor clarity give the exterior constants separate names:\n\n    a=7/10, b=6/5, k=3/5, tau=3/5=k,\n    b1=1+aA, b2=1+bA.\n\nThe complete physical words are\n\n    H_F=(p_m,b1,q_m,tau,b2,q_m,1),\n    H_M=(q_m,b1,p_m,tau,b2,q_m,1),\n    H_E=(q_m,b1,q_m,tau,b2,p_m).\n\nWrite each as (L0,b1,L1,tau,b2,L2), with these COMPLETE lists:\n\n| Word X | L0 | S0=sum L0 | L1 | S1=sum L1 | L2 | S2=sum L2 |\n|---|---|---:|---|---:|---|---:|\n| F | p_m | A+e | q_m | A-1 | (q_m,1) | A |\n| M | q_m | A-1 | p_m | A+e | (q_m,1) | A |\n| E | q_m | A-1 | q_m | A-1 | p_m | A+e |\n\nThus N_F=N_M=3*2^m+2 and N_E=3*2^m+1, the ACTUAL horizons.\nFor F/M the final unit step is explicitly inside L2. For E the objective\ncap is explicitly inside L2. The front/middle objective cap in F/M is\ninside the indicated first/middle list. All internal q_m queries remain.\n\nThe following small bound will pay every endpoint guard:\n\n    (e+8/5)/A < 1/200. One static planar envelope, with every slope and weight specified\n\nDefine\n\n    v=(-3/8,sqrt7/8), ||v||^2=1/4,\n    t=t_X=A/(1+S0),\n    P=t*e1/A, Q=t*v/A, R=-k*Q,\n    D=conv{0,P,Q,R},\n    f_X(x)=max_(g in D){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}, x in R2,\n    x0=e1.                                          (2)\n\nThe local vector R in(2) is the last slope; it is distinct from the\nproject's scalar worst-case notation R_n=W_n/2.\n\nIn particular t_F=A/(A+e+1), t_M=t_E=1. M and E use the SAME\nstatic function(2); their different words yield different actual ends.\nThere is no function switching, new origin, rescaling of a module's\nstarting point, new metric or oracle-dependent choice.\n\nFor an explicit affine-plane specification, the support function is\n\n    J(u)=max{0,<P,u>,<Q,u>,<R,u>}.\n\nIts four plane offsets are0 and its four weights are1. The vectors P,Q,R\nare all printed in(2). The function in(2) is its unit quadratic envelope;\nthe definition by the attained maximum in(2) alone specifies it globally.\nThe positive endpoint heights below arise from the actual nonzero support\nvalues, not unprinted intercepts or an added constant.\n\nThe radicand7 is positive. P and Q are linearly independent because\nP has zero second coordinate and Q has positive second coordinate.\nSince R=-3Q/5, zero lies on the segment[Q,R]. The triangle with vertices\nP,Q,R has nonempty interior in the stated REAL two-dimensional metric.\nAll parameters, including t, depend only on the known complete word.\n\n## 3. Global convexity, C1 smoothness, complete minimum and TRUE radius\n\nFor every x the maximizer in(2) is unique: completing the square makes\nit the Euclidean projection g=P_D(x). The normal characterization is\n\n    x=u+g, g in D,\n    <u,z-g><=0 for every z in D.                     (3)\n\nTo justify this directly, for any z in D the squared-distance difference\nis ||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2=||z-g||^2-2<u,z-g>. Condition(3) gives\nminimality; the necessary inequality follows by differentiating along\nthe segment from g to z at its initial endpoint. Strict convexity of\nthe squared distance gives uniqueness.\n\nEquivalently g is a support maximizer for J(u). It is enough to check\nthe four vertices0,P,Q,R because every z in D is their convex combination.\nFor any such pair the exact value is\n\n    f_X(x)=<u,g>+||g||^2/2.                          (4)\n\nThe normals for x,g and x',g', applied with each other's projected\nvectors, give <x-x',g-g'>>=||g-g'||^2. Cauchy--Schwarz proves that\nthe projection map is1-Lipschitz. The two attained maxima bound\n\n    0<=f_X(x+d)-f_X(x)-<g,d><=<g'-g,d><=||d||^2,\n    g'=P_D(x+d).\n\nThus f_X is differentiable, grad f_X=P_D, and its gradient is globally\n1-Lipschitz and continuous. The function is finite everywhere because\nD is compact, and convex as a supremum of affine functions of x.\nOn the nonempty interior of D, projection is the identity, giving\nf_X(x)=||x||^2/2 there. Hence its least global smoothness is EXACTLY1.\nIt is nonquadratic: on an open normal cone beyond P the gradient is the\nconstant P, whereas it is the identity on the triangle's interior.\n\nSince0 is in D, f_X>=0. Its COMPLETE minimum set and minimum value are\n\n    Cmin={x:<P,x><=0,<Q,x><=0,<R,x><=0}\n        ={x:x1<=0, -3x1+sqrt7*x2=0},\n    min f_X=0.                                     (5)\n\nSufficiency follows since every g in D then has <x,g><=0.\nFor necessity, if one vertex z has <x,z>>0, a sufficiently small\npositive multiple eps*z belongs to D and makes\neps<x,z>-eps^2||z||^2/2>0 in(2). Opposite Q/R force equality in\ntheir common direction. In particular0 is an attained minimizer.\n\nFor any x in Cmin, ||e1-x||^2=(1-x1)^2+x2^2>=1, with equality only\nat0. Thus the complete minimum set's UNIQUE closest point to x0 is0,\nand the TRUE initial radius is1. This is independent of X and m.\n\n## 4. Every initialized first-list query and the first bridge\n\nAt each original query in L0, and at its FOLLOWING b1 query, let c be\nthe exact sum of the original entries of L0 already taken. All support comparisons\nin(3) are exhaustive:\n\n    H_P=<P,u(c)>, H_P>=0,\n    <Q,u(c)>=(-3/8)*H_P<=0,\n    <R,u(c)>=(9/40)*H_P<=H_P,\n    zero height=0<=H_P.\n\nHere H_P names only this displayed first-sector support height.\nAt c=S0 all support heights\nare0, but the unique projection at x=P is still P. This proves ALL\ninitialized updates by induction, including every internal q_m step\nand the objective cap when X=F. No module is replaced by a single oracle.\n\nAt the physical b1 query, x=P and g=P. Its ACTUAL update is\n\n    P-(1+aA)P=-a*t*e1=:XQ. No finite or all-depth list of these specified modes implies complete PSD.", "evidence": "# Conditional multiscale backward terminal paths: full construction and scoped failure\n\nJOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT — UNVERIFIED. Worker max, allocated invocation76\nafter ended baseline75, ended ceiling76 / attempted ceiling77, cap1.\nTHREE explicit laws are constructed. No formal gate, mathematical program,\nnumerical/arithmetic replay, executed trajectory/recurrence, optimizer,\ninstallation, consult or subagent is used. Controller completion and\ncounters are not observed. The original exact-profile goal remains OPEN.\n\nAll three laws are genuinely destination dependent: each original terminal\nreceiver has its own admissible dyadic nodes and its own normalization.\nThey use a backward path through THREE original boundary queries, rather\nthan an independent intermediate vertex. Law A uses every dyadic depth:\nit pays all first four-block screens, but a larger ancestor defeats\nthe entire old H4 plane. Law B uses even dyadic depths: its entire old\nH4 plane, actual root gradient plane and first four-block screens at\nEVERY relevant depth pass. A different, two-gradient zero-displacement\nvector defeats its FULL original matrix. Law C appends the original\nfirst query as a relay; the named screens still pass, but its original\nfirst-gradient diagonal is negative. Only these three specified laws\non this specified base are excluded.\n\n## 1. Original target, physical word and authority\n\nFor a nonnegative length-n word h and L>0, W_n(h) is the supremum of\n2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over all finite-dimensional\nEuclidean spaces, convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz\ngradient and a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, using the actual GD\nupdates x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R. Define the original\nall-split benchmark by\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nEvery split k is an integer. Fix ONE enclosing integer m>=1536 and put\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    Hword_0=(), Hword_(j+1)=(Hword_j,1+a*c^j,Hword_j),\n    n_j=2^j-1, S_j=sum Hword_j, A_j=1+S_j,\n    d_j=a*c^j, D_j=2A_j-1, t_j=d_j/A_j, t_star=1/lambda.\n\nHword_j denotes a PHYSICAL word, not the conjugate quadratic H_j in\nfact1252b8823bb5a290. All child words keep the SAME enclosing m and\nepsilon. The final physical word is h=Hword_m=H_(m,1/4), n=n_m,\nD=D_m. No child reset, coordinate compression or horizon change occurs.\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290 was read in full and supplies the original\ninterpolation convention and the exact terminal-square theorem.\nAccepted04219554e41d9750 Sections10–11 supplies the full primitive and\nobjective conventions. Accepted5fd396eba75651e7 Section10 supplies ONLY\n\n    B_(n_m)/D_m<237/250, m>=1536.                         (1)\n\nAn actual full-function upper zeta_m/D_m with sup zeta_m<250/237\nwould produce an original benchmark separation on unbounded horizons.\nThis report produces no such upper. Restricted witness-family values\nand negative formal arrays are not W upper bounds.\n\nFULL9161eec3956e6969 and scoped companions e66de81a517b1e1d,\n18deb80e726bbbcc,37353de9815ac111,c20e154f37432d63,ffe859645ec57eec\nwere recalled as UNVERIFIED awareness. The full shared evidence equals\nthe local prior manuscript read in bounded portions. No formula from\nit is used as an accepted brick: all needed algebra is derived jointly\nbelow. Guidance87f657463efe08da and plan65f1af923eccb229 govern this\nbounded invocation; its initial one-law selection is followed by the\nsecond and third permitted laws after the audits described below,\nrecorded in plan95b53e237fb46483. Latest guidancebd2b8e8fc46ee8bb\nexplicitly leaves max76 unchanged; no new allocation is inferred.\n\n## 2. Full original matrix and total common/contact prices\n\nNormalize smoothness1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Keep every independent\nformal vector (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), including optimum, terminal, every bridge\nand any zero-step query. Put\n\n    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, Ppos_i=sum_(t<i)h_t,\n    S=sum h, D=1+2S, h_n=0,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nActual legal samples have Q_ij<=0 by accepted1252b8823bb5a290,\nproof paragraph1. Let K_ij be finite-node multipliers, nu_i outgoing\noptimum multipliers i->star, xiCert_i incoming optimum multipliers\nstar->i, and K_ii=0. Full value cancellation is\n\n    sum_j K_ij-sum_j K_ji+nu_i-xiCert_i=2*1_(i=n).        (2)\n\nAfter cancellation, the ENTIRE original certificate is\n\n    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum K_ij Q_ij\n                       +sum nu_i Q_(i,star)+sum xiCert_i Q_(star,i)\n        =q||x_0||^2-sum nu_i<x_0,g_i>+Cgrad(g).          (3)\n\nWriting out_i=nu_i+sum_j K_ij, its full gradient matrix is\n\n    Cgrad_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i sum_(v>i)K_iv,\n    2Cgrad_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j sum_(v>j)K_iv\n                            +h_i[nu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. (4)\n\nIndeed half the incident multiplier mass at i equals out_i-1_(i=n)\nby(2). A forward position term contributes -h_i K_iv on its source\ndiagonal. For i<j the difference-square terms give -(K_ij+K_ji),\nsource i's later destinations give the second term, and source j's\nearlier destinations and optimum row give the last term. This derives\nALL diagonals, off-diagonals and the initial mixed column -nu/2.\nFor q>0, full PSD is equivalent to\n\n    Cgrad-nu nu^T/(4q)>=0 on the ENTIRE gradient space.    (5)\n\nKeep both optimum directions explicit before selecting xiCert=0.\nLet e be the all-ones gradient column, csum=Cgrad e,\nX=sum xiCert_i, E=sum(1+Ppos_i)xiCert_i, Hprice=D X-E.\nSumming(2) gives sum nu=2+X. Multiplying(2) by Ppos_i and summing\ngives sum K_ij(Ppos_i-Ppos_j)=2S-sum(nu_i-xiCert_i)Ppos_i.\nConsequently\n\n    e^T Cgrad e=D+E,\n    M(x_0=r,g=t e)=q r^2-(2+X)r t+(D+E)t^2.           (6)\n\nThe one-sided Huber function is zero on z<=0, z^2/2 on[0,1], and\nz-1/2 on z>=1. Its gradient is1-Lipschitz and its minimum is zero.\nStarting at x_0=D gives all gradients1 and all positions D-Ppos_i>=1.\nAll finite and outgoing optimum residuals vanish; the incoming one is\nQ_(star,i)=-(2S-Ppos_i). At q=zeta/D,\n\n    M(D,e)=(zeta-1)D-Hprice.                            (7)\n\nThus Hprice<=(zeta-1)D is ONE whole-word allowance. Completing the\ninitial/common block, for a transverse gradient vector v with e^T v=0,\nrequires the exact charge\n\n    Price(v)=[(D+E)(nu^T v)^2/4\n      -(2+X)(nu^T v)(csum^T v)/2+q(csum^T v)^2]\n          /[q(D+E)-(2+X)^2/4]                         (8)\n\nwhen the denominator is positive. It is obtained by inverting the\n2-by-2 matrix in(6), with coupling(-nu^T v/2,csum^T v).\nWhen that matrix is singular, the coupling must annihilate its kernel.\nNo common, initial or singular direction is deleted.\n\nAll three laws choose every xiCert_i=0 and preserve every nu_i. Then\nsum nu=2, e^T Cgrad e=D, Hprice=0. Set\n\n    dcontact=csum-D nu/2, e^T dcontact=0.\n\nFor zeta>1, the full charge becomes\n\n    Price(v)=(csum^T v)^2/D\n                    +(dcontact^T v)^2/[D(zeta-1)].     (9)\n\nFor example zeta=101/100 has the coefficient100/D on the second\nsquare. These are exact retained expenses, not claimed paid estimates.\nAn original negative direction with x_0=0 survives every inflation of q.\n\n## 3. Explicit base signs and value flow\n\nWe prescribe a base, not a PSD-selection oracle. Its coefficients are\nclosed finite sums/products over actual dyadic nodes. Summing the word\ngives\n\n    v_h=a/(c-2),\n    A_j=v_h c^j+(1-v_h)2^j,\n    t_j=(c-2)/[1+(1/v_h-1)(2/c)^j].                   (10)\n\nHere0<v_h<1 because c(c-2)>1>beta. Therefore t_j increases and\nt_j<c-2<1/2. Exact small identities are\n\n    b=1+a=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],\n    A_1=lambda-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)], d_1=1-epsilon,\n    A_2=lambda^2-epsilon[1+4/(lambda(1+epsilon))],\n    d_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2), d_3=c*d_2,\n    lambda d_1-A_1=-epsilon[lambda-2/(lambda(1+epsilon))],\n    lambda d_2-A_2=epsilon[1+4/(lambda(1+epsilon))-lambda^2 epsilon]. (11)\n\nThe first bracket is positive and the second is positive, using\n12/5<lambda<5/2 and epsilon<=1/6144. Also t_0<t_star. Hence\n\n    t_0,t_1<t_star<t_2,\n    0<t_j-t_star<lambda epsilon for j>=2,\n    A_(j+1)>lambda A_j for j>=2, A_j>=2^j.            (12)\n\nThe last inequality follows from A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j. Elementary rational\nenclosures707/500<sqrt(2)<283/200 in(11) also give\n\n    7/5<b<sqrt(2)<10/7,\n    582/100<A_2<584/100,\n    241/100<d_2<242/100, d_3<584/100,\n    A_3<15, A_2+d_2<9.                              (13)\n\nFor instance A_2=3+2b-epsilon and the displayed formula for b give\nthe A_2 enclosure directly; d_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2) gives its interval;\nd_3=lambda^2(1-epsilon^2)(1+epsilon)<584/100 follows by inserting\nlambda<483/200 and epsilon<=1/6144. All are textual rational inequalities.\n\nDefine a primitive finite array Lambda^j on0..n_j, starting Lambda^0=0.\nAt the join Hword_(j+1)=(Hword_j,1+d_j,Hword_j), let p be the bridge,\nz its terminal, and i range over nonterminal queries of its right child.\nEmbed the left array with weight1 and the right array with weight k_j;\nadd the four row types\n\n    i->p: u_j h_i,   i->z: v_j h_i,\n    z->p: w_j,       p->z: z_j,\n    u_j=2lambda,\n    v_j=[2lambda d_j+2(A_j+d_j)]/A_j,\n    k_j=2+lambda+(lambda+1)t_j,\n    w_j=4A_j+2d_j-2lambda(A_j-1), z_j=2A_j.          (14)\n\nAll other additions are zero and overlapping rows are summed. The\nunrolled coefficient of each original pair is the finite sum of these\nfour row types over every dyadic node, multiplied by k_l over exactly\nthe ancestors in whose right child the node lies; left ancestor factors\nare1. This specifies every coefficient without computing a recurrence.\n\nAll rows are nonnegative: u,v,k,z are positive and\nw_j=2lambda+2(d_j-A_j/lambda). For j>=2 use(12); for j=0 use\nw_0=4+2a; for j=1 use A_1<lambda and w_1>2lambda-2>0.\nThe identities u_j+v_j=2(k_j-1) and\nu_j(A_j-1)+w_j-z_j=2(A_j+d_j) prove divergence -2h_i\nat each nonterminal query and2(A_(j+1)-1) at its terminal.\n\n## 4. All-direction source and base identities, jointly expanded\n\nDefine the complete source polynomial\n\n    V_j=2sum_(i<n_j)h_i(f_i-f_(n_j))\n          +sum_(i<n_j)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n          -A_j(A_j-1)||g_(n_j)||^2,\n    Psrc_j=V_j+sum Lambda^j_iv Q_iv.\n\nAt one join put r=sum_(i right,i<z)h_i g_i,\nY=r-(A_j-1)g_z, Z=g_p-g_z. The four added row polynomials in(14),\nbefore their scalar coefficients, are respectively\n\n    d_j<r,g_p>+||r||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2\n                                     +(A_j-1)||g_p||^2/2;\n    -||r||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2\n                                     +(A_j-1)||g_z||^2/2-<r,g_z>;\n    d_j<g_z,g_p>+<g_z,r>+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2;\n    -(d_j+1/2)||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2/2-<g_p,r>-<g_p,g_z>.\n\nTheir r^2,r*g_p,r*g_z coefficients are(u_j-v_j)/2,\nu_j d_j-z_j,w_j-v_j. Adding the displayed V_j and child polynomials\nand using(14) yields the identity on ALL original gradients\n\n    Psrc_(j+1)=Psrc_j^left+k_j Psrc_j^right\n       +(t_star-t_j)[(lambda+1)||Y||^2\n          -2lambda A_j<Y,Z>+A_j^2(2+t_star+t_j)||Z||^2]. (15)\n\nThe bracket is positive definite: its determinant divided by A_j^2\nis(lambda+1)(2+t_star+t_j)-lambda^2>=lambda>0. Since t_j>t_star\nfor j>=2, this identity does not prove positive-epsilon child PSD.\nAn all-common child has Psrc_j=0 and zero derivative by(15).\n\nDefine the complete objective base Kbase^j,mubase^j by\nKbase^0=0,mubase^0_0=2,xiCert=0. At the same join put\n\n    A=A_j-1, bjoin=1+d_j, Dp=D_(j+1), Dc=D_j,\n    s_j=(A+bjoin)/(3A+2bjoin), theta_j=1-s_j.\n\nWith original maps L_l=l,p=L_(n_j),R_i=n_j+1+i, prescribe\n\n    Kbase_(L_l,L_v)=Lambda^j_lv/Dp,\n    Kbase_(R_i,R_v)=Kbase^j_iv,\n    Kbase_(R_i,p)=s_j mubase^j_i,\n    mubase_(L_l)=2h_l/Dp for l<n_j,\n    mubase_p=2s_j-2A/Dp,\n    mubase_(R_i)=theta_j mubase^j_i.                  (16)\n\nUnlisted finite rows and all incoming optimum rows are zero. The bridge\noptimum coefficient is positive because\n(A+bjoin)Dp-A(3A+2bjoin)=A^2+4A*bjoin+2bjoin^2+A+bjoin>0.\nHere s_0=1/2 and1/3<s_j<1/2 for j>=1. Left primitive divergence\nis canceled by its optimum coefficients and the bridge; each right row\nkeeps its flow since s_j+theta_j=1. This proves(2) and sum mubase=2.\nThe original terminal has no incoming finite rows.\n\nFor a closed original-index formula at depth r, each right-spine node\nof depth l has offset o_l=2^r-2^l and bridge\np_l=n_r-2^(l-1),1<=l<=r. Include Lambda^(l-1)/D_l on its\nleft block[o_l,p_l], and s_(l-1)mubase^(l-1)_i from each right\nquery, including the final query, to p_l. These are all finite rows.\nThe optimum row at i<p_l in that left block is\n(2h_i/D_l)product_(t=l)^(r-1)theta_t; at p_l it is\n[2s_(l-1)-2(A_(l-1)-1)/D_l]product_(t=l)^(r-1)theta_t.\nAt n_r it is2product_(t=0)^(r-1)theta_t. The same closed formula\nwithin each indicated child supplies every mubase in the spine rows.\n\nLet astar_r=product_(t=0)^(r-1)theta_t, astar_0=1. Every original\nterminal outgoing probability is therefore explicitly\n\n    alpha_star^base=astar_r,\n    alpha_(p_l)^base=s_(l-1)astar_(l-1),1<=l<=r,\n    alpha_i^base=0 otherwise.                        (17)\n\nThey sum to1 since s_j astar_j=astar_j-astar_(j+1).\n\nTo retain the initial and all inherited mixed terms, put\nr_R=sum_i mubase^j_i g_(R_i),\nx_p=x_0-sum_(l<n_j)h_l g_(L_l),\nRchild_R=Cbase^j(g_R)-Dc||r_R||^2/4, kappa_j=Dc-Dp theta_j^2.\nAfter extracting the source, the remaining full polynomial is\n\n    ||x_p||^2/Dp+2(A/Dp-s_j)<x_p,g_p>\n      +(A^2/Dp+2s_j)||g_p||^2-theta_j<x_p,r_R>\n      +(bjoin-s_j)<g_p,r_R>+Dc||r_R||^2/4.\n\nUsing s_j(3A+2bjoin)=A+bjoin completes it to\n\n    Mbase_(j+1)(1/Dp)=Psrc_j(g_L)/Dp+Rchild_R\n      +||x_p+(A-Dp s_j)g_p-(Dp theta_j/2)r_R||^2/Dp\n      +(kappa_j/4)||r_R-2g_p||^2.                    (18)\n\nNeither Rchild_R nor kappa_j is assumed nonnegative at epsilon>0.\nAt x_0=Dp,g=e, the source and all displayed squares have value and\nderivative zero, by the inherited contact and sum mubase=2. Starting\nfrom the empty square proves the BASE contact identity\n\n    Cbase e=D mubase/2.                              (19)\n\nIt is an algebraic contact identity, not a PSD inference.\n\n## 5. THREE complete conditional destination/scale-dependent laws\n\nA dyadic node inside Hword_r of depth d and offset o means the actual\nembedded child word Hword_d with query indices o..o+n_d. These nodes\nare specified equivalently by offsets o=k*2^d,0<=k<2^(r-d).\nFor each PROPER node of depth d=s+2>=4, define three original queries\n\n    i1(vnode)=o+2^s-1,\n    i2(vnode)=o+2^(s+1)-1,\n    i3(vnode)=o+3*2^s-1,\n    end(vnode)=o+2^(s+2)-1,\n    wt(vnode)=A_s.                                   (20)\n\nThe node label vnode is distinct from an original receiver index v.\nThe physical steps at these three queries are1+d_s,1+d_(s+1),1+d_s.\nFor each finite base terminal receiver v=p_l, make exactly these THREE\nchoices of eligible sets and total fractions:\n\n    Law A: Elig_v contains ALL proper dyadic vnode of depth s+2>=4\n           with end(vnode)<=v; rhoPath=256epsilon=64/m<=1/24.\n    Law B: Elig_v contains exactly the EVEN-depth proper vnode,\n           depth=s+2>=4 with s even and end(vnode)<=v;\n           rhoPath=4096epsilon=1024/m<=2/3.\n    Law C: SAME Elig_v, ZPath_v, conditional probabilities and fraction\n           as Law B, but append ORIGINAL query0 to its prefix path.\n\nFor each specified law separately, set\n\n    ZPath_v=sum_(vnode in Elig_v) A_s,\n    cond_(vnode|v)=A_s/ZPath_v on Elig_v, zero elsewhere. (21)\n\nFor r>=5 every ZPath_v is positive in ALL three laws: the first depth4\nnode ends at15<=p_r<=p_l and is allowed in both choices. For r<=4\nthe eligible sets are empty and all three laws make no change. At r>=5 set\n\n    omegaPath_(v,vnode)=rhoPath alpha_v^base A_s/ZPath_v. (22)\n\nFor every listed v and vnode, replace exactly2omegaPath of the old\nterminal edge n_r->v by the FOUR edges\n\n    n_r->i3(vnode)->i2(vnode)->i1(vnode)->v.           (23)\n\nThis is the path in Laws A and B. Law C replaces the same terminal\nmass by the FIVE-edge path\n\n    n_r->i3(vnode)->i2(vnode)->i1(vnode)->0->v.        (23c)\n\nIts original indices are all distinct; i1>=3 and v>i3. In particular\nno coordinate at the first physical query is omitted or identified.\n\nEvery index is an ORIGINAL query and\ni1<i2<i3<end<=v<n_r. This changes both terminal and prefix rows;\nthere is no averaged primal trajectory or expected-orthogonality step.\nEligibility and normalization depend on v; node mass depends on s.\nFor example the last receiver's prefix admits right-child nodes that\nthe root receiver's prefix excludes. The law is not separable pi_i*alpha_v.\n\nHere are every FINAL terminal probability and finite/optimum multiplier,\nwith n=n_r and alpha_v=alpha_v^base. For r>=5,\n\n    alpha_star^path=astar_r,\n    alpha_i^path=(1-rhoPath)alpha_i\n       +sum_(v,vnode)omegaPath_(v,vnode)*1_(i=i3(vnode)), i<n,\n    Kpath_(n,i)=2alpha_i^path, nupath_n=2astar_r,\n    Kpath_(i,n)=0 for i<n,\n    Kpath_(u,v)=Kbase_(u,v)\n       +2sum_(w,vnode)omegaPath_(w,vnode)*[\n          1_(u=i3(vnode),v=i2(vnode))\n         +1_(u=i2(vnode),v=i1(vnode))\n         +1_(u=i1(vnode),v=w)], u,v<n,u!=v,\n    nupath_i=mubase_i for i<n, xiCertpath_i=0 for all i. (24)\n\nFormula(24) gives Laws A and B exactly. Law C has the SAME explicit\nterminal probabilities, terminal rows and optimum rows as Law B, but\nin its finite-prefix bracket replace1_(u=i1,v=w) by the TWO indicators\n1_(u=i1,v=0)+1_(u=0,v=w). This gives every original Law C coefficient\nas the same closed finite sum with FOUR prefix edges per selected path.\n\nAll repeated original pairs are summed. No diagonal path edge occurs.\nAt r<=4 use the complete base unchanged, and at r=0 use the empty\ncertificate of1252b8823bb5a290. Formula(24), together with(10),(14),\n(16),(17),(20),(21), is a closed finite sum/product formula for every row.\n\nThe sole decrement at each actual terminal column v is\n2sum_(vnode in Elig_v)omegaPath=2rhoPath alpha_v. It is paid by that\nSAME original edge2alpha_v, leaving(1-rhoPath)2alpha_v>=0.\nNo prefix row or optimum row is decremented. All final signs are thus\nnonnegative. Each replacement path has exactly the original divergence:\nsource n has unchanged outgoing mass, destination v has unchanged\nincoming mass, and every internal path vertex has one incoming and one\noutgoing addition. Therefore every original value-flow equation(2) holds.\nInside the prefix its divergence is exactly2alpha_i^path, including\ncoincident occurrences from different paths. The terminal mass remains2.\n\nWith Tmass=1-astar_r, the ENTIRE terminal decrement is2rhoPath Tmass,\nthe shifted terminal probability is rhoPath Tmass, and the ENTIRE\nprefix addition is6rhoPath Tmass in Laws A/B and8rhoPath Tmass in\nLaw C. The respective total bounds are1/4,4,16/3 (strict in B/C).\nLaws B/C retain at least one third of each original terminal edge's\ncoefficient; Law A retains at least23/24. These are sums over ALL\nallowed scales in this ONE word. No objective allowance is renewed\nper node, and prefix mass is not itself a PSD or objective-slack bound.\n\n## 6. Complete path polynomial, terminal square and contact defect\n\nFor original indices n,k,i,v, direct cancellation gives\n\n    Q_(n,k)+Q_(k,i)-Q_(n,i)=<g_n-g_k,y_k-y_i>, y_i=x_i-g_i.\n\nThe values cancel. The position terms give <g_n-g_k,x_k-x_i>;\nthe gradient-difference terms give -<g_n-g_k,g_k-g_i>.\nApplying this identity successively along(23), or expanding its four\nrows, yields the WHOLE original polynomial\n\n    Mpath(q)=Mbase(q)+2sum_(v,vnode)omegaPath EPath_(v,vnode),\n    EPath=<g_n-g_i3,y_i3-y_v>\n           +<g_i3-g_i2,y_i2-y_v>\n           +<g_i2-g_i1,y_i1-y_v>.                    (25)\n\nThis is EPath in Laws A/B. Law C's complete path polynomial is\n\n    EPath_C=EPath_B+<g_i1-g_0,y_0-y_v>.              (25c)\n\nIt follows by the same triangle expansion of i1->0->v. Law C is\nconstructed directly from the base by(23c), so it decrements no original\nprefix row: the minus term in the comparison with B is paid by B's\nexplicit ADDED i1->v row, not an undeclared original edge.\n\nEvery original position in y uses the unchanged physical prefix. There\nis no x_0 contribution to a position difference, so the entire mixed\ncolumn remains -mubase/2. Equations(4),(24),(25) specify all coefficients.\n\nUsing the ACTUAL alpha^path and prefix rows in(24), accepted\n1252b8823bb5a290(c) gives the exact full terminal completion\n\n    V_path=x_n-sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j^path y_j,\n    R_path=q||x_0||^2-2sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j^path delta_j\n              -||V_path||^2+sum_(i!=j in J_pre)lambda^pre_path_ij Q_ij,\n    delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2, delta_star=y_star=0,\n    Mpath=||g_n-V_path||^2+R_path.                    (26)\n\nHere J_pre={star,0,...,n-1}; lambda^pre consists exactly of the prefix\nKpath and nupath, with every incoming optimum row zero. No earlier\nprefix interpolation pair is deleted. With\nw_t^path=astar_r+sum_(i<=t)alpha_i^path,\n\n    V_path=astar_r*x_0-sum_(t<n)(h_t w_t^path-alpha_t^path)g_t.\n\nThese are explicit finite coefficients, not a free feasible-set oracle.\nFull PSD requires PSD of the ENTIRE R_path, not a conditional covariance\nor selected-plane estimate.\n\nAt a common gradient, y_i-y_v=Ppos_v-Ppos_i. Differentiating(25)\ngives the FULL contact defect, since the base contact is(19): sum the\nfollowing column increments over all v,vnode:\n\n    at n:  omegaPath*(Ppos_v-Ppos_i3),\n    at i3: omegaPath*(Ppos_i3-Ppos_i2),\n    at i2: omegaPath*(Ppos_i2-Ppos_i1),\n    at i1: omegaPath*(Ppos_i1-Ppos_v).                 (27)\n\nFor Law C keep the n,i3,i2 increments unchanged and replace the last\none by increments omegaPath*Ppos_i1 at i1 and -omegaPath*Ppos_v at0.\nThese are obtained by differentiating(25c), with Ppos_0=0. Their sum\nis again zero; no original initial-point mixed row changes.\n\nEvery other component is zero for that path; overlapping components are\nsummed. Their total is zero. The n component is strictly positive because\nevery path has i3<v and all physical steps are positive. Thus the\nsingular zeta=1 common/initial block fails its required kernel condition:\ndcontact!=0. For zeta>1, substitute this full column in(9); the exact\ntransverse charge remains. Huber slack is exactly(zeta-1)D, the entire\ncommon block is [[zeta/D,-1],[-1,D]], and the initial Schur charge is\nmubase mubase^T/(4q). Nothing in these charges is claimed free or paid\nby the screens below. The final counterdirection evaluates the ENTIRE\npolynomial(25), including the terminal square and all these coordinates.\n\n## 7. Entire H2 and Silver calibrations\n\nAt r<=4 all laws have no path; their H2 calibration is the complete base.\nThe first original join gives\n\n    Psrc_1=(2-b^2)||g_0-g_1||^2,\n    Mbase_1(1/D_1)=||x_0-D_1(g_0+g_1)/2||^2/D_1\n                                 +(3-2b)||g_0-g_1||^2/4. (28)\n\nThese follow by substituting A_0=1,d_0=b-1 into(15),(18).\nBoth are PSD since b<sqrt(2). At H2 the physical bridge is2-epsilon;\nD_1>19/5,D_2<75/7, and\n\n    theta_1=(2b+2-epsilon)/(3b+4-2epsilon)<59/100.\n\nThe ratio increases in b and decreases in the bridge. Use b<10/7,\n2-epsilon>199/100, then cross-multiply. It follows that\nD_2 theta_1^2<15/4 and kappa_1>1/20. The ENTIRE H1 Schur remainder\nis PSD by(28), and(18) then proves full original-vector H2 PSD.\n\nAt epsilon=0, all rhoPath are0 and t_j=t_star at every depth, so Psrc_j=0\nby(15). Writing A=A_j-1 and bjoin=1+t_star(A+1), exact expansion gives\n\n    (3A+2bjoin)^2*kappa_j\n      =2(1-t_star)A^3+(6-8t_star)A^2\n        +(1+t_star)(8t_star-2)A+(1+t_star)^2(1-2t_star)>0. (29)\n\nEvery coefficient is positive because1/4<t_star<1/2. Starting with the\nempty square, use(18),(19) to prove FULL Silver PSD inductively.\nOnly in this proved epsilon=0 calibration is child PSD invoked.\n\n## 8. One exact enclosing-m normalization for every screen\n\nAt depth m let\n\n    Z_all=sum_(s=2)^(m-3)2^(m-s-2)A_s,\n    XiPath=sum_(l=1)^m alpha_(p_l)^base/ZPath_(p_l).    (30)\n\nThere are exactly2^(m-s-2) proper depth-(s+2) nodes in Hword_m.\nEvery eligible-node normalization is <=Z_all. Put Bmass=A_(m-1).\nBy(12), A_s<Bmass/lambda^(m-1-s). Therefore, using lambda(lambda-2)=1,\n\n    Z_all<(Bmass/2)sum_(r=2)^infinity(2/lambda)^r\n          =2Bmass,\n    D_m/Z_all>(4Bmass-1)/(2Bmass)>3/2.               (31)\n\nSince theta_j<=2/3, astar_m<1/100; for example m>=12 and\n(2/3)^12<1/100 suffice. Hence Tmass>99/100 and\n\n    D_m XiPath>Tmass*D_m/Z_all>297/200>7/5,\n    2rhoPath D_m XiPath>700epsilon in Law A,\n    2rhoPath D_m XiPath>11000epsilon in Laws B/C.      (32)\n\nThese are sums/bounds over the SAME entire enclosing word, not a\nseparate constant allowance at every scale.\n\nFor any test with x_0=0, zero total physical displacement and zero\ngradient at the terminal of the FIRST root left child, supported\nwithin that child, every base receiver and its y_v are zero.\nFor Laws A/B, all ALLOWED nodes in that root left child occur in EVERY Elig_(p_l); their\nomega coefficients sum to rhoPath XiPath A_s. Nodes outside it have\nall path-endpoint gradients zero. Thus(25) reduces EXACTLY to\n\n    D_m Mpath(q)=D_m Mbase(q)\n       +2rhoPath D_m XiPath*sum_(active nodes)A_s EPath_node. (33)\n\nEquation(33) concerns Laws A/B. Law C's additional origin-relay term\ncan be nonzero on nodes outside the root left child; its ENTIRE extra\nsum is retained separately in Section13. The named screens below\nidentify every active node. No change of\nnormalizer, child PSD estimate or locally repeated slack enters(33).\n\n## 9. Entire old two-dimensional original H4 plane\n\nPut Wloc=A_2+d_2,Uloc=A_2+d_3,rloc=Uloc/Wloc and\n\n    Edef=(t_2-t_star)A_2^2(lambda+t_2)>0,\n    delta3=t_3-t_star>0.\n\nChoose ORIGINAL scalar gradients u on0..3, v0 on4..7, w on8..11,\nand zero on12..n_m, with x_0=0 and Wloc(u+w)+Uloc*v0=0.\nCall this two-dimensional plane N and normN=u^2+v0^2+w^2.\nThere is no equality constraint on formal coordinates: this is a\nparticular assignment of their test values.\n\nThe two H3 joins and then the H4 join in(15) give the entire source\n\n    P_N=-Edef(u-v0)^2-k_3 Edef*w^2\n       -delta3[(lambda+1)Wloc^2*w^2\n        -2lambda A_3 Wloc*w*v0+A_3^2(2+t_star+t_3)*v0^2]. (34)\n\nFor the left H3, Y=0,Z=u-v0; the right H3 gives -Edef*w^2;\nat H4, Y=Wloc*w,Z=v0. All later all-left source joins have Y=Z=0.\nAll boundary and right variables in the final(18) vanish. Therefore\nD_m Mbase(q)=P_N for every real q. By(11)–(13),\nEdef<24epsilon,k_3<7,delta3<(5/2)epsilon,Wloc<9,A_3<15.\nBound the cross coefficient by338(w^2+v0^2), and use\n(u-v0)^2<=2(u^2+v0^2),lambda+1<4,2+t_star+t_3<3. This yields\n\n    P_N>-3000epsilon*normN.                         (35)\n\nThe first depth4 node has endpoints3,7,11. Its actual proximal points are\n\n    y_3=-A_2 u,\n    y_7=-Wloc*u-A_2*v0,\n    y_11=-Wloc*u-Uloc*v0-A_2*w.\n\nSubstitution into the THREE terms of(25), retaining y_v=0, gives\n\n    FPath_N^target=A_2*[A_2(u^2+v0^2+w^2)+d_2*u*v0+d_3*v0*w]. (36)\n\nIndeed -w*y_11+(w-v0)*y_7+(v0-u)*y_3 cancels the nonlocal\nu*w terms and leaves precisely the bracket in(36). This displayed\nthree-variable bracket is positive definite: by(13) and Young's inequality,\n\n    bracket >=[A_2-(d_2+d_3)/2]*normN\n             >(169/100)*normN,\n    FPath_N^target>9normN.                          (37)\n\nHowever Law A has ONE additional active node: the first depth5 ancestor,\nwhose FIRST selected boundary is query7 carrying v0, and whose other\nselected boundaries15,23 have gradient zero. Its exact energy is\n-v0*y_7=Wloc*u*v0+A_2*v0^2. Every larger ancestor's selected endpoints\nhave zero gradients. Thus the WHOLE Law A restriction is\n\n    FPath_N^A=FPath_N^target\n                +A_3[Wloc*u*v0+A_2*v0^2].          (38a)\n\nAt w=0,v0=1,u=-rloc, put t=d_2/A_2,q_3=d_3/A_2=c*t,\nq_0=t(2+t)>1. Direct substitution into(38a) gives\n\n    FPath_N^A/A_2^2=\n      [q_3^2-t(2+t)(3+t)q_3+(2+t)]/(1+t)^2<0.       (38b)\n\nIndeed q_3>q_0 by(10), while c<5/2 implies\n2q_3-t(2+t)(3+t)=t[2c-(2+t)(3+t)]<0. The numerator decreases\nthroughout[q_0,q_3], and at q_0 equals(2+t)(1-q_0^2)<0.\nThe base P_N is strictly negative by(34),(15), so Law A's COMPLETE\noriginal matrix is negative on this N vector for EVERY real q.\n\nThe depth5 ancestor is excluded in Law B. No other allowed node has\nnonzero endpoint gradients on N. Therefore its whole FPath_N^B is\nEXACTLY FPath_N^target. Using(32),(33),(35),(37), at every nonzero N point,\n\n    D_m Mpath_B(q)|N>96000epsilon*normN>0.           (38c)\n\nThis pays the WHOLE old plane at the actual global normalization for\nLaw B only. Before shared publication, the manuscript audit found\nthe missing depth5 term in the initial Law A draft. That draft's old\nplane positivity claim was withdrawn; (38a),(38b) give the corrected\ncomplete restriction. No false screen result is published as a fact.\n\n## 10. All relevant first four-block depths, with exact total action\n\nFor each integer0<=j<=m-3 set Kblock=2^j and Lblock=A_j+d_j.\nInside the FIRST actual Hword_(j+2), choose original scalar gradients\n1 on[0,Kblock-1],0 on[Kblock,2Kblock-1],-1 on[2Kblock,3Kblock-1],\nand zero on[3Kblock,n_m], with x_0=0. Both active blocks are the\nphysical Hword_j followed by its actual bridge1+d_j. Their displacements\ncancel exactly. Every later gradient and position, the optimum and\nglobal terminal included, is zero.\n\nPut E_j=(t_j-t_star)A_j^2(lambda+t_j). Identity(15) gives\n\n    P_j^four=-(1+k_(j+1))E_j\n          -(t_(j+1)-t_star)(lambda+1)Lblock^2,\n    D_m Mbase(q)=P_j^four.                          (39)\n\nThe first Hword_(j+1) has Y=0,Z=1; the third active copy gives the\nsame source; the Hword_(j+2) join has Y=-Lblock,Z=0.\nAt j>=2, all coefficients in(39) are losses, and(12) gives\n\n    -100epsilon*A_j^2<P_j^four<0.                  (40)\n\nIn detail E_j<(15/2)epsilon A_j^2,1+k_(j+1)<8,\nand the second loss is less(45/2)epsilon A_j^2; their sum is <100.\nAt j=0, both t_0-t_star and t_1-t_star are negative, hence(39)>0.\nAt j=1, |t_1-t_star|>(6/25)epsilon,A_1>12/5,lambda>12/5 imply\n|E_1|>3epsilon and1+k_2>5. Also(11) implies\nt_2-t_star<epsilon/5 and Lblock=A_1+d_1<7/2.\nThe negative second term is then <343epsilon/40, so P_1^four>6epsilon.\nThus every depth0..m-3 is explicitly accounted for.\n\nAt j=0,1 no selected node has a nonzero endpoint gradient, so the path\naddition is zero. At j=2 the only active node is the target depth4 node,\nand its EPath=2A_2. For j>=3 Law A's full weighted action in(33) is\n\n    FPath_j^A=2A_j^2+A_(j-1)(A_j-d_j)\n       +sum_(s=2)^(j-2)2^(j-s-2)A_s[2A_s+d_(s+1)-d_j]. (41)\n\nEmpty sums are zero, and FPath_2^A=2A_2^2.\nHere is the full node accounting. The target node has endpoint\ngradients(1,0,-1), giving EPath=2A_j. Its two depth-(j+1) children\nhave endpoint gradients(1,1,0) and(-1,-1,0), with paired energy\nA_j-d_j; their common weight is A_(j-1). Every smaller node of depth\ns+2 in a constant active block has all three endpoint gradients equal,\nso EPath=-g_i3*y_i3. Pair its first and third-block copies. At that\nfixed depth there are2^(j-s-2) node pairs. Palindromicity of Hword_j\ngives mean node-start physical prefix(S_j-S_(s+2))/2. In Hword_(s+2),\n\n    Ppos_i3=3A_s+d_s+d_(s+1)-1,\n    2Ppos_i3-S_(s+2)=2A_s+d_(s+1)-1.\n\nTherefore the paired sum is2A_s+d_(s+1)-d_j, giving the last term\nof(41). Every inactive block has zero endpoint gradients. Every larger\nancestor's selected endpoints are in zero-gradient blocks, so contributes\nzero. This accounts for ALL nodes and their actual prefix positions.\n\nFinally(12) gives\n\n    sum_(s=2)^(j-2)2^(j-s-2)A_s\n       <(A_j/4)sum_(r=2)^infinity(2/lambda)^r=A_j.\n\nDiscard the positive child term in(41) and the positive2A_s+d_(s+1)\npart of the last sum. Since d_j<A_j/2,\n\n    FPath_j^A>(3/2)A_j^2 for every j>=2.             (42)\n\nUsing the SAME XiPath and D_m in(32),(33), all these original modes have\n\n    D_m Mpath_A(q)>950epsilon*A_j^2,2<=j<=m-3.       (43a)\n\nFor Law B, retaining exactly the even s in the SAME complete node\naccounting gives\n\n    FPath_j^B=2*1_(j even)A_j^2\n       +1_(j odd)A_(j-1)(A_j-d_j)\n       +sum_(2<=s<=j-2,s even)2^(j-s-2)A_s\n                                     [2A_s+d_(s+1)-d_j]. (43b)\n\nFor even j, the proof of(42) applies with fewer negative descendants,\nso FPath_j^B>(3/2)A_j^2. For odd j>=3, use c<49/20 and\nt_j<c-2<9/20. Its positive child term is >11A_j^2/49.\nWriting r=j-s, its descendant exponents are odd r>=3, and\n\n    sum_(2<=s<=j-2,s even)2^(j-s-2)A_s\n       <(A_j/4)sum_(r>=3,r odd)(2/lambda)^r\n       <(125/264)A_j,\n\nbecause2/lambda<5/6 and the geometric sum is125/66. Their total\nnegative part is less(75/352)A_j^2. Hence\n\n    FPath_j^B>[11/49-75/352]A_j^2\n              =(197/17248)A_j^2>A_j^2/100, j odd.\n\nCombining its OWN same-word XiPath with(32),(33),(40) now gives\n\n    D_m Mpath_B(q)>10epsilon*A_j^2,2<=j<=m-3.        (43c)\n\nThe positive base action at j=0,1 remains unchanged. In particular\nthe prior actual larger j=m-3 mode is paid in BOTH laws, irrespective\nof its parity. This is an all-depth screen\ncalculation, not an assertion that these modes exhaust the original space.\n\n## 11. Actual root bridge/right-first gradient plane\n\nLet j=m-1,Bmass=A_j,p=n_j,qright=p+1. No path endpoint equals p:\nthe highest bridge p belongs only to the excluded whole-word root node.\nNo endpoint equals qright either: all selected endpoints are3 modulo4,\nwhereas qright is0 modulo4. In the test with x_0=g_n=0 and only\ng_p,g_qright nonzero, EVERY gradient difference multiplying y in(25)\nis zero in Laws A/B. The WHOLE path action is zero on this plane, including\nall remote receivers and all positions.\n\nFor an explicit full base check let pterm_j=Psrc_j with only its\nterminal gradient1. Substitution in(15) gives the finite sum\n\n    pterm_j=sum_(l<j)(t_star-t_l)F_l product_(v=l+1)^(j-1)k_v,\n    F_l=A_l^2(1+t_l)-2A_l+(lambda+1)>0.             (44)\n\nThis is an exact symbolic identity, not an executed recurrence. For l>=2,\n\n    k_l-(2+t_l)^2=(t_star-t_l)(1+t_l)<0,\n    F_l/A_(l+1)^2<=(1+t_l)/(2+t_l)^2<=1/4.\n\nThe first two terms are nonnegative. If the normalized diagonal is\nnegative, multiplication by k_l/(2+t_l)^2 in(0,1) increases it;\nif positive, the product stays positive. Each later decrement is at most\nlambda epsilon/4. Therefore\n\n    pterm_j>=-j lambda epsilon*A_j^2/4>-5A_j^2/32,\n                                                   j=m-1. (45)\n\nCollecting the original rows(16) with(4), or expanding(18) on the\ntwo-query restriction, gives\n\n    Cbase_pp=2s_j+[(Bmass-1)^2+pterm_j]/D_m,\n    Cbase_qright,qright=2/D_j,\n    Cbase_p,qright=b(1+d_j-s_j)/D_j.               (46)\n\nThe source terminal coefficient is A_j^2-1+pterm_j before insertion;\nthe right-first row has mubase_0=2b/D_j. These identities retain all\ninherited finite rows. Since Bmass>1250,2Bmass/5<d_j<Bmass/2,\nD_m<5Bmass, (45),(46) give\n\n    Cbase_pp>Bmass/6,\n    Cbase_qright,qright>1/Bmass,\n    7/25<Cbase_p,qright<2/5. Hence the entire specified\nroot bridge/right-first GRADIENT plane passes unchanged. This does\nnot remove the initial/terminal variables or claim their larger Schur\nspace is PSD: those variables and charges remain in(3)–(9),(25),(26).\n\n## 12. A different original two-gradient wave defeats FULL PSD\n\nFor EVERY integer2<=j<=m-3 let rbridge=2^j-1 and hbridge=1+d_j.\nUse the following scalar values for the ORIGINAL independent vectors:\n\n    x_0=0,\n    g_0=-hbridge/b,\n    g_rbridge=1,\n    EVERY other g_i=0, INCLUDING g_n.                (48)\n\nThe physical first step is b, the physical step at rbridge is hbridge.\nThus their exact displacement is b*g_0+hbridge*g_rbridge=0.\nEvery subsequent original point, including every base terminal receiver,\nis zero. The original query at rbridge has x_rbridge=hbridge,\ny_rbridge=d_j. No interpolable primal status is presumed or needed\nfor a test of full-space PSD.\n\nThe first Hword_j has only its first and terminal gradients nonzero.\nFor j>=2, their source cross coefficient is ZERO by(15): g_0 appears\nonly in the all-left child chain, whereas the source join variables Y,Z\ndepend only on the bridge/right/terminal gradients. Its first diagonal\nis2-b^2 by(28), and its terminal diagonal is pterm_j by(44).\nAt the Hword_(j+1) join, Y=0,Z=1. Every larger join has Y=Z=0,\nand(18)'s whole-word boundary and right variables vanish by the exact\nzero displacement. Hence the ENTIRE original base action is\n\n    D_m Mbase(q;48)=(2-b^2)(hbridge/b)^2+pterm_j-E_j. (49)\n\nWe bound its complete value from above without a child PSD claim.\nEquation(11) gives0<2-b^2<3epsilon and0<t_star-t_1<epsilon.\nAlso k_1<7,F_1<13, so pterm_2<34epsilon<2epsilon*A_2^2.\nFor l>=2 the normalized multiplier k_l/(2+t_l)^2 is in(0,1)\nand the new term is negative. A positive normalized pterm therefore\ncannot exceed its depth2 value; a negative one remains negative.\nConsequently\n\n    pterm_j<2epsilon*A_j^2 for every j>=2.           (50)\n\nSince A_j>5,d_j<A_j/2,b>7/5,\nhbridge/b<(7A_j/10)/(7/5)=A_j/2. With E_j>0, (49),(50) yield\n\n    D_m Mbase(q;48)<3epsilon*A_j^2.                 (51)\n\nNow account for EVERY selected path node in(25). Index0 is never a\nselected endpoint. The original bridge rbridge is the FIRST selected\nboundary of the first depth-(j+2) node. That node has EPath=-d_j,\nbecause y_rbridge=d_j and its other two endpoint gradients are zero.\nIf j>=3, rbridge is also the SECOND selected boundary of the first\ndepth-(j+1) node. Complete old/minimum/terminal rows and exact radius10(T+9/10)^2 paid; every whole-prefix completion also zero.", "evidence": "# A full-word counterexample to removing the acute condition\n\nStatus: **JOINT UNVERIFIED counterexample**, max2 invocation66. This is law A\nof the three-law screen, not a fourth law. It supplies a scope boundary and\nalso refutes the inference from a local cap return to terminal persistence.\nNo verifier, computation or executed trajectory is used.\n\nThe false assertion tested is: for a full binary-character pyramid, a\nnonzero polar normal r_p orthogonal to a chosen character ray v forces\nP_D(r_p+s*v)=clip(s,[0,1])*v for every real s, without an acute-body condition.\nConsequently a negative s would always be absorbing. The construction keeps\nthe stated normal and actual-function hypotheses and falsifies that conclusion.\n\nFix ANY m>=1536 and the SAME complete physical word\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), e=1/(4m), beta=1-e,\n    c=lambda(1+e), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1,\n    l=1+a, k=1+beta, H=1+beta*c, T=2l+k.\n\nEvery child keeps these enclosing parameters. All original steps are kept;\nthe first seven are l,k,l,H,l,k,l. The exact all-split benchmark is\nB_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, with its original recurrence; no denominator comparison\nis needed for this counterexample, since its full terminal payoff is zero.\n\nOn real Euclidean R^2 take the full Z/2Z character orbit, slopes\nv_+=(1,3),v_-=(1,-3), offsets0, weights1/2, and\n\n    D=conv{0,v_+,v_-}={(u,z):0<=u<=1, |z|<=3u},\n    F(x)=max_(g in D){<x,g>-||g||^2/2},\n    C=cone D, C^polar={(u,z):u+3|z|<=0},\n    x_0=(T,3(1+T)),\n    v=v_+, r_p=(-9/10,3/10), t=T+9/10.\n\nThis is one static finite convex dictionary and one global convex C1\n1-smooth function. Its unique gradient is P_D(x): the full normal comparison\nat two queries proves projection1-Lipschitz, and comparison of the attained\nmaxima proves differentiability. The body has interior, where its gradient\nis the identity, so the least smoothness is1.\n\nWe have x_0=r_p+t*v, r_p in C^polar and <r_p,v>=0. Its transverse coordinate\nis outside its base interval. The closest initial minimum is EXACTLY r_p,\nwith squared distance10t^2: x_0-r_p=t*v belongs to C and is orthogonal to r_p;\nfor every y in C^polar the nearest-point inequality\n<x_0-r_p,y-r_p><=0 holds. Moreover min F=0 and argmin F=C^polar: points in\nthe polar give only nonpositive objectives, while a small positive multiple\nof a positive-inner-product vertex gives a positive objective outside it.\n\nHere is the actual original cap return and the ENTIRE subsequent payoff,\nderived jointly rather than borrowed from an unverified prefix finding.\nAt the first three actual queries let C_i=T-sum_(j<i)h_j. Then C_0=T,\nC_1=l+k,C_2=l, all >1, and\n\n    x_i=(C_i,3(1+C_i)), g_i=v_+, f_i=10C_i+4, i=0,1,2.\n\nThe projection threshold at carrier1 is1-10C_i<0; its clipped transverse\ncoordinate is3. Their literal updates give\n\n    x_3=(0,3)=r_p+(9/10)*v,\n    g_3=(9/10,27/10), f_3=81/20.\n\nThe residual r_p is a full cone polar normal, orthogonal to g_3, so this is\nthe actual strictly uncapped positive-side projection. The NEXT original\nlevel2 bridge H gives\n\n    x_4=(-9H/10,3-27H/10)\n       =r_p+(9/10)(1-H)*v.\n\nIts scalar coefficient is negative. Nevertheless its actual projection is\n\n    g_4=v_-=(1,-3).\n\nIndeed x_4-v_-=(-9H/10-1,6-27H/10); its second coordinate is negative and\nits dot product with v_- is(36/5)H-19>0. Thus it maximizes its residual\nlinear form on the full D at v_-, rather than zero or the other vertex.\nThe full cap and opposite-side normal conditions hold. It has value\nF(x_4)=(36/5)H-14. For n>=0 and h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1))>=0,\nlet W_n(h) be the supremum of\n\n    2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L*||x_0-x_star||^2),\n\nover convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient, attained minimum,\na chosen minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under the ACTUAL updates\nx_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)*grad f(x_i). Multiplying\nthe WHOLE fixed objective by L gives exactly the same points with h_i/L,\nand exactly the same payoff. Every physical zero step is retained.\n\nThe original benchmark uses ALL integer splits:\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nThe requested comparison would be: for EVERY epsilon>0, ONE N_epsilon\nindependent of h, ALL n>=N_epsilon and ALL h>=0, V(h)>=(1-epsilon)/B_n.\nNo surrogate benchmark, exponent-only claim, family-only theorem, finite\nnumerical comparison, or limit along selected horizons substitutes for it.\n\nAccepted correctness premises, at precisely these scopes:\n\n* fact_id1252b8823bb5a290: original signed interpolation residual;\n  complete-prefix maximal conjugate completion, including every queried\n  value and gradient; its direct proof of smoothness and interpolation.\n* fact_idb98bb807d514e80d: the legal canonical/single-cut channel K_n and\n  the original all-split definitions.\n* fact_id5fd396eba75651e7: ONLY the exact SAME-m hierarchy definition\n  and Section10's unconditional numerical original-benchmark inequality\n  B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S_m)<237/250 at tau1/4,m>=1536. No prototype lemma, frozen F/M/E comparison, unverified table,\nor audit verdict is a correctness premise for the new laws.\n\n## 2. Law A: a real parameter rule from the whole word\n\nDomain of A and B: h contains at least two entries strictly greater than1.\nSelect the two largest such entries; rank by decreasing value, then by\nearlier original index to break ties. Write their chronological indices\nj<k. This is ONE specified pair, not an optimization over unspecified\ninterpolation or certificate data. Retain all original blocks and put\n\n    P=sum_(i<j)h_i, S=sum_(j<i<k)h_i, T=sum_(i>k)h_i,\n    a_sel=h_j-1>0, b_sel=h_k-1>0, M_sel=S+1,\n    sigma=1/(P+1), Delta=b_sel/2,\n    ell=(M_sel+Delta)/a_sel,\n    kappa=Delta/(2T+1), C_sel=M_sel+b_sel.\n\nHere ell,kappa are positive real numbers. They are fixed from h BEFORE\ndefining the function. Define the two finite guard charges\n\n    E_Q=M_sel*(ell+1)-a_sel*ell,\n    Z_max=C_sel-kappa                     if ell>=kappa,\n          C_sel-kappa*(T+1)               if ell<kappa,\n    E_R=a_sel*kappa*ell+Z_max*(ell-kappa),\n    chi=1/[ell^2+kappa*ell+(E_Q)_+/a_sel+(E_R)_+/a_sel].       (A1)\n\nThe symbol (u)_+ means max(u,0). All denominators are positive and finite.\nIn the real plane with e1=(1,0), e2=(0,1), set\n\n    v=(-ell*chi, sqrt(chi-ell^2*chi^2)),\n    p_A=sigma*e1, q_A=sigma*v, r_A=-kappa*q_A,\n    D_A=conv{0,p_A,q_A,r_A},\n    f_A(x)=max_(g in D_A){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}, x0=e1.             (A2)\n\nThus ||v||^2=chi and <e1,v>=-ell*chi. Formula(A1) gives\n\n    chi*ell^2<1, chi*kappa*ell<1,\n    chi*E_Q<a_sel if E_Q>0,\n    chi*E_R<a_sel if E_R>0.                                  (A3)\n\nThe radicand in(A2) is STRICTLY positive. There is no complex vector,\nindefinite metric, dimension limit, or free parameter left to choose.\nAll four support planes have offsets0 and weights1. The complete static\nsupport function is J_A(u)=max{0,<p_A,u>,<q_A,u>,<r_A,u>}.\n\n## 3. Law A: every initialized query, all support guards, actual terminal\n\nFor any compact convex D, the attained maximizer in\nmax_(g in D)(x.g-||g||^2/2) is the UNIQUE Euclidean projection of x\nonto D. For u=x-g its exact necessary and sufficient condition is\n\n    g in D, <u,z-g><=0 for every z in D.                     (A4)\n\nSufficiency follows by expanding ||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2. Necessity follows\nby differentiating that distance along each segment from g to z.\nIt suffices to check the vertices of D_A. We now check ALL four at\nEVERY original query, including selected bridges and zeros.\n\nFirst sector: for original i<=j, set p_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, 0<=p_i<=P.\nThen\n\n    x_i=(1-sigma*p_i)e1,\n    g_i=p_A,\n    u_i=x_i-p_A=sigma*(P-p_i)e1,\n    delta_i=<u_i,p_A>=sigma^2*(P-p_i),\n    f_i=delta_i+||p_A||^2/2.                                (A5)\n\nThe zero support is below p_A. The q_A comparison is nonpositive\nbecause its first coordinate is negative. The r_A comparison is\nsigma^2*(P-p_i)*(kappa*ell*chi-1)<=0 by(A3).\nThese include BOTH future slopes. At the selected query x_j=p_A,\nu_j=0; all support ties leave the projection gradient uniquely p_A.\nIts ACTUAL update is\n\n    x_(j+1)=p_A-(1+a_sel)*p_A=-sigma*a_sel*e1.                (A6)\n\nMiddle sector: for j<i<=k let mu_i=sum_(j<t<i)h_t, 0<=mu_i<=S.\nThen\n\n    x_i=sigma*[-a_sel*e1-mu_i*v], g_i=q_A,\n    u_i=sigma*[-a_sel*e1-(mu_i+1)*v],\n    delta_i=sigma^2*chi*[a_sel*ell-(mu_i+1)],\n    f_i=delta_i+||q_A||^2/2.                                (A7)\n\nThe middle height is PAID and strictly positive at EVERY such query:\n\n    delta_i>=sigma^2*chi*Delta>0.                            (A8)\n\nHence zero and r_A are below q_A. The comparison against the RETAINED\nold p_A is\n\n    <u_i,p_A-q_A>\n     =sigma^2*[-a_sel+chi*((mu_i+1)*(ell+1)-a_sel*ell)]\n     <=sigma^2*[-a_sel+chi*E_Q]<0.                           (A9)\n\nIf E_Q<=0 the last strict inequality follows directly from a_sel>0;\nif E_Q>0 it follows from(A3). Thus(A7) is the ACTUAL original gradient\nat every middle physical step, no matter its individual size. In\nparticular x_k=sigma[-a_sel*e1-S*v] and the second selected update is\n\n    x_(k+1)=sigma[-a_sel*e1-C_sel*v].                        (A10)\n\nLast sector: for k<i<=n let nu_i=sum_(k<t<i)h_t, 0<=nu_i<=T,\nwhere i=n is the actual terminal. Define Z_i=C_sel-kappa*(nu_i+1).\nThen\n\n    x_i=sigma[-a_sel*e1-(C_sel-kappa*nu_i)*v], g_i=r_A,\n    u_i=sigma[-a_sel*e1-Z_i*v],\n    delta_i=sigma^2*kappa*chi*(Z_i-a_sel*ell)\n           =||r_A||^2*(2T-nu_i),\n    f_i=delta_i+||r_A||^2/2.                                (A11)\n\nThe equality uses Delta=kappa*(2T+1), not an unpaid height assignment.\nThe least Z_i is C_sel-kappa*(T+1), and\n\n    Z_i-a_sel*ell=Delta-kappa*(nu_i+1)>=kappa*T>=0.\n\nConsequently zero and q_A are below r_A. Against the RETAINED old p_A,\n\n    <u_i,p_A-r_A>\n     =sigma^2[-a_sel+chi*(a_sel*kappa*ell+Z_i*(ell-kappa))]\n     <=sigma^2[-a_sel+chi*E_R]<0.                            (A12)\n\nThe choice of Z_max in(A1) is exactly the worst endpoint for this\nlinear expression, including ell=kappa. If E_R<=0 the inequality\nis immediate from its displayed negative term; otherwise use(A3).\nAll old, present, future and remote dictionary supports are retained.\n\nInduction on original indices using(A5)--(A12) proves every actual\nupdate. Nonnegative steps put partial masses inside the stated closed\nintervals even if individual entries are unbounded or unordered. Empty\nblocks, interior zero steps, trailing zeros, T=0, and k=n-1 are included.\nNo reset or rescaling of an inherited state took place.\n\nIn particular the UNQUERIED terminal and its REAL gradient/value are\n\n    x_n=sigma[-a_sel*e1-(C_sel-kappa*T)*v],\n    grad f_A(x_n)=r_A,\n    V_A(h)=2f_A(x_n)=sigma^2*chi*Delta^2/(2T+1)\n                         =(2T+1)*||r_A||^2>0.              (A13)\n\nT=0 gives zero proximal height at the terminal, but still the printed\npositive gradient-square objective. Its value was evaluated on the fixed\nfunction, not inferred from a preceding positive-height snapshot.\n\n## 4. Law A: global legality, every row, minimum, true radius, actual Gram\n\nThe projection normal inequalities at x,x' give\n<x-x',g-g'> >= ||g-g'||^2. Thus the projection is1-Lipschitz.\nEvaluation of the two attained maxima bounds\nf_A(x+d)-f_A(x)-g.d between0 and(g'-g).d, whose absolute value\nis at most||d||^2. This proves differentiability with continuous\ngradient g=projection_D_A(x), global convexity, and1-smoothness.\nThe nonempty interior of the triangle D_A has gradient x and Hessian I,\nso the least global smoothness is exactly1. The function is finite\neverywhere, and all parameters were fixed before GD.\n\nThe COMPLETE attained minimum set and minimum value are\n\n    C_A={z:z1<=0, <v,z>=0}, min f_A=0.                       (A14)\n\nIndeed all slope inner products are nonpositive there. Conversely a\npositive inner product with a vertex makes f_A positive by taking a\nsufficiently small positive multiple of that vertex in D_A. Opposite\nq_A,r_A force equality in their common direction. For z in C_A,\n||e1-z||^2=1-2z1+||z||^2>=1, with equality only at z=0.\nThus origin is the UNIQUE closest minimizer, TRUE radius1.\n\nFor EVERY ordered pair of actual samples, including terminal and any\nminimizer, define Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\nFor two projection samples the exact cancellation is\n\n    Q_ij=<u_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                                  (A15)\n\nThis uses the complete normal(A4), so it covers both orientations and\nremote pairs without a nearest-neighbor assumption. For a minimum z,\ng_star=f_star=0, Q_(i,star)=z.g_i<=0 and\nQ_(star,i)=-u_i.g_i<=0. Repeated actual jets have Q=0.\n\nAn explicit recipe gives EVERY actual Gram entry. Write every relevant\nvector as sigma*(a_u e1+b_u v); then\n\n    <u,w>=sigma^2*[a_u*a_w-ell*chi*(a_u*b_w+b_u*a_w)\n                                      +chi*b_u*b_w].         (A16)\n\nFor (x0,g0,...,gn), use coefficient(1/sigma,0) for x0, (1,0) for\neach first-sector gradient, (0,1) for each middle gradient, (0,-kappa)\nfor each last-sector gradient including gn, and(0,0) for origin.\nPositions have their explicit coefficients in(A5),(A7),(A11).\nThe two-dimensional metric in(A16) has positive determinant\nchi*(1-ell^2*chi), so these are actual REAL Euclidean Grams and are PSD.\nActual gradient repetitions at zero steps are retained, not deleted as\nformal coordinates in an upper certificate.\n\n## 5. Law B: complete retained prefix, six anchors, new true minimum\n\nKeep the SAME parameters, initial point, full original old prefix and\nterminal point as law A. Retain only actual QUERIED endpoints when\ncompressing affine anchors. Define a finite list of pairs(y,delta):\n\n    O:       (0,0),\n    P0:      ((1-sigma)e1, sigma*(1-sigma)),\n    Q0:      (sigma[-a_sel*e1-v],\n                       sigma^2*chi*(a_sel*ell-1)),\n    Qend:    (sigma[-a_sel*e1-M_sel*v], sigma^2*chi*Delta).      (B1)\n\nIf k<n-1, append the two RETAINED r_A-query anchors\n\n    R0:      (sigma[-a_sel*e1-(C_sel-kappa)*v],\n                                             2T*||r_A||^2),\n    Rlast:   (sigma[-a_sel*e1-\n                       (C_sel-kappa*(T-h_(n-1)+1))*v],\n                                    (T+h_(n-1))*||r_A||^2).   (B2)\n\nIf k=n-1, neither R0 nor Rlast is a queried anchor, so append NEITHER.\nThis distinction includes a final selected bridge and prevents inserting\nan unpromised terminal jet. Repeated anchors may be kept; they cause no\nchange of maximum. There are at most SIX anchors.\n\nFor each original prefix jet let y_i=x_i-g_i,delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.\nThe conjugate lower quadratic of accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 is\nexactly ||w||^2/2+y_i.w-delta_i. In each of the THREE sectors in\n(A5),(A7),(A11), BOTH y_i and delta_i are affine in its spent mass.\nFirst-sector endpoints are P0 and the zero anchor at the actual j-query.\nMiddle endpoints are Q0 and Qend, both actual queries. Last endpoints,\nwhen queried, are R0 and Rlast with last spent mass T-h_(n-1).\nEvery intervening prefix anchor is a convex combination of those two\nQUERIED endpoints. If the spent interval has length0 all its anchors\ncoincide. This proves equality of the FULL original prefix maximum with\n\n    H_B(w)=||w||^2/2+max_(a in(B1),(B2)){y_a.w-delta_a},\n    f_B(x)=sup_w{x.w-H_B(w)}.                               (B3)\n\nAll offsets and weights(1), the dimension2, metric and initialized\nprefix are fully specified. Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 applies\nto the whole prefix because(A15) proves every ordered row. It preserves\nEVERY preterminal x_i,g_i,f_i, including zeros and bridge queries.\nThus one f_B fixed BEFORE the actual GD run has exactly the original\nprefix by induction. It is globally finite, convex C1 and1-smooth.\nIt is the maximal completion of THIS prefix, not an unknown optimal\ntranscript or a black-box terminal oracle.\n\nThe new COMPLETE minimum set follows independently from(B3). H_B(0)=0,\nso f_B>=0. Exactly the zero-offset anchors are active at0 and\nargmin f_B=partial H_B(0), their convex hull. P0 has positive delta\nunless P=0, when its slope is0; the zero bridge anchor is already O.\nBoth Q deltas are strictly positive. If T>0, both r-query deltas\nare positive. If T=0 and k<n-1, all remaining steps are zero and\nboth zero-offset r anchors coincide at\n\n    y_R=-sigma*a_sel*(e1+ell*v), (y_R)1<0.                  (B4)\n\nIf k=n-1 there is no r-query anchor. Consequently\n\n    argmin f_B={0}                   if T>0 or k=n-1,\n    argmin f_B=conv{0,y_R}            if T=0 and k<n-1,\n    min f_B=0.                                             (B5)\n\nEvery minimum has first coordinate<=0. The distance calculation used\nin(A14), applied to THIS new hull, proves origin is uniquely closest\nto e1 and the TRUE radius is1. The old radius was not carried across\ncompletion without proof. A finite maximum of affine planes has an\nopen region of a unique winning plane; on its translated conjugate\nregion f_B has Hessian I. Hence its least global smoothness is1.\n\n## 6. Law B: complete finite terminal evaluation, both orientations, Gram\n\nHere is a closed finite real calculation on the already-proved SIX-anchor\ntemplate, not optimization of an unknown feasible interpolation/PSD set.\nAt x=x_n from(A13), enumerate every nonempty subset I of the known\nanchor list with at most3 affinely independent slopes. Choose its first\nanchor0 as base (this subscript means the subset's base, not necessarily O).\nFor a singleton put w=x-y_0. For a larger subset let D_I have columns\ny_a-y_0 and let e_I have components delta_a-delta_0. Set\n\n    K_I=D_I^T D_I,\n    lambda_I=K_I^(-1)[D_I^T(x-y_0)-e_I],\n    w_I=x-y_0-D_I*lambda_I.                                (B6)\n\nK_I is either a positive1x1 or positive2x2 matrix. Invert it by the\nordinary scalar reciprocal or determinant formula; all entries are\ngiven real scalar products in(A16). Retain the subset if\n\n    every lambda component>=0, sum lambda<=1,\n    y_a.w_I-delta_a<=y_0.w_I-delta_0 for EVERY retained anchor. (B7)\n\nA singleton has vacuous lambda conditions. The last inequalities,\nincluding all six anchors, are mandatory. Use the lexicographically\nfirst retained subset. Define\n\n    g_n^B=w_I,\n    f_B(x_n)=(x_n-y_0).w_I-||w_I||^2/2+delta_0,\n    V_B(h)=2f_B(x_n).                                      (B8)\n\nTo prove existence, maximize x.w-H_B(w). Quadratic growth gives\nan attained unique maximizer. Its optimality says\nx-w is in the convex hull of its active anchor slopes. A point in a\nplanar finite convex hull is a convex combination of at most3 affinely\nindependent slopes: whenever more than3 are present, an affine dependence\nof their coefficients permits moving weights until one becomes0;\nrepeat, and delete any remaining dependent coefficients. The surviving\nslopes satisfy(B6),(B7). Conversely(B6) imposes equal active-plane\nvalues and x-w=y_0+D_I*lambda_I. Conditions(B7) make this a convex\ncombination of ACTUAL active slopes. Thus x is a subgradient of H_B\nat w_I, proving optimality and(B8). All retained subsets yield the\nsame w_I because of strict convexity. This also handles tied faces,\nboundary weights, zero repeats, small/zero last step and T=0.\n\nFor ANY actual prefix/terminal/minimum pair, f_i=x_i.g_i-H_B(g_i)\nand x_i is a subgradient of H_B at g_i. Its1-strong convexity gives\n\n    H_B(g_i)>=H_B(g_j)+x_j.(g_i-g_j)+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nThe EXACT cancellation of the signed residual is\n\n    Q_ij=H_B(g_j)-H_B(g_i)+x_j.(g_i-g_j)\n                                        +||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0. (B9)\n\nThis is ALL directed and optimum rows in BOTH orientations, not only\nthe outgoing terminal rows used to find a completion. With h_(n-1)=0,\nx_n=x_(n-1), so the preserved jet forces(B8) to equal the last prefix\nvalue and gradient. No fresh objective charge at that repeated point\nis possible. The separately proved(B5) supplies the actual radius.\n\nEvery original gradient before n is still p_A,q_A,r_A. The terminal\ngradient is the REAL vector w_I in(B6), which need not equal r_A.\nIf w_I=(w1,w2), let b_w=w2/v2 and a_w=w1+ell*chi*b_w, so\nw_I=a_w e1+b_w v. Use(a_w/sigma,b_w/sigma) in(A16) for gn.\nThat formula specifies the FULL actual Gram, including every queried\ncoordinate. Its PSD follows from its displayed real positive metric.\n\nSince f_A is one legal interpolant of the same prefix and retained optimum,\nthe maximality part of fact1252b8823bb5a290 gives f_B(x_n)>=f_A(x_n).\nTheir two true radii were separately proved1. Thus V_B>=V_A; no illegal\ntrajectory average or exchanged radius is involved.\n\nOne useful quantitative upper for later diagnostics is also explicit.\nIf k<n-1, the LAST r-anchor alone in(B3) gives, for d=h_(n-1),\n\n    V_B<=2delta_Rlast+||x_n-y_Rlast||^2\n         =V_A+d^2*||r_A||^2\n         =V_A*[1+d^2/(2T+1)].                              (B10)\n\nThis is an upper only on THIS full-prefix completion and THIS paid\nprefix, not on unrestricted W. It retains the original last query.\n\n## 7. Law C: two fixed scalar curvatures, full prefix, sorted formula\n\nThis law is defined for EVERY word. For n=0 use f(x)=x^2/2,x0=1,\npayoff1. For n>=1 choose eta in the TWO-element set{1,1/2}.\nStart the old scalar quadratic eta*x^2/2 from1 and specify directly\n\n    xi_i=product_(t<i)(1-eta*h_t), 0<=i<=n,\n    gamma_i=eta*xi_i, f_i^old=eta*xi_i^2/2, 0<=i<n,\n    y_i=(1-eta)*xi_i, delta_i=eta*(1-eta)*xi_i^2/2.           (C1)\n\nThese are symbolic finite formulas, not an executed recurrence or\narithmetic replay. They include every original query with its value\nand gradient, any sign, order, size or zero. For old prefix pairs,\n\n    Q_ij=-eta*(1-eta)*(xi_i-xi_j)^2/2<=0,                    (C2)\n\nincluding the optimum xi_star=gamma_star=f_star=0.\nDefine the WHOLE completed function, fixed from h before GD, by\n\n    H_eta(w)=w^2/2+max{0,max_(0<=i<n)(y_i*w-delta_i)},\n    F_eta(x)=sup_w{x*w-H_eta(w)}.                            (C3)\n\nFact1252b8823bb5a290 and(C2) preserve every old prefix jet. The\nactual run has x_i=xi_i up to n, by physical-index induction; the\nterminal gradient is evaluated on F_eta, rather than assumed eta*xi_n.\nBoth completions are globally convex C1 and1-smooth. At eta1,\nH_1=w^2/2 and F_1=x^2/2. At eta1/2, delta_i=y_i^2/2.\n\nFor eta1/2 sort the distinct known slopes{0,y_0,...,y_(n-1)} into\nu_0<...<u_r; every original jet is still retained, and only identical\nplanes are merged for evaluation. For each adjacent pair define\ntheta_a=(u_a+u_(a+1))/2. Plane a wins exactly on the interval\n[theta_(a-1),theta_a], with the first interval unbounded below and\nthe last unbounded above. Indeed the equality of any two parabolic\nplanes is at their midpoint; ordering shows all more distant planes\nare below on the displayed interval. At x=xi_n set\n\n    w_a=clip(x-u_a to that interval),\n    E_a=(x-u_a)*w_a-w_a^2/2+u_a^2/2.                        (C4)\n\nClipping is the usual minimum/maximum with the finite endpoint(s);\nan absent endpoint imposes no bound. Choose the first a with largest\nE_a. Then\n\n    grad F_(1/2)(xi_n)=w_a, 2F_(1/2)(xi_n)=2max_a E_a.        (C5)\n\nThis is a finite explicit real rule on known scalar anchors after\nthe full original lift, without curvature optimization or a feasible-set\noracle. Strict concavity makes tied maximizing intervals give the\nsame gradient. Set\n\n    V_C(h)=max{xi_n(eta1)^2, 2F_(1/2)(xi_n(eta1/2))}.         (C6)\n\nThe minimum value is0 and the COMPLETE minimum set is{0}: H_eta(0)=0;\nfor eta1 all affine slopes are0, and for eta1/2 a zero-offset plane\nhas y_i=0. Therefore partial H_eta(0)={0}. The true radius from1\nis1 for each completion. The smoothness constant is exactly1 on a\ntranslated region of any unique winning plane.\n\nEquation(B9), with H_eta and scalar products, proves every directed\nprefix/terminal/optimum residual in both orientations. It includes\nall repeated queries and the actual terminal from(C5). The FULL Gram\nof(x0,g0,...,gn) is G_ab=z_a*z_b, where z_x0=1,\nz_i=eta*xi_i for i<n and z_n is the real terminal gradient from\n(C3)--(C5); eta1 gives z_n=xi_n. This is an actual scalar PSD Gram.\n\nFor n>=1, the LAST retained quadratic alone gives the template upper\n\n    2F_eta(xi_n)<=eta*xi_(n-1)^2\n                         *[1-2eta*d+eta*d^2], d=h_(n-1).    (C7)\n\nIt follows either by maximizing that one conjugate lower quadratic or\nby smoothness at the preserved last jet. It is a completed-family upper,\nnever a bound on unrestricted W. If d=0, the actual terminal is exactly\nthat preserved last jet and its value is the original quadratic value.\n\n## 8. A genuinely quantitative channel beyond K, not just a recursion\n\nTake the exact word h=(5,5); both selections have P=S=T=0,\na_sel=b_sel=4,M_sel=sigma=1. Direct symbolic substitution gives\n\n    ell=3/4, kappa=2, E_Q=-5/4,\n    E_R=4*2*(3/4)+3*(3/4-2)=9/4,\n    chi=1/(9/16+3/2+9/16)=8/21,\n    V_A=32/21.                                             (E1)\n\nHere K_2(5,5)=16/11 and V_A/K_2=22/21>1. The paid static\ntriangle therefore adds a channel beyond K. The same pattern also\nhas a symbolic gain for h=(b,b), b=1+A, real A>=16. Here\n\n    ell=1/2+1/A, kappa=A/2,\n    E_Q=(1-A)/2+1/A<0,\n    E_R=A/4+1+1/A,\n    chi=1/[A/4+1+2/A+2/A^2],\n    V_A=A^2/[A+4+8/A+8/A^2].                               (E2)\n\nThe formula for E_R in(E2) follows by expanding\nA*kappa*ell+(1+A-kappa)*(ell-kappa), since T=0.\nThe accepted K for this word is\n\n    max{1/(4A+5), A^2/(2A+3), A^2/(A+2)^2}\n             =A^2/(2A+3) at A>=16.                        (E3)\n\nFor A>=16, A+4+8/A+8/A^2<2A+3, because\n1+8/A+8/A^2<A. Therefore(E2) is STRICTLY above K.\nThis checks a nontrivial attained terminal, not a trajectory reset or\nan unpriced static projection recursion. B is at least A on the same\nword with its independently proved radius. C's unit quadratic can be\neven stronger on this example; no domination over all other channels\nis asserted. No mathematical program was used to evaluate any example.\n\n## 9. Precise portfolio and accepted special-word channel\n\nIf A/B's domain is empty put V_A=V_B=0 as unavailable channels, not as\nclaimed positive-radius constant-function witnesses. The accepted K is\n\n    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2s(h)),\n         max_j (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2*(1+2T_j)]}.            (P1)\n\nFactb98bb807d514e80d supplies every attained nonzero single-cut term:\nfrom1, derivative clip(x to[-a_H,c_H]) with\nc_H=1/(1+P_j), a_H=c_H*(h_j-1)/(1+2T_j), minimum0 and true radius1.\nThe full prefix gradient is c_H and the full suffix gradient is-a_H.\nIts explicit value is x^2/2 on[-a_H,c_H], c_H*x-c_H^2/2\non x>=c_H, and -a_H*x-a_H^2/2 on x<=-a_H. Before the\nselected original update x_i=1-c_H*sum_(t<i)h_t; immediately\nafter it x_(j+1)=-a_H*(2T_j+1), and at later spent suffix mass u\nx=-a_H*(2T_j+1-u).
