{"id": "a7ddcde42ed5a4f3", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T13:38:58.086208+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "UNVERIFIED max4 scoped physical counterexamples: every word (L0,1+aA,L1,1+bA,L2) with m>=24,A=(1+sqrt2)^m, nonnegative a,b, exact block lengths (2^m-1,2^(m+1)-1,2^m) and sums (A-1,(1+sqrt2)A-1,A) has an explicit single static function at its OWN closest radius1 with B_(2^(m+2)) V>1+1/40000. This includes the unequal-middle basic law and all within-block nonnegative rearrangements. The complete original certificate system is separated by the actual full Gram transcript. Original minimax theorem OPEN.", "evidence": "# Unequal-middle modules: a full-cone physical obstruction\n\nProject gd-exact-profile-20260926. Worker max4. Analytical invocation74.\nActual ended baseline73; ended ceiling74 / attempted ceiling75; cap1.\nSteering: TASK.md and master_guidance d3e800bc68686d6e.\nRequested transport/model regime: direct reasoning, consultOFF, Astra/ultra.\n\nSTATUS: ONE JOINT MATHEMATICAL DRAFT, ENTIRELY UNVERIFIED.\nNo assertion below is a new fact. No fact_submit, other formal call, mathematical\nprogram, executed trajectory/recurrence, arithmetic replay, optimizer, consult,\ninstallation, or subagent was used. The original minimax target remains OPEN.\n\nThis invocation opens ONE new candidate law, with unequal module levels, and\nstops retuning it after deriving its declared-domain obstruction. It does not\nreopen the old equal-basic F/M/E layout. The full previously shared exclusion\neea0512960806ccc was read for awareness only and is not a proof premise.\n\n## 1. Original problem and the precise scoped output\n\nFor every integer n>=0 and h in [0,infinity)^n, W_n(h) is the supremum of\n\n  2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2)\n\nover every finite dimension, every convex differentiable function with globally\nL-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum, every minimizer x_star, and every\nx_0 different from x_star. The actual iteration is\nx_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). L>0 is arbitrary. Thus W=2R.\n\nThe original all-integer-split benchmark is\n\n  Omega(c,d)=(c+d+sqrt((c+d)^2+4cd))/2,\n  G(c,d)=2c+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8cd)/2,\n  C_0=B_0=1,\n  C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n  B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),       n>=1,\n  U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nThe project asks whether W_n^*/U_n^ZJ tends to1, uniformly in the lower-bound\ndirection over all nonnegative words. A negative answer would require an ACTUAL\nfamily with a full-class upper bound below U_n^ZJ by a fixed factor at unbounded\nactual horizons. No such upper bound is supplied here.\n\nThe candidate exclusion proposed in this joint draft is instead:\n\nFor EVERY integer m>=24, EVERY finite a,b>=0, and EVERY physical word described\nin Section2, one explicitly selected static function in dimension1 or2, with\ncomplete minimum set and OWN closest initial distance1, has terminal payoff V\nsatisfying\n\n  B_N V > 1+1/40000,       N=2^(m+2).                       (1)\n\nConsequently W_N(h)>(1+1/40000)U_N^ZJ for this entire scoped family.\nIn particular, the FULL original-coordinate objective certificate system has\nno feasible solution at ANY level q_cert<=(1+1/40000)/B_N on this family.\nThis is a candidate physical obstruction to a negative construction, NOT a\nnegative resolution or an unrestricted affirmative result.\n\nAll new benchmark estimates, reductions, functions, trajectories and inequalities\nare derived together below. No unverified intermediate is imported by citation.\n\n## 2. The ONE physical law and its full block-sum scope\n\nPut lambda=1+sqrt2 and A=lambda^m. Define the actual basic words by\n\n  q_0=(),\n  q_(j+1)=(q_j, 1+(lambda-2)lambda^j, q_j),     j>=0.\n\nInduction gives length(q_j)=2^j-1 and sum(q_j)=lambda^j-1:\nthe next sum is 2(lambda^j-1)+1+(lambda-2)lambda^j=lambda^(j+1)-1.\nAll these entries are positive.\n\nThe explicit candidate is\n\n  H_m(a,b)=(q_m, 1+aA, q_(m+1), 1+bA, q_m, 1).            (2)\n\nThe middle block has level m+1, whereas the outside basic blocks have level m.\nThis is not one of the old three-equal-basic-module F/M/E words. Expanding the\nmiddle recursion prints four level-m lists with the bridge pattern\n\n  (q_m,1+aA,q_m,1+(lambda-2)A,q_m,1+bA,q_m,1).\n\nBoth displays specify the same literal physical word. No child is an oracle,\nand there is no restart, momentum, adaptation, averaging, or extra state.\n\nThe actual horizon and sum are exactly\n\n  N=(2^m-1)+1+(2^(m+1)-1)+1+2^m=2^(m+2),\n  S=sum H_m=(2+lambda+a+b)A.                               (3)\n\nIn fact the proof covers the following entire block-sum class, which contains\n(2). Write h=(L0,1+aA,L1,1+bA,L2), with EVERY entry of the three real lists\nnonnegative, retaining their literal positions, and require\n\n  length L0=2^m-1,        sum L0=A-1;\n  length L1=2^(m+1)-1,    sum L1=lambda A-1;\n  length L2=2^m,          sum L2=A.                        (4)\n\nFor (2), L0=q_m, L1=q_(m+1), L2=(q_m,1).\nThere is no restriction on internal order, individual positive lower bounds,\nor an upper bound independent of m. Zero entries, repeated query positions,\nand entries as large as an entire block mass are included. The exterior a,b\nmay be arbitrarily large and may depend on m. They are selected before the\nobjective and initialization.\n\nThe original zero-based bridge queries are\n\n  j1=2^m-1,      j2=3*2^m-1;\n\nthe final block begins at original query 3*2^m and ends at N=4*2^m.\nAll terminal formulas below refer to this N, not to either bridge or to N-1.\n\nThe extension from (2) to (4) is not a second candidate schedule law or an\nassumed module invariance. It follows directly from every physical query\nformula: only nonnegative partial sums and the three fixed total sums enter.\n\n## 3. Accepted inputs and retrieval scope\n\nAccepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original ordered interpolation\norientation, exact full objective-certificate existence, and its selected\nterminal-square parameterization, INCLUDING zero-step formal-coordinate\nhandling. Its full proof was read.\n\nAccepted factb98bb807d514e80d supplies the legal global-Huber/single-cut\nnecessary screen with W=2R. Its full statement and proof were read. The actual\nfunctions and all physical query formulas are reprinted here.\n\nAccepted fact04219554e41d9750 fixes the same ORIGINAL benchmark and contains\nfull original-space composition identities. The relevant identities and\nbenchmark arguments, as well as the full fact text, were read. No conditional\nimproving seed is presumed, and no saved numerical endpoint or program in that\nfact is executed. Section5 gives its needed benchmark bound directly from\nthe displayed defining recurrence; it does not use a stored finite DP table.\n\nThe former draft eea0512960806ccc was fully read in own-file bounded sections.\nIt is awareness, not an accepted predecessor. The frozen earlier prototype\n7d434affa1888c02 was neither changed nor used as a mathematical premise.\n\nSearch-math-results was used in mutation mode for the mature unequal-module\nupper-certificate program. One Matlas query returned bounded-step/self-contracted\nand unrelated optimization results; none supplied the required full long-step\ncertificate. Primary-source web retrieval recovered the original concatenation\nliterature. Exact OWN extracted text\ndownloads/zhang-jiang-2410.12395.txt was read at Definitions4,5,7, Theorem6 and\nits proof, Proposition8 and its proof, Theorems11,12, and Lemma15 INCLUDING its\nproof. Its primitive/dominant assumptions require actual all-function Lyapunov\ninequalities; a free oversized bridge is not licensed by the theorem. In\nparticular the positive denominator sum(right)+2-bridge in Lemma15 is an\nexplicit hypothesis, not a globally valid algebraic simplification.\n\nNo external theorem is a black-box premise of the new proof. The literature\nis used for construction context; accepted facts and the derivations below\nprovide all mathematical support.\n\n## 4. Complete canonical upper certificate, with no deleted directions\n\nThis is the full system attacked, not a displayed feasible multiplier array.\nThe physical obstruction will prove its infeasibility at the claimed improving\nlevels, so an improving array cannot honestly be supplied for this law.\n\nNormalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Let I={star,0,...,N}. Retain all formal\nvectors x_0,g_0,...,g_N independently, INCLUDING those at zero steps.\nFor each finite i define\n\n  x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nThere is a separate variable Lambda_ij for EVERY ordered distinct pair in I.\nPut Lambda_ii=0. The full sign and flow conditions are\n\n  Lambda_ij>=0,                          every i!=j,\n  sum_j Lambda_ij-sum_j Lambda_ji=2*1_(i=N),    0<=i<=N.\n\nThe star flow is then -2 by summing finite flows. All (N+2)(N+1) ordered\nmultipliers are retained. Define standard formal-coordinate columns\nE_x,E_0,...,E_N in R^(N+2), E_star=0,\n\n  X_i=E_x-sum_(t<i)h_t E_t,       X_star=0,\n  K_ij=sym(E_i (X_j-X_i)^T)+(E_i-E_j)(E_i-E_j)^T/2,\n  M(q_cert,Lambda)=q_cert E_x E_x^T+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij K_ij,       (5)\n\nwhere sym(Z)=(Z+Z^T)/2. The full certificate requirement is PSD of (5)\non the ENTIRE R^(N+2), not merely on a trajectory subspace, quotient, common\ngradient complement, or endpoint subspace. Initial, terminal, common and\nsingular directions are all present. Flow cancels the value terms in\n\n  q_cert||x_0||^2-2f_N+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij.                  (6)\n\nFor completeness, the exact selected parameterization from accepted\nfact1252b8823bb5a290 may also be used WITHOUT assuming its support of every\noptimum. Set J={star,0,...,N-1}; choose alpha_j>=0 summing to1 and all ordered\nnonnegative prefix multipliers lambda^pre_ij. Require finite prefix flow\nout_i^pre-in_i^pre=2alpha_i. Put\n\n  y_j=x_j-g_j, delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2, y_star=delta_star=0,\n  V_alpha=x_N-sum_(j in J)alpha_j y_j,\n  R_alpha=q_cert||x_0||^2-2sum_(j in J)alpha_j delta_j\n          -||V_alpha||^2+sum_(i!=j in J)lambda^pre_ij Q_ij.\n\nThen Lambda_(N,j)=2alpha_j, Lambda_(j,N)=0 yields the identity\n\n  (6)=||g_N-V_alpha||^2+R_alpha.                               (7)\n\nFull PSD is equivalent to PSD of the ENTIRE prefix form R_alpha. Every prefix\npair remains. No two-point support or zero remainder is assumed. We make no\nclaim that these restrictive choices would be lossless at earlier query cuts.\n\nDirect upper attempt1 was standard primitive/dominant gluing with all child\ndisplacement terms retained. Its known bridge formulas alone do not certify\nthe arbitrary a,b here or an improvement over the original maximizing tree.\nDirect upper attempt2 was the unrestricted system (5)-(7). The exact primal\nscreen below refutes the desired level even in that larger system. This\nsecond failure is a physical obstruction, not a failed sparse-array guess.\n\n## 5. A textual benchmark lower bound at the ACTUAL horizon\n\nWe derive, for every m>=24,\n\n  B_(2^(m+2)) > (27/2)lambda^m.                               (8)\n\nAll recurrences in this section are symbolic definitions and induction\narguments. None was numerically evaluated or executed.\n\nBalanced primitive splits give C_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j. The admissible dominant\nsplit into two children of length 2^(j-1)-1, together with monotonicity of G,\ntherefore gives a scalar lower comparison. Let\n\n  t_0=1,\n  T(t)=(t+sqrt(t^2+8))/(2sqrt(lambda)),\n  t_j=T(t_(j-1)),\n  t_inf=sqrt(2/(lambda-sqrt(lambda))).\n\nThen t_inf is a fixed point of T, since\n(lambda-sqrt(lambda))*t_inf^2=2. It lies between1 and2.\nFor example lambda>12/5, sqrt(lambda)<8/5 imply\nlambda-sqrt(lambda)>4/5, so t_inf^2<5/2<4;\nlambda<5/2 and sqrt(lambda)>3/2 imply t_inf^2>2>1.\n\nT is increasing, T(1)=2/sqrt(lambda)>1, and\n\n  0<T'(t)=(1+t/sqrt(t^2+8))/(2sqrt(lambda))<1/sqrt(lambda)<2/3\n\nfor t>=0. Thus 1<=t_j<t_inf and\n0<t_inf-t_j<(2/3)^j for j>=1, with the weak corresponding bound at j=0.\nInduction using sqrt(G(1,t^2))=(t+sqrt(t^2+8))/2 proves\n\n  B_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j t_j^2,\n  t_j^2>t_inf^2-4(2/3)^j.                                   (9)\n\nFor exact radical bounds,\n1207/500<lambda<483/200 and sqrt(lambda)>1553/1000.\nIndeed 707^2<2*500^2, 283^2>2*200^2, and\n1553^2/1000000=2411809/1000000<1207/500.\nUsing lambda^2=2lambda+1,\n\n  lambda^2 t_inf^2=2lambda^2/(lambda-sqrt(lambda))\n                   > (1457/125)/(431/500)\n                   =5828/431>338/25.                       (10)\n\nThe last rational comparison is 145700>145678. Also lambda^2<6.\nThe original benchmark is strictly increasing:\nB_(n+1)>=G(C_0,B_n)=G(1,B_n)>B_n.\n\nPut j=m+2. Equations(9)-(10) therefore give\n\n  B_(2^(m+2))/A\n    >338/25-24(2/3)^(m+2)\n    >338/25-24/10000\n    >27/2.\n\nHere (2/3)^6=64/729<1/10, so (2/3)^24<1/10000, and m+2>=26.\nThis proves(8), at the ACTUAL horizon. It needs neither a numerical value of\na phase constant nor a benchmark upper bound. Furthermore\n\n  1/A<1/10000000,       every m>=24,                         (11)\n\nbecause lambda>2 and 2^24=16777216>10000000.\n\n## 6. Necessary screens and a fixed selector on the ENTIRE bridge quadrant\n\nDefine the three coefficient screens\n\n  H0=1/[2(2+lambda+a+b)],\n  H1=a^2/[2(lambda+1+b)],\n  H2=b^2/[2(1+lambda+a)^2],\n  q_screen=2000/26999.                                     (12)\n\nThey are used to select a function, not asserted to equal W_N.\nUse the following priority rule, determined from the whole word before the run:\n\n  if H0>=q_screen, choose the global interval law;\n  else if H1>=q_screen, choose the first-bridge interval law;\n  else if H2>=q_screen, choose the second-bridge interval law;\n  otherwise choose the planar law of Section8.\n\nEvery equality boundary is assigned to an interval law. Zero a or b is\nincluded. Arbitrarily large coefficients are included.\n\nThe EXACT physical scalar payoffs are\n\n  V0=1/[2(2+lambda+a+b)A+1],\n  V1=a^2/[1+2(lambda+1+b)A],\n  V2=b^2/[(1+lambda+a)^2(1+2A)].                            (13)\n\nTo check the indices, the first bridge has 1+P_j1=A and\nT_j1=(lambda+1+b)A; the second has\n1+P_j2=(1+lambda+a)A and T_j2=A.\nThese formulas retain every physical entry of every block.\n\nMultiplication by A gives, for k=0,1,2,\n\n  A Vk >= Hk/(1+1/A).                                      (14)\n\nThe only corrections are the displayed +1 terms in(13); the relevant leading\ndenominators are all at least1. If an interval law is selected, (8),(11),(14)\ngive\n\n  B_N Vk > (27000/26999)/(1+1/A)\n          > (1+1/27000)(1-1/10000000)\n          > 1+1/30000.                                    (15)\n\nFor the last comparison,\n1/27000-1/30000=1/270000 exceeds\n(1+1/27000)/10000000<1/5000000.\nThis is uniform even for unbounded a,b.\n\nIf all three scalar screens fail, then\n\n  a+b>18999/4000-lambda>7/3,\n  a^2<(4000/26999)(lambda+1+b),\n  b^2<(4000/26999)(1+lambda+a)^2.                          (16)\n\nFor the sum, lambda<483/200 makes the right side exceed9339/4000>7/3.\nSince 4000/26999<3/20<(39/100)^2 and lambda<29/12, (16) implies\n\n  b<(39/100)(41/12+a),\n  a>1201/1668>18/25.\n\nIndeed substitution into a+b>7/3 gives the first lower bound on a,\nand 1201*25=30025>30024=18*1668.\nIt also implies a<9/10: otherwise\na^2<(3/20)[(139/100)(41/12)+(39/100)a].\nAt a=9/10 the left side minus the right is1799/40000>0, and its\nderivative 2a-117/2000 is positive for every a>=9/10.\nFinally b>7/3-9/10=43/30 and\nb<(39/100)(41/12+9/10)=3367/2000<17/10.\n\nIt suffices to prove the planar law on the LARGER closed domain\n\n  18/25<=a<=9/10, 43/30<=b<=17/10, a+b>=7/3.               (17)\n\nNo scalar boundary or parameter-dependent large-m threshold is lost.\n\n## 7. The interval law: ONE static objective and every physical step\n\nFor real ell<=0<u put I=[ell,u] and\n\n  F_I(x)=max_(g in I) (xg-g^2/2),       x_0=1.              (18)\n\nIts gradient is the projection onto I. Equivalently,\n\n  F_I(x)=ell*x-ell^2/2,   x<=ell;\n          x^2/2,         ell<=x<=u;\n          u*x-u^2/2,     x>=u.\n\nThus F_I is finite, convex, C1, globally1-smooth, and nonquadratic.\nThe least global Lipschitz constant of its gradient is1.\n\nFor the global interval law, set ell=0, u=1/(1+2S).\nIts COMPLETE minimum set is (-infinity,0], its minimum is0, and the\nunique closest point to x_0=1 is0. At EVERY original query i and at N,\nwrite c_i=sum_(t<i)h_t. Then\n\n  x_i=1-u c_i, g_i=u,\n  y_i=x_i-g_i=1-u(c_i+1)>=0,\n  f_i=u*x_i-u^2/2.                                        (19)\n\nThe inequality follows from c_i<=S and u(S+1)<=1.\nThe literal update x_i-h_i u=1-u(c_i+h_i) proves induction through the\nentire word, even at zero entries. At N, 2f_N=V0 from(13).\n\nFor either selected-cut law, let j=j1 or j2; put\nP_j=sum_(i<j)h_i, T_j=sum_(i>j)h_i and\n\n  u=1/(1+P_j),\n  d_minus=u(h_j-1)/(1+2T_j), ell=-d_minus.                  (20)\n\nThe selected cut has h_j>1, so both u,d_minus are positive. The COMPLETE\nminimum set is now {0}, and the actual closest initial distance is1.\n\nFor i<=j, with p_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, the exact jets are\n\n  x_i=1-u p_i>=u, g_i=u, y_i=x_i-u>=0,\n  f_i=u*x_i-u^2/2.\n\nAt j, x_j=u and the actual bridge sends it to\n\n  x_(j+1)=-d_minus(1+2T_j).\n\nFor every suffix query and for N put w_i=sum_(j<t<i)h_t. Then\n\n  x_i=-d_minus(1+2T_j)+d_minus w_i<=-d_minus,\n  g_i=-d_minus, y_i=x_i+d_minus<=0,\n  f_i=-d_minus*x_i-d_minus^2/2.                            (21)\n\nAll suffix partial sums lie in[0,T_j]. Each actual update increases w_i\nby the literal nonnegative h_i and preserves the displayed projection.\nThe terminal is x_N=-d_minus(1+T_j), with payoff\n2f_N=d_minus^2(1+2T_j)=Vj from(13).\n\nThus these are whole-run static objectives, not hypothetical independent\nblock witnesses. The supports are ell*y,u*y,0 at every sample, and the\ndeclared endpoint wins all comparisons by the displayed sign of y.\n\n## 8. The planar law: explicit slopes, function, and complete minimum set\n\nFor parameters in(17) define\n\n  eta=1/10000, d=lambda+eta, k=b/2,\n  D0=lambda+b-a*b^2/(2lambda), D=D0+1/1000,\n  sigma=a/D, theta=a^2/(dD),\n  v=(-sigma,sqrt(theta-sigma^2)),\n  Pvec=e1/A, Qvec=v/A, Rvec=-k v/A,\n  K=conv{0,Pvec,Qvec,Rvec},\n  F_K(x)=max_(g in K) (<x,g>-||g||^2/2), x_0=e1.           (22)\n\nThe ambient space is ordinary Euclidean R^2, e1=(1,0).\nAll four support offsets are0 and their coefficients are1.\nThere is no hidden proximal metric or function-value shift.\n\nSince ab<=153/100 and lambda>2, ab/(2lambda)<1/2.\nConsequently D0>lambda+b/2>lambda+43/60>d,\ntheta-sigma^2=a^2(D-d)/(dD^2)>0, and\nk sigma=ak/D<1. The radicand is positive throughout the closed domain.\nThe hull is a nondegenerate triangle;0 is retained on its Qvec--Rvec edge.\n\nFor completeness, the global regularity does not require an interpolation\nexistence oracle. Completing the square proves that the unique maximizer in\n(22) is g=P_K(x). With y=x-g, the necessary and sufficient projection condition is\n\n  <y,z-g><=0,                 EVERY z in K.                 (23)\n\nNecessity follows by differentiating squared distance along each segment from\ng to z. Sufficiency follows from\n||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2=||z-g||^2-2<y,z-g>.\nChecking the four displayed vertices suffices for their whole convex hull.\n\nAt a projected sample,\n\n  F_K(x)=<y,g>+||g||^2/2.                                 (24)\n\nAdding the projection inequalities for two points gives\n<x-x',g-g'>>=||g-g'||^2, hence ||g-g'||<=||x-x'||.\nThe two defining attained maxima give\n0<=F_K(x+h)-F_K(x)-<g,h><=<g'-g,h><=||h||^2.\nIt follows that F_K is differentiable with continuous1-Lipschitz gradient g.\nIt is finite because K is compact and convex as a supremum of affine functions.\nProjection is the identity on the nonempty interior of K, so the least global\ngradient Lipschitz constant is exactly1. Its gradient is constant on open\nexterior sectors, so the function is nonquadratic.\n\nThe COMPLETE attained minimum set is the polar cone\n\n  Cmin={x:<Pvec,x><=0,<Qvec,x><=0,<Rvec,x><=0}\n      ={x:x1<=0, -sigma*x1+sqrt(theta-sigma^2)*x2=0}.        (25)\n\nEvery such x gives F_K(x)=0. Conversely, if a vertex z has positive <x,z>,\na sufficiently small positive multiple of z lies in K and makes the maximand\nstrictly positive. Thus(25) is exact, not just a selected minimizer set.\nIt is a ray, and zero is its unique closest point to e1:\nfor x in Cmin, ||e1-x||^2=(1-x1)^2+x2^2>=1, with equality only at0.\nThe OWN closest initial radius is exactly1.\n\n## 9. All first-block and positive-height middle-block queries\n\nFIRST BLOCK: at each L0 query and at its following bridge, let c be the\nalready executed L0 mass, 0<=c<=A-1. Then\n\n  x(c)=e1-c Pvec, g(c)=Pvec,\n  y(c)=[1-(c+1)/A]e1.\n\nPut H=<Pvec,y(c)>>=0. The four support heights are\n\n  P height H, Q height -sigma H, R height k sigma H, 0 height0.\n\nBecause k sigma<1, Pvec wins all comparisons, including the endpoint\ny=0. Thus(23) proves every listed jet. Its value is\nf(c)=1/A-(c+1/2)/A^2. Each literal step subtracts h_i Pvec and increments c\nby h_i. At j1, x=Pvec, and the ACTUAL first bridge gives\n\n  XQ=Pvec-(1+aA)Pvec=-a e1.                               (26)\n\nMIDDLE BLOCK: at every L1 query and the second-bridge query, let s1 be the\nalready executed L1 mass, 0<=s1<=lambda A-1, and z=(s1+1)/A<=lambda.\nThe exact candidate jets are\n\n  x(s1)=XQ-s1 Qvec, g(s1)=Qvec,\n  y(s1)=-a e1-z v,\n  f(s1)=(a sigma-z theta)/A+theta/(2A^2).                  (27)\n\nTheir four support heights are\n\n  HQ=(a sigma-z theta)/A=theta(d-z)/A>0,\n  HP=(-a+z sigma)/A,\n  HR=-k HQ,     Hzero=0.\n\nAlso, using z<=d and d theta=a sigma,\n\n  HQ-HP=[a(1+sigma)-z(theta+sigma)]/A\n         >=[a-d sigma]/A\n         =a(1-d/D)/A>0.                                  (28)\n\nThus Qvec wins against Pvec,Rvec and0 at EVERY middle query. In particular,\nthe positive queried height is at least theta eta/A, not silently discarded.\nEvery literal L1 step subtracts its own h_i Qvec, proving the full induction\neven if an internal step is0 or the entire middle mass is concentrated in\none entry. Its resulting query still satisfies z<=lambda.\n\nThe actual second bridge, with value1+bA, gives\n\n  XR=XQ-(lambda A-1+1+bA)Qvec\n    =-a e1-(lambda+b)v.                                  (29)\n\nNo child was reset or evaluated on another function.\n\n## 10. The entire final block and its actual unqueried terminal\n\nAt each final-block query and at N, let w be the already executed L2 mass,\n0<=w<=A. Define\n\n  C(w)=lambda+b-k(w+1)/A,\n  x(w)=XR-w Rvec,\n  g(w)=Rvec,\n  y(w)=-a e1-C(w)v.                                      (30)\n\nThe exact endpoint coefficients are\n\n  C_start=lambda+b-k/A,\n  C_N=lambda+k-k/A.                                      (31)\n\nThe physical +1 in the second bridge and the +1 in x-g have both been retained.\nIn particular C_N is not replaced by its limit lambda+k.\n\nSince k>=43/60, eta/k<=3/21500<1/7000. From(11),\n\n  (C_N-d)/k=1-eta/k-1/A>1-1/5000.                        (32)\n\nHence C(w)>d on the ENTIRE actual suffix. The four heights are\n\n  HQ=theta(d-C)/A<0,\n  HR=k theta(C-d)/A>0,\n  HP=(-a+C sigma)/A,\n  Hzero=0.\n\nThe remote R--P comparison requires a separate check. Define\n\n  Psi(C)=C+ak(1-C/d).\n\nIt has positive slope because ak<1<d, and direct expansion gives\n\n  HR-HP=(a/A)[1-Psi(C)/D].\n\nSince C<=lambda+b,\n\n  Psi(C)<=Psi(lambda+b)\n          =D0+ak eta(lambda+b)/(lambda d)\n          <D0+1/8000 <D.                                (33)\n\nFor the strict estimate, ak<1, lambda+b<5, lambda d>4 and eta=1/10000.\nIt follows that D-Psi(C)>7/8000 throughout the suffix. Thus Rvec wins\nagainst EVERY vertex, not just the previous Qvec or the optimum.\n\nCondition(23) now proves all suffix jets in(30). Each physical L2 step\nsubtracts h_i Rvec and changes w to w+h_i in[0,A], so all its internal\nqueries and the final step are included. (34)\n\nThis is positive and uses the closest radius1 from(25).\n\n## 11. Exact planar payoff bound on the entire closed parameter domain\n\nPut\n\n  Phi(a,b)=a^2 b^2/(2lambda D0).\n\nIt is increasing in a and b separately on(17). Along this boundary\nb(s-b) decreases because b>=43/30>s/2.\nThe denominator D0(s-b,b)=lambda+b-s b^2/(2lambda)+b^3/(2lambda)\nincreases: its derivative has minimum 1-s^2/(6lambda)>0.\nHence Phi(s-b,b) decreases as b grows to b0, proving again\n\n  Phi(a,b)>=Phi(a0,b0)>3/40. The denominator satisfies\n\n  2lambda D0=2+(542/75)lambda-263538/140625\n             <2+15718/900-263538/140625\n             <88/5.\n\nThe last inequality reduces to\n263538*450>839*140625, namely118592100>117984375.\nThus Phi(a0,b0)>(33/25)/(88/5)=3/40.\n\nDiscarding only the positive final term in(34) gives\n\n  A V_K >= Phi(a,b)*(lambda/d)*(D0/D)*[(C_N-d)/k].\n\nEach factor is uniform on(17):\n\n  lambda/d>1-1/20000,\n  D0/D>1-1/2000,\n  (C_N-d)/k>1-1/5000.\n\nThe first uses lambda>2 and eta=1/10000; the second uses D0>2.\nTheir product exceeds1-(1/20000+1/2000+1/5000)>999/1000.\nCombining(8),(35) yields\n\n  B_N V_K >(27/2)*(3/40)*(999/1000)\n           =80919/80000\n           >1+1/40000.                                  (36)\n\nThis pays the whole finite-A terminal correction, not just a limiting\ncoefficient. No numerical parameter optimization or sampling is involved.\n\n## 12. Every ordered interpolation row and full certificate separation\n\nFor both the interval and planar laws, every original sample INCLUDING N has\ng_i in its one fixed hull, y_i=x_i-g_i satisfying the full normal(23), and\n\n  f_i=<y_i,g_i>+||g_i||^2/2.\n\nEvery minimizer in its COMPLETE minimum set has gradient0, value0, and its\nnormal to the same hull at0. (37)\n\nThe reverse orientation follows from the normal at i. This checks ALL rows,\nnot just adjacent transitions. The original actual Gram matrix G_actual of\n(x_0,g_0,...,g_N) is PSD because\nc^T G_actual c=||c_x x_0+sum_i c_i g_i||^2>=0.\nZero-step repeated samples are kept as actual separate indices.\n\nFor any claimed full certificate (5) at a level\nq_cert<=(1+1/40000)/B_N, evaluate(6) on the selected actual transcript.\nThe flow identities cancel the values, so\n\n  trace(M G_actual)\n    =q_cert-2f_N+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij\n    <=q_cert-V<0,                                        (38)\n\nby(15) or(36). But PSD of both M and G_actual would imply nonnegative trace.\nThis is a direct contradiction on the ORIGINAL coordinates. It covers dense\nmultipliers, arbitrary rank and nonzero or singular PSD remainders, all\ninitial mixed terms, common gradients, and both terminal edge orientations.\nThe selected system(7) cannot repair it either.\n\nThus the family fails physically before any certificate support restriction.\nIt is not legitimate to report an improving feasible array for this law.\nNo claim is made that zero remainder or two-endpoint sparsity is lossless.\n\nFinally for arbitrary advertised L>0 take f=L F_I or f=L F_K and use h_i/L.\nThe actual points stay the same; every gradient and function value scales byL,\nthe whole minimum set stays the same, smoothness is exactlyL, and the OWN\nclosest initial radius remains1. The payoff is precisely V, in W=2R.\nEach ordered original residual with 1/(2L) is L times(37).\n\n## 13. Joint conclusion, hostile checks and genuinely remaining interface\n\nThe selector(12) covers every a,b>=0. In an interval branch, (15) gives a\nuniform physical margin. In its complement, (16) puts the parameters in the\nlarger closed domain(17), and (36) gives a uniform planar margin. Every\nconstructed objective and trajectory obeys all original quantifiers for the\nscoped class(4). This completes the proposed joint argument for(1).\n\nThe following boundary issues are explicitly included:\na=0 or b=0; equality at any selector threshold; unbounded and m-dependent\nbridge coefficients; zero entries anywhere in L0,L1,L2; arbitrarily reordered\nor concentrated block masses; complete non-singleton minimum sets; the final\nphysical unit in(2); the +1/A corrections; W=2R; and the correctly oriented\nbenchmark LOWER bound at N, rather than at a fictitious module horizon.\nThe planar law is used only where its radicand is strictly positive.\n\nThis is an analytical self-check, NOT an independent audit and NOT verifier\nacceptance. The statement remains UNVERIFIED and cannot serve as an accepted\npremise in another proof.\n\nThe new structural information is that, for these exact block sums and counts,\nchanging the internal ordering or supplying a denser certificate cannot help:\nthe same fixed function sees only nonnegative cumulative masses in three\nchronological sectors. In particular rearrangements within the middle\nlevel-(m+1) block do not create an effective additional resource for this law.\n\nA genuinely different remaining interface is a schedule whose intended\nimprovement requires re-entry into earlier geometric sectors, or several\nindependently tunable bridges interlaced across the three cut boundaries so\nthat the exact block-sum hypotheses(4) no longer hold. A proof for such a\nschedule must retain the corresponding cross-block displacement and initial\nmixed terms in the whole matrix(5), AND exhibit a feasible base. The present\nargument does not give a lossless reduction to(4), does not exclude every\nunequal layout, and does not rule out a longer coupled-state construction\nencoded in a predetermined step list.\n\nThat is an interface, not a new fourth law, not an existence theorem, and\nnot a conditional recurrence presented as a negative solution.\nNo second candidate law is opened after the first law's exact obstruction.\nThe one authorized analytical invocation ends with this scoped handoff;\nthere is no automatic renewal, target completion, finalization, publication,\nrevocation, or swarm Repeated position0 has inconsistent gradients, so this is expressly NOT an interpolable primal run or W bound.", "evidence": "# Global finite circulation and two-way optimum flow: original-space obstruction\n\nJOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT — UNVERIFIED. Worker max, allocated invocation77\nafter observed baseline76, ceiling77/attempted78, cap1. No controller\ncompletion/counters are inferred. TWO concrete laws are screened. No\nmathematical program, numerical/arithmetic replay, executed recurrence,\ntrajectory execution, optimizer, formal call, fact_submit, installation,\npaid consult or subagent was used. No automatic renewal is allocated.\n\nThe result is a scoped failure, not the original theorem. It excludes\nevery nonnegative mixture of a complete finite-node rank-product\ncirculation and a common-profile two-way optimum circulation on the\nspecified inherited base. This includes arbitrary mixture coefficients,\nnot merely the two displayed choices. The obstruction is a negative\ndirection of the FULL original matrix. It is explicitly NOT an\ninterpolable primal run or a lower bound on W.\n\nA second, general calculation shows why Eulerian balance alone cannot\ngive a free PSD correction: a nonnegative balanced finite circulation\nhas a strictly positive physical-prefix contact moment unless all its\nedges join identical physical prefixes. Every claim and calculation\nin this report belongs to this ONE joint unverified draft.\n\n## 1. Target, accepted inputs, and original variables\n\nFor every finite nonnegative physical word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) and\nL>0, define W_n(h) as the supremum of\n\n    2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)\n\nover every finite-dimensional Euclidean space, every convex C1 function\nwith L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer x_star, and every x_0!=x_star,\nunder x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R, where R_n(h)=W_n(h)/2. The original benchmark is\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.        (1)\n\nAll splits k are integers. The original question is whether\nlim_n B_n inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n)W_n(h)=1. No schedule restriction\nin the present certificate experiment is a restriction of that target.\n\nFix ONE enclosing m>=1536. Define\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    Hword_0=(), Hword_(j+1)=(Hword_j,1+a*c^j,Hword_j),\n    n_j=2^j-1, S_j=sum Hword_j, A_j=1+S_j,\n    d_j=a*c^j, D_j=2A_j-1, t_j=d_j/A_j, tstar=1/lambda. (2)\n\nAll children retain these SAME m and epsilon. The final actual word is\nh=Hword_m=H_(m,1/4), with length n=n_m and D=D_m. The auxiliary symbol\nHword is distinct from a conjugate quadratic in fact1252b8823bb5a290.\n\nAccepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 was read in full. It supplies the exact\nsmooth-convex interpolation convention, full formal-space certificate\ninterpretation, and incoming-terminal-free selection theorem. The\npresent circulation may add incoming terminal rows, and no selected\nterminal support theorem is misapplied to the altered array.\nAccepted fact04219554e41d9750, Sections10–11, supplies the complete\nprimitive/objective conventions. Accepted fact5fd396eba75651e7,\nSection10, supplies only the unconditional benchmark consequence\n\n    B_(n_m)/D_m < 237/250, every m>=1536.                 (3)\n\nTherefore a genuine full PSD certificate at q=21/(20D) would imply\nB_(n_m)W_(n_m)(h)<4977/5000, a fixed relative gap. Neither new law\nhas such a certificate. In fact both fail at EVERY real q.\n\nThe complete previous joint report d031c723cebd5dde was read as\nunverified awareness. No result from it is adopted as a premise by\ncitation. The base definitions and every needed identity are given\nand derived within this joint draft. TASK.md, guidanceff3d21d24d20197e,\nand plan1fa89a2e348197ec govern this invocation. Latest guidance\n58e2501222fb10fe was read and explicitly leaves this allocation unchanged.\n\nNormalize L=1 and the optimum jet to x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Keep\nEVERY original independent formal vector (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), including\nevery zero-step query if present. Put h_n=0 and\n\n    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n    P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, S=sum h, D=1+2S,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.          (4)\n\nActual legal samples have Q_ij<=0 by fact1252b8823bb5a290. The general\nalgebra below allows all finite nonnegative words: zero, huge and\nunordered steps included. The eventual failure claim concerns only(2).\n\n## 2. Complete matrix and all endpoint charges\n\nLet K_ij>=0 be all ordered finite-node multipliers, K_ii=0. Let\nnu_i>=0 multiply Q_(i,star), and xiCert_i>=0 multiply Q_(star,i).\nFull value cancellation is\n\n    sum_j K_ij-sum_j K_ji+nu_i-xiCert_i=2*1_(i=n).           (5)\n\nNo original row is omitted from the array; an unspecified coefficient\nis explicitly zero. Once(5) holds, the full polynomial is\n\n    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum K_ij Q_ij\n                        +sum nu_i Q_(i,star)+sum xiCert_i Q_(star,i)\n        =q||x_0||^2-sum nu_i<x_0,g_i>+Cgrad(g).           (6)\n\nWriting out_i=nu_i+sum_j K_ij, every entry of its gradient matrix is\n\n    C_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i sum_(v>i)K_iv,\n    2C_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j sum_(v>j)K_iv\n                         +h_i[nu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. (7)\n\nHalf the total incident mass is out_i-1_(i=n), by(5). The source\nposition in a forward row supplies the diagonal -h_i K_iv. For i<j,\nthe gradient-difference terms give -(K_ij+K_ji), source i's later\ndestinations supply the second term, and source j's earlier\ndestinations and outgoing optimum row supply the last term. Thus(7)\nderives ALL entries, including the independent terminal diagonal and\ninitial mixed column -nu/2. For q>0 the entire PSD condition is\n\n    C - nu nu^T/(4q) >= 0.                              (8)\n\nLet e be the all-ones column in R^(n+1), csum=Ce, Xin=sum xiCert_i,\nEin=sum(1+P_i)xiCert_i. Summing(5) gives sum nu_i=2+Xin. Weighting(5) by\nP_i and summing yields the exact common-gradient block\n\n    M(x_0=r,g=t e)=q r^2-(2+Xin)rt+(D+Ein)t^2.             (9)\n\nFor completeness the finite position contribution is\nsum K_ij(P_i-P_j)=2S-sum(nu_i-xiCert_i)P_i; inserting the optimum\ncontributions gives e^T C e=D+Ein. Nothing is discarded at n or star.\n\nThe one-sided Huber function Hub_1 is 0 on z<=0, z^2/2 on[0,1],\nand z-1/2 on z>=1. Its full minimum set is (-infinity,0]. Starting\nat D gives all gradients1 and positions D-P_i>=1. Its OWN closest\nradius is D. On this actual static trajectory, all finite and outgoing\noptimum residuals vanish, while Q_(star,i)=-(2S-P_i). Consequently\n\n    M(D,e)=(qD-1)D-Hprice,\n    Hprice=sum_i(2S-P_i)xiCert_i=D*Xin-Ein.                    (10)\n\nThis is one WHOLE-WORD Huber charge. It is not renewed at each node.\nFor a transverse column v with e^T v=0, write nuv=nu^T v and csv=csum^T v.\nIf Delta=q(D+Ein)-(2+Xin)^2/4>0, completing both x_0 and the common\ngradient gives the exact additional expenditure\n\n    Price(v)=[(D+Ein)nuv^2/4-(2+Xin)nuv*csv/2+q*csv^2]/Delta. (11)\n\nIndeed the common block is [[q,-(2+Xin)/2],[-(2+Xin)/2,D+Ein]],\nand its coupling to v is (-nuv/2,csv). Inverting this 2-by-2 matrix\ngives(11). Full PSD requires v^T C v>=Price(v) for every such v,\nin addition to PSD of the common block. If that block is singular,\nits kernel must annihilate every coupling. These are full-space\nconditions, not claims that the charges have been paid.\n\n## 3. Explicit inherited base on the actual hierarchy\n\nHere is the base used in the experiment. These definitions fix every\ncoefficient without a feasibility oracle. Summing the finite geometric\nseries in the word definition gives\n\n    v_h=a/(c-2),\n    A_j=v_h c^j+(1-v_h)2^j,\n    t_j=(c-2)/[1+(1/v_h-1)(2/c)^j].                    (12)\n\nSince c(c-2)>1>beta, 0<v_h<1. Hence t_j increases and t_j<c-2<1/2.\nPut b=1+a, the first physical step. Direct expansion of(2) gives\n\n    b=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],\n    A_1=lambda-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)], d_1=1-epsilon,\n    A_2=lambda^2-epsilon[1+4/(lambda(1+epsilon))],\n    d_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2),\n    lambda d_2-A_2\n       =epsilon[1+4/(lambda(1+epsilon))-lambda^2 epsilon]. (13)\n\nThe last bracket is positive since lambda^2<6 and epsilon<=1/6144.\nAlso lambda d_1-A_1=-epsilon[lambda-2/(lambda(1+epsilon))]<0.\nIt follows that\n\n    t_0,t_1<tstar<t_2,\n    0<t_j-tstar<lambda epsilon for j>=2,\n    A_j>=2^j, 7/5<b<sqrt(2), A_1>12/5, A_2>5.          (14)\n\nFor A_1 use lambda>241/100 and 2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]<epsilon;\nthen A_2=2A_1+1-epsilon>5. The scalar estimates in this proof are\nwritten inequalities, not machine evaluations of any hierarchy.\n\nDefine a primitive array Lambda^j on0..n_j, starting Lambda^0=0.\nAt a join of two Hword_j copies, let p=n_j be the bridge, z=n_(j+1)\nthe terminal, and i range over nonterminal queries of the right child.\nEmbed the left array with weight1 and the right array with weight k_j;\nadd precisely\n\n    i->p: u_j h_i,  i->z: v_j h_i,  z->p: w_j,  p->z: z_j,\n    u_j=2lambda,\n    v_j=[2lambda d_j+2(A_j+d_j)]/A_j,\n    k_j=2+lambda+(lambda+1)t_j,\n    w_j=4A_j+2d_j-2lambda(A_j-1), z_j=2A_j.             (15)\n\nThe unrolled coefficient of any original ordered pair is the finite\nsum of these four indicators over every dyadic node, weighted by the\nproduct of k_l over precisely the ancestors whose right child contains\nthat node. Left ancestor weights are1. Repeated pairs are summed.\nThis finite sum/product is the intended explicit formula; no recurrence\nhas been executed. All entries are nonnegative. For w_j, write\nw_j=2lambda+2(d_j-A_j/lambda); use(14) at j>=2, w_0=4+2a,\nand w_1>2lambda-2. The identities\nu_j+v_j=2(k_j-1) and u_j(A_j-1)+w_j-z_j=2(A_j+d_j)\nprove primitive divergence -2h_i at each nonterminal query and\n2(A_(j+1)-1) at the terminal.\n\nDefine\n\n    V_j=2sum_(i<n_j)h_i(f_i-f_(n_j))\n         +sum_(i<n_j)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n         -A_j(A_j-1)||g_(n_j)||^2,\n    Psrc_j=V_j+sum Lambda^j_iv Q_iv.                    (16)\n\nAt a join set r_R=sum_(i right,i<z)h_i g_i,\nY=r_R-(A_j-1)g_z and Z=g_p-g_z. The four weighted-row types before\ntheir scalar multipliers in(15), with value parts suppressed only\nafter their stated divergence cancels them, have gradient parts\n\n    d_j<r_R,g_p>+||r_R||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2\n                                            +(A_j-1)||g_p||^2/2;\n    -||r_R||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2\n                              +(A_j-1)||g_z||^2/2-<r_R,g_z>;\n    d_j<g_z,g_p>+<g_z,r_R>+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2;\n    -(d_j+1/2)||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2/2-<g_p,r_R>-<g_p,g_z>.\n\nCollecting r_R^2,r_R*g_p,r_R*g_z and the diagonals gives the full identity\n\n    Psrc_(j+1)=Psrc_j^left+k_j Psrc_j^right\n      +(tstar-t_j)[(lambda+1)||Y||^2-2lambda A_j<Y,Z>\n                           +A_j^2(2+tstar+t_j)||Z||^2]. (17)\n\nFor example the three listed coefficients before adding V and children\nare(u_j-v_j)/2, u_j d_j-z_j, w_j-v_j. Substitution of(15) gives(17).\nThe bracket is positive definite: its determinant divided by A_j^2\nis (lambda+1)(2+tstar+t_j)-lambda^2>=lambda>0.\nThus positive epsilon does NOT make these primitive children PSD.\n\nDefine objective arrays Kbase^r and mubase^r with Kbase^0=0,\nmubase^0_0=2, and zero incoming optimum rows. At a join of two depth-j\nchildren put A=S_j, bjoin=1+d_j, Dp=D_(j+1), Dc=D_j, and\n\n    s_j=(A+bjoin)/(3A+2bjoin), theta_j=1-s_j.\n\nWith maps L_l=l and R_i=n_j+1+i, let\n\n    Kbase_(L_l,L_v)=Lambda^j_lv/Dp,\n    Kbase_(R_i,R_v)=Kbase^j_iv,\n    Kbase_(R_i,p)=s_j mubase^j_i,\n    mubase_(L_l)=2h_l/Dp for l<n_j,\n    mubase_p=2s_j-2A/Dp,\n    mubase_(R_i)=theta_j mubase^j_i.                    (18)\n\nAll other entries are zero. The bridge optimum coefficient is positive\nbecause (A+bjoin)Dp-A(3A+2bjoin)=A^2+4A*bjoin+2bjoin^2+A+bjoin>0.\nHere s_0=1/2 and 1/3<s_j<1/2 for j>=1. Each right query has unchanged\nflow since s_j+theta_j=1. Left primitive flow is canceled by its\ndisplayed optimum rows and the bridge row. Thus(5) holds and\nsum mubase=2. The base has no incoming terminal row.\n\nTo make(18) an explicit finite formula, in a depth-r word take the\nright-spine node of depth l at offset o_l=2^r-2^l and bridge\np_l=n_r-2^(l-1), for1<=l<=r. Include Lambda^(l-1)/D_l on\n[o_l,p_l]. For each of that node's right queries add\ns_(l-1)mubase^(l-1)_i into p_l. These are all finite rows.\nThe optimum weight of a left nonterminal i at that node is\n\n    (2h_i/D_l) product_(t=l)^(r-1)theta_t;\n\nthe optimum weight at p_l is\n\n    [2s_(l-1)-2S_(l-1)/D_l] product_(t=l)^(r-1)theta_t;\n\nand its final optimum weight is 2product_(t=0)^(r-1)theta_t.\nEach query is in exactly one such left block, bridge, or final category.\nThe same explicit product formula within the relevant right child\nspecifies every weight used in a spine edge. Together with(15), this\ngives every base coefficient as a finite sum/product on original indices.\n\nWrite Cbase^j for its gradient matrix and Mbase_j(q) for(6). At a join,\nlet T_R=sum_i mubase^j_i g_(R_i), x_p=x_0-sum_(l<n_j)h_l g_(L_l),\nRchild_R=Cbase^j(g_R)-Dc||T_R||^2/4, and\nkappa_j=Dc-Dp theta_j^2. Expanding all original rows(18) gives\n\n    Mbase_(j+1)(1/Dp)=Psrc_j(g_L)/Dp+Rchild_R\n      +||x_p+(A-Dp s_j)g_p-(Dp theta_j/2)T_R||^2/Dp\n      +(kappa_j/4)||T_R-2g_p||^2.                    (19)\n\nTo check the completion, after removing the source and Rchild, the\nremaining polynomial before completing squares is\n\n    ||x_p||^2/Dp+2(A/Dp-s_j)<x_p,g_p>\n    +(A^2/Dp+2s_j)||g_p||^2-theta_j<x_p,T_R>\n    +(bjoin-s_j)<g_p,T_R>+Dc||T_R||^2/4.\n\nExpanding the two squares in(19) reproduces this polynomial by\ns_j(3A+2bjoin)=A+bjoin. No child PSD is asserted at epsilon>0.\nThe common-gradient contact identity is\n\n    Cbase^j e=D_j mubase^j/2.                         (20)\n\nIndeed Psrc is zero with zero derivative at all-common gradients by(17).\nAt x_0=Dp and all gradients1, x_p=Dp-A and T_R=2; both squares\nin(19) have zero vector argument. Induction from ||x_0-g_0||^2\ntherefore gives zero value and derivative of the full quadratic, which\nis precisely(20). Contact is algebraic, not a PSD claim.\n\n## 4. Two explicit GLOBAL laws and their entire polynomial\n\nAt the final depth m abbreviate mu=mubase^m. In the following formulas\nthe coefficients eta,chi are arbitrary nonnegative scalars. Define\n\n    K_ij=Kbase_ij+eta*mu_i*mu_j, i!=j; K_ii=0,\n    nu_i=(1+chi)mu_i,\n    xiCert_i=chi*mu_i,                         0<=i,j<=n. (21)\n\nThe two concrete laws are\n\n    Law A: eta=64epsilon, chi=0;\n    Law B: eta=0, chi=128epsilon.                     (22)\n\nFor a calibration child r<5 use eta=chi=0; for r>=5 use(22) with\nthe SAME enclosing epsilon. At epsilon=0 both laws use eta=chi=0 at\nevery depth. The scope extension to arbitrary eta,chi in(21) is an\nexclusion of the whole fixed two-parameter family, not another law\nor a parameter search. No retuning is performed after the obstruction.\n\nEvery final coefficient is a finite formula supplied by(15),(18),(21).\nThere are no decrements, so no zero coefficient is subtracted from.\nAll finite pairs, including pairs INTO and OUT OF the actual terminal,\nare retained. Law A adds equal mass to each pair i->j and j->i;\nLaw B adds equal mass to i->star and star->i. Thus every original\nvalue equation(5) stays exact. These are global balanced circulations,\nnot source-dependent terminal paths or changes of physical GD steps.\n\nTheir total additions over the ONE word are\n\n    finite addition = eta*(4-sum_i mu_i^2) <=4eta,\n    outgoing optimum addition = 2chi,\n    incoming optimum addition = 2chi.                (23)\n\nIn A the finite addition is at most256epsilon=64/m<=1/24.\nIn B each optimum addition is256epsilon=64/m<=1/24 and their\ncombined addition is at most1/12. There is no per-node allowance.\n\nFor the entire original vector family define\n\n    Tmu=sum_i mu_i g_i,\n    Xmu=sum_i mu_i x_i,\n    Vmu=sum_i mu_i[||g_i||^2-<g_i,x_i>].              (24)\n\nValue cancellation and Q_(i,star)+Q_(star,i)=||g_i||^2-<g_i,x_i>\ngive the exact polynomial\n\n    M_(eta,chi)(q)=Mbase_m(q)\n           +eta*[2Vmu-||Tmu||^2+<Tmu,Xmu>]+chi*Vmu. (25)\n\nFor the finite term, sum over unordered pairs mu_i mu_j(Q_ij+Q_ji).\nThe difference-square part is 2sum mu_i||g_i||^2-||Tmu||^2 because\nsum mu=2. The position part is <Tmu,Xmu>-2sum mu_i<g_i,x_i>.\nThis proves(25) with every coordinate, including x_0 and g_n, present.\nIn the eta term the x_0 contributions cancel; the chi term contributes\n-chi<Tmu,x_0>. The full initial mixed column is therefore\n-(1+chi)mu/2 as required by(21), and its Schur expenditure is\n(1+chi)^2 mu mu^T/(4q). The terminal diagonal and every incoming\nterminal row are retained by(7),(21); a unit terminal-square coefficient\nfrom an incoming-free theorem is NOT assumed for this new array.\n\nHere is the exact terminal completion for the ACTUAL altered array.\nThe base terminal has total outgoing mass2 and no incoming rows, so\nCbase_nn=1. The final original terminal entries are\n\n    K_(i,n)=eta*mu_i*mu_n for i<n,\n    K_(n,i)=Kbase_(n,i)+eta*mu_n*mu_i for i<n,\n    nu_n=(1+chi)mu_n, xiCert_n=chi*mu_n,\n    gammaTerm=C_nn=1+eta*mu_n*(2-mu_n)+chi*mu_n>0.\n\nWith C from(7), put\n\n    vterm=(nu_n/2)x_0-sum_(i<n)C_(i,n)g_i,\n    Rterm=q||x_0||^2-sum_(i<n)nu_i<x_0,g_i>\n              +sum_(i,j<n)C_ij<g_i,g_j>-||vterm||^2/gammaTerm.\n\nThen the COMPLETE original polynomial, without a re-selection, is\n\n    M_(eta,chi)(q)=gammaTerm*||g_n-vterm/gammaTerm||^2+Rterm.\n\nEvery coefficient in this identity is fixed by the finite formulas\nabove. Thus the actual terminal expenditure ||vterm||^2/gammaTerm\nis retained, and full PSD is equivalent to PSD of the entire Rterm.\nIt is not enough to check individual prefix planes or the common block.\n\n## 5. Whole-word common/contact payment for the two laws\n\nSet Pbar=sum_i mu_i P_i and Utail_i=sum_(j>i)mu_j. From(21),\n\n    Xin=2chi, Ein=chi(2+Pbar),\n    Hprice=chi(2D-2-Pbar), 0<=Pbar<=D-1.              (26)\n\nAt q=21/(20D), both laws have a positive initial/common block. For A\nits determinant is1/20. For B, chi<=1/48, and\n\n    q(D+Ein)-(1+chi)^2\n       >=21/20-(49/48)^2>0.                         (27)\n\nThe final strict inequality follows by cross multiplication:\n21*2304>20*2401. B's WHOLE Huber charge is paid within that advertised\nslack, since\n\n    0<=Hprice/D<2chi<=1/24<1/20.                    (28)\n\nA has Hprice=0. These checks do not imply full PSD. They retain the\nsame whole-word D and do not rescale a child or count slack twice.\n\nFor clarity, the full contact vector can also be written explicitly.\nThe finite addition has row-sum vector\n\n    dfin_i=eta*mu_i*(P_i-Pbar/2),                    (29)\n\nand Vmu's gradient-matrix row sum is\n\n    cstar_i=mu_i*(1+P_i/2)+(h_i/2)*Utail_i.          (30)\n\nTo derive(29), vary all gradients around the common value1. Balanced\nfinite position variations cancel; the remaining row-i position\ndifference is eta mu_i(2P_i-Pbar), half of which is the matrix row sum.\nFor(30), write Vmu=sum mu_i||g_i||^2-<Tmu,x_0>\n+sum_i mu_i<g_i,sum_(t<i)h_t g_t> and take its gradient row sums.\nThus the final row-sum vector is\n\n    csum=D*mu/2+dfin+chi*cstar.                       (31)\n\nEquations(11),(21),(26),(31) give the EXACT remaining transverse\nexpenditure, including every common, initial and terminal coupling.\nNo bound paying this transverse expenditure is claimed.\n\nFor A alone, dcontact=csum-D*mu/2=dfin. Completing the common block\nat q=zeta/D, zeta>1, gives the simpler exact expression\n\n    Price_A(v)=(csum^T v)^2/D\n                  +(dfin^T v)^2/[D(zeta-1)].        (32)\n\nAt zeta=1 the singular common block instead requires dfin=0.\nAs mu has positive weights at different physical prefixes, A at\nepsilon>0 does not satisfy that singular-contact requirement.\nAt zeta=21/20 the cost in(32) is finite but remains a real full-space\nexpense. The counterdirection below defeats both laws even before\nany initial Schur expenditure is deducted.\n\n## 6. General balanced-circulation contact obstruction\n\nThis calculation is not limited to the rank-product law. Let J_ij>=0\nbe ANY zero-diagonal finite-node array with equal row and column sums\nat every node. On ANY finite nonnegative word define\n\n    R_J(g)=sum_(i!=j)J_ij[<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2].\n\nIts value terms cancel by balance and its x_0 terms cancel in position\ndifferences. Let C_J be its full symmetric gradient matrix. At common\ngradients, R_J(e)=0. Its row-sum vector satisfies\n\n    (C_J e)_i=(1/2)sum_j J_ij(P_i-P_j),\n    P^T C_J e=(1/4)sum_(i,j)J_ij(P_i-P_j)^2.         (33)\n\nFor the first equality, the derivative of the source gradient gives\nsum_j J_ij(P_i-P_j). The derivatives of the positions cancel because\nrow and column masses agree. The difference-square derivative vanishes\nat e. Divide the derivative by2. For the second equality, expand the\nsum of squared differences and use balance to equate the P_i^2 and\nP_j^2 sums. This proves both equalities directly without invoking a\ngraph theorem.\n\nIt follows that C_J e=0 if and only if every positive J_ij joins\nequal P_i and P_j. Necessity follows from the nonnegative sum in(33);\nsufficiency follows from its first equality. With nonnegative h,\nP_i=P_j means all intervening steps are zero, hence x_i=x_j as a\nFORMAL identity even with independent gradients. In this case\n\n    R_J(g)=(1/2)sum J_ij||g_i-g_j||^2 >=0.           (34)\n\nConversely, if some positive edge has unequal physical prefixes,\nC_J e!=0 while e^T C_J e=0. Taking v=C_J e and expanding\nR_J(e+t v)=2t||C_J e||^2+t^2 v^T C_J v shows a negative value\nfor sufficiently small negative t. Thus C_J is not PSD. In particular,\non a strictly positive word EVERY nonzero nonnegative balanced finite\ncirculation is an indefinite correction, despite exact value balance.\n\nMoreover no collection of such additions can cancel its physical-\nprefix contact work: the right side of(33) is additive and nonnegative.\nAt an exactly contacting base with zero incoming optimum rows and\nq=1/D, the common kernel condition forces C_J e=0. Hence no nonzero\nnonnegative finite circulation preserves full PSD at that exact level\non a positive word. This is NOT a statement at q>1/D, about signed\nchanges with paid decrements, about changed optimum profiles, or about\narbitrary full certificates. The explicit paid test in the next\nsection is needed to exclude our concrete laws at larger q.\n\n## 7. Entire zero-displacement restriction of BOTH global corrections\n\nConsider any original gradient assignment supported strictly within\nthe root's first left child, with x_0=0, zero gradient at that child's\nterminal, and\n\n    sum_(i in support)h_i g_i=0.                     (35)\n\nAll later formal positions are zero. On the supported indices(18)\ngives mu_i=2h_i/D. Therefore Tmu=0 exactly. By(24),(25),\n\n    D[M_(eta,chi)(q)-Mbase_m(q)]\n      =(2eta+chi) sum_(i in support)h_i(2-h_i)||g_i||^2. (36)\n\nHere is a direct verification retaining all prefix positions. Since\nx_i=-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n\n    sum_i h_i<g_i,x_i>\n      =-(1/2)||sum_i h_i g_i||^2+(1/2)sum_i h_i^2||g_i||^2\n      =(1/2)sum_i h_i^2||g_i||^2.\n\nMultiplying Vmu by D consequently gives exactly the diagonal sum\nin(36). Every unsupported term in Vmu has gradient zero, and Tmu=0\nkills BOTH global rank terms in(25), including their terminal and\nremote-query contributions. Thus(36) is an entire subspace identity,\nnot a selected-edge or conditional-covariance approximation.\n\n## 8. The inherited base itself is negative on a deep original wave\n\nWe now strengthen the earlier two-coordinate screen by retaining the\nnegative terminal-source accumulation at ALL depths. Let pterm_j be\nPsrc_j evaluated with only its terminal gradient equal to1. Define\n\n    Fterm_j=A_j^2(1+t_j)-2A_j+(lambda+1).\n\nSubstitute Y=-(A_j-1), Z=-1 into(17). The bracket is precisely Fterm_j,\nand the left child is zero. Thus the finite closed expression is\n\n    pterm_j=sum_(l<j)(tstar-t_l)Fterm_l\n                              product_(v=l+1)^(j-1)k_v. (37)\n\nThis is a symbolic finite formula and has not been executed. At depth1,\npterm_1=2-b^2<3epsilon. At depth2, k_1<7,\n0<tstar-t_1<epsilon, and Fterm_1<13, so\n\n    pterm_2<34epsilon<2epsilon*A_2^2.                 (38)\n\nFor the last inequalities use(13),(14): k_1<2+5/2+(7/2)/2<7;\ntstar-t_1=(lambda epsilon-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)])/(lambda A_1)\n<epsilon; and Fterm_1=A_1^2-(1+epsilon)A_1+lambda+1<13.\nAlso A_2>5 makes34<2A_2^2. The step from depth2 to depth3 adds a\nnegative term and multiplies the normalized old term by a number in(0,1).\nIt follows that pterm_3/A_3^2<2epsilon, regardless of its sign.\n\nFor every l>=3, monotonicity of t_l and the exact relation\nt_(l+1)=c*t_l/(2+t_l) give\n\n    t_3>c*tstar/(2+tstar)=(1+epsilon)tstar,\n    t_l-tstar>(2/5)epsilon.                          (39)\n\nSince A_l>=8 and t_l>2/5, Fterm_l>A_l^2. Also A_(l+1)=(2+t_l)A_l\nand t_l<1/2. Hence\n\n    (t_l-tstar)Fterm_l/A_(l+1)^2>(8/125)epsilon.          (40)\n\nThe normalized propagation factor is exactly\n\n    rnorm_l=k_l/(2+t_l)^2\n       =1-(t_l-tstar)(1+t_l)/(2+t_l)^2,\n    1-epsilon<rnorm_l<1, l>=2.                          (41)\n\nIndeed (1+t)/(2+t)^2<=1/4 and t_l-tstar<lambda epsilon< (5/2)epsilon.\nThe product of any at most m such factors is greater than\n(1-epsilon)^m>=1-m epsilon=3/4; the elementary last inequality\nfollows by induction from (1-u)^k>=1-ku for0<=u<=1.\nNo recurrence or product has been numerically evaluated.\n\nUnroll(37) from depth3, divide by A_j^2, bound the initial propagated\nterm above by2epsilon, and apply(40),(41) to EVERY negative term.\nFor3<=j<=m,\n\n    pterm_j/A_j^2\n       <[2-(6/125)(j-3)]epsilon.                    (42)\n\nThere are j-3 terms, l=3,...,j-1. Each negative contribution has\nmagnitude greater than (8/125)epsilon times3/4. This explains the\ncoefficient and retains the entire same-m depth range. No PSD of a\npositive-epsilon child has been used.\n\nChoose ANY integer62<=j<=m-3. Let rbridge=2^j-1 and Hstep=1+d_j\nbe its actual physical step. Assign the original scalar vectors\n\n    x_0=0, g_0=-Hstep/b, g_rbridge=1,\n    g_i=0 for every other original i, including i=n.  (43)\n\nThe displacement is exactly b*(-Hstep/b)+Hstep=0. All later points and\nthe root left terminal are zero, so(35) holds. In the first Hword_j,\nonly its first and terminal gradients are nonzero. For j>=2 there\nis no source cross coefficient between them: g_0 enters only the\nfirst depth1 bracket in(17), while the final terminal enters disjoint\nbrackets. Exact finite original-space lift of arbitrary three-sector data\n\nThis section applies to any original nonnegative finite word written\n\n    h=(block_0,b,block_1,c_step,block_2),\n    P=sum block_0, M=sum block_1, T=sum block_2.\n\nThe blocks are their actual complete chronological lists; no entry is\nreplaced or executed by a different algorithm. The proposed data at a query\nwith already executed sector mass s are\n\n    (Z_p-s p,p,F_p-s||p||^2), 0<=s<=P,\n    (Z_q-s q,q,F_q-s||q||^2), 0<=s<=M,\n    (Z_r-s r,r,F_r-s||r||^2), 0<=s<=T. All three endpoint pairs and the zero\noptimum jet (0,0,0) form a set E of at most seven jets. Require ALL\nordered residuals Q_ij<=0 between these endpoints and the optimum.\nThis is an exact characterization of legal data of this form, not a\nPSD-Gram-only relaxation.\n\nNecessity of the displayed values for any real function follows by\nusing both convexity inequalities between two equal-gradient samples:\ntheir value difference is exactly the inner product with that gradient.\nIn particular each physical step within a sector pays its own\nh_i||g_i||^2 decrease, even when h_i is huge, unordered, or zero.\n\nFor sufficiency, a residual between two sector points is affine in\neach of the two masses separately (in fact independent of the source\nmass after cancellation). Its values throughout their rectangle are\nconvex combinations of its endpoint values. Residuals within one\nsector are zero. Residuals against the optimum are affine in the one\nmass. For every endpoint i put\n\n    y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_{i in E}{<y_i,v>-delta_i},\n    F(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H(v)}. Running actual GD is now a finite induction using (3.1)--(3.2),\nand its terminal is Z_r-T r with gradient r and value F_r-T||r||^2.\n\nThe complete minimum value and set of this explicit function are\n\n    min F=0,\n    argmin F=conv{y_i : i in E and delta_i=0}.             (3.4)\n\nIndeed H(0)=0, the conjugate objective has a unique maximizer, and\ngrad F(x)=0 precisely when x is in partial H(0). The latter is the\nconvex hull of the slopes of the active affine terms, namely (3.4).\nThis follows directly by separation: a vector outside that hull has\na direction violating the subgradient inequality for the finite\nmaximum. Thus the minimum is attained, and 0 is an actual minimizer.\nThe construction's payoff about this chosen minimizer is exactly\n2(F_r-T||r||^2)/||x_0||^2 when x_0!=0. We do NOT assert that 0 is\nits closest minimizer. The upper bounds below apply to every minimizer\nafter translation, and therefore also to its actual closest one.\n\nThis is a real original-space lift, with x_0 and the gradients supplied\nas real vectors; no unspecified Gram realization is needed. One can\nproject all points onto span{p,q,r} if desired: all residuals and\nfunction values are unchanged. If the original radius had an\northogonal component, this only decreases that chosen radius. The\nupper argument below already holds in any dimension without using\nthis projection or claiming it preserves the complete minimum set.\n\n## 4. A mass-selected certificate without height restrictions\n\nWrite d=b-1 and e=c_step-1. For this section the following algebra\ndoes not require d,e positive. Let z be a target value such that\n\n    z <= F_r-t||r||^2                                   (4.1)\n\nfor a specified real charge t. For Family E, z=f(x_n), t=T, and\n(4.1) is equality. Section7 supplies a different paid t for Family Q.\nDefine\n\n    u=P+1, v=M+1, w=t+1/2,\n    A=u+d=P+b, B=v+e=M+c_step,\n    Lc=A+v=u+v+d, D=u+v+w+d+e.                          (4.2)\n\nAll these names are local certificate scalars, not benchmark masses.\nAt the last p query and first q query, the original ordered row gives\n\n    F_p >= F_q + P||p||^2 + b<p,q> + ||p-q||^2/2.\n\nAt the last q query and first r query, it gives\n\n    F_q >= F_r + M||q||^2 + c_step<q,r> + ||q-r||^2/2.\n\nThe original rows from each first-sector query to the chosen zero\noptimum give\n\n    F_p <= <p,x_0>-||p||^2/2,\n    F_q <= <q,x_0-Ap>-||q||^2/2,\n    F_r <= <r,x_0-Ap-Bq>-||r||^2/2.\n\nCombining these with (4.1), without assigning or optimizing ANY\nqueried height, yields all three inequalities\n\n    z <= <p,x_0>-u||p||^2-v||q||^2-w||r||^2\n                    -d<p,q>-e<q,r>,\n    z <= <q,x_0>-v||q||^2-w||r||^2-A<p,q>-e<q,r>,\n    z <= <r,x_0>-w||r||^2-A<p,r>-B<q,r>.                (4.3)\n\nTheir validity includes every previously retained affine face or\nremote row: those are additional necessary constraints on an actual\nfunction. We are taking nonnegative combinations of necessary rows,\nnot asserting that these five rows alone imply interpolation.\nThe exact lift in Section3 uses ALL rows when a function is built.\n\nAssume D>0, 2D-u>0, 2D-Lc>0, and put\n\n    alpha=A/(2D-u), eta=(Lc+e)/(2D-Lc).                  (4.4)\n\nIf 0<=alpha<=eta<=1, combine (4.3) with respective weights\nalpha, eta-alpha, 1-eta. Define\n\n    gbar=alpha*p+(eta-alpha)*q+(1-eta)*r,\n    s=p-q, tvec=q-r,\n    J11=alpha*(u-D*alpha),\n    J22=eta*(Lc-D*eta),\n    J12=alpha*u*(1-eta)/2.                              (4.5)\n\nThe exact weighted quadratic identity is\n\n    z <= <gbar,x_0>-D||gbar||^2\n          -J11||s||^2-2J12<s,tvec>-J22||tvec||^2.       (4.6)\n\nFor completeness, before subtracting D||gbar||^2 the coefficients\nof ||p||^2,||q||^2,||r||^2 are alpha*u, eta*v,w, while the\ncoefficients of <p,q>,<p,r>,<q,r> are respectively\n\n    alpha*d+(eta-alpha)*A, (1-eta)*A,\n    eta*e+(1-eta)*B.\n\nAt p=q=r this quadratic is D||r||^2. The p row sum is\n[(1+alpha)u+d]/2=D*alpha. The sum of its p and q row sums is\n[(1+eta)Lc+e]/2=D*eta. Both equalities follow from (4.4).\nSubstitute p=r+s+tvec, q=r+tvec. All terms involving r cancel\nafter subtracting D||gbar||^2; the remaining entries are (4.5).\nIn particular the mixed entry is\nalpha*u/2+eta*A/2-D*alpha*eta=alpha*u*(1-eta)/2.\nThis proves (4.6) in the full original vector space.\n\nIf the real symmetric matrix J with entries (4.5) is PSD, completing\nthe square gives\n\n    z <= ||x_0||^2/(4D),  2z/||x_0||^2 <= 1/(2D).       (4.7)\n\nIf J is positive definite and either p!=q or q!=r, the inequality\nis strict. Thus this certificate handles nonparallel q,r, any third\ncoordinate, nonradial initialization and arbitrary positive heights\nat once. It is a closed formula selected by the schedule, not an SDP,\na search over an unspecified feasible set, or a numerical Gram test.\n\n## 5. Exact hierarchy masses and physical checkpoint partitions\n\nFor the hierarchy of Section1 set\n\n    X=X_(m-2), A_h=a*c^(m-2), zeta=A_h/X,\n    Y=X_(m-1)=2X+A_h, d0=1+a.\n\nThen the original total mass satisfies\n\n    S_m+1=2Y+c*A_h=[4+(2+c)zeta]X.                      (5.1)\n\nAll three pair partitions are printed here, with their FULL masses.\nColumns d,e are checkpoint excesses, not physical steps.\n\n|pair|P+1|M+1|T+1|d|e|\n|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|\n|(j_L,j_O)|X|X|Y|A_h|c*A_h|\n|(j_O,j_R)|Y|X|X|c*A_h|A_h|\n|(j_L,j_R)|X|2X+c*A_h|X|A_h|A_h|\n\nFor example, in the third row block_1 is exactly\n(H_(m-2),1+c*A_h,H_(m-2)); its mass is 2X+c*A_h-1.\nNo middle root or last leaf is removed. The other rows consist of\nthe actual adjacent children from the defining concatenations.\n\nThe following rational enclosure is proved symbolically:\n\n    12/5<c<29/12,  2/5<a<zeta<c-2<5/12,\n    X>=2^(m-2)>=2048,  1<d0<3/2.                        (5.2)\n\nAt l=0 the strict a<zeta need not hold, but here l=m-2>0, so it does.\nTo check (5.2), sqrt(2)>7/5 and sqrt(2)<99/70, by squaring positive\nrationals. Thus c>12/5 and\nc<(169/70)*(6145/6144)<29/12; the last comparison reduces to\n2028<2*6144. Also beta>=6143/6144>99/100, so\na>(99/100)*(12/29)=297/725>2/5. The identity\nlambda*(lambda-2)=1 and beta<1 show a<c-2. The mass recurrence is\n\n    X_(l+1)=2X_l+a*c^l, X_0=1.\n\nIts exact closed form, checked by substitution, is\n\n    X_l=v_h*c^l+(1-v_h)*2^l, v_h=a/(c-2) in (0,1).\n\nTherefore a<=a*c^l/X_l<c-2 for l>0, and X_l>=2^l. The stated\nbounds on m give X>=2048. Finally a<5/12<1/2 gives d0<3/2.\nThis is a symbolic identity and inequalities, not an executed recurrence.\n\nFor either of the two terminal charges used below, write\n\n    w/X=(T+1)/X-delta,\n    D/X=4+(2+c)zeta-delta,\n    0<=delta<1/1000.                                   (5.3)\n\nFor Family E, delta=1/(2X). For Family Q, Section7 gives\ndelta=(1+d0^2)/(2X)<2/X<=1/1024<1/1000. Thus both are covered\nby exactly the same rational analysis, with the ACTUAL last entry paid.\nIn particular\n\n    23/4 < D/X < 117/20.                                (5.4)\n\nFor the lower bound use 4+(2+12/5)*(2/5)-1/1000>23/4.\nFor the upper bound use 4+(2+29/12)*(5/12)=841/144<117/20.\n\n## 6. Complete positive-definiteness proof over this whole domain\n\nHomogeneity permits dividing u,v,w,d,e,D and J by X in this section.\nAll subsequent bounds hold for EVERY c,zeta,delta in (5.2)--(5.3),\nso they do not rely on sampling m or individual parameters.\n\nDefine L0=2+zeta and E0=c*zeta. There are two useful weight types:\n\n    alpha0=(1+zeta)/(2D-1),\n    eta0=(L0+E0)/(3L0+2E0-2delta),\n    eta1=[3+(2+c)zeta]/[5+(3+c)zeta-2delta].              (6.1)\n\nHere D denotes D/X ONLY in this normalized section. All denominators\nare positive by (5.2)--(5.4). The bounds are\n\n    13/100 < alpha0 < 27/200,\n    9/25 < eta0 < 37/100 < 3/8,\n    33/50 < eta1 < 67/100.                              (6.2)\n\nFor alpha0, use\nalpha0>(7/5)/(117/10-1)=14/107>13/100 and\nalpha0<(17/12)/(23/2-1)=17/126<27/200.\n\nFor eta0>9/25, cross multiplication reduces to\n7E0+18delta>2L0. Its left side is at least 7*(24/25), while\n2L0<=29/6, proving the claim. For eta0<37/100 it suffices that\n26E0+74delta<11L0. After substituting L0 and E0, its only nonconstant\nleft side is (26c-11)zeta+74delta, which is less than\n1555/72+74/1000<22. Hence this upper bound holds throughout the domain.\n\nFor eta1>33/50 the required inequality is\n(1+17c)zeta+66delta>15. Its left side exceeds\n(1+17*(12/5))*(2/5)=418/25>15. For eta1<67/100 it is enough that\n(33c-1)zeta+134delta<35. The left side is less than\n525/16+134/1000<35. This proves (6.2), with no evaluation of a schedule.\n\nFor each pair, the weight choices (alpha,eta) from (4.4) are as follows:\n\n|pair|u|Lc|alpha|eta|\n|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|\n|(j_L,j_O)|1|2+zeta|alpha0|eta0|\n|(j_O,j_R)|2+zeta|3+(1+c)zeta|eta0|eta1|\n|(j_L,j_R)|1|3+(1+c)zeta|alpha0|eta1|\n\nThe second-row equality alpha=eta0 follows by expanding 2D-u;\nthe third-row middle mass was retained in Section5. In all rows\n0<alpha<eta<1, so every certificate multiplier in (4.3) is nonnegative.\n\nFirst pair. Using eta<3/8, (5.4) and (6.2), the entries in (4.5) obey\n\n    J11 >= (13/100)*(1-(117/20)*(27/200)) > 27/1000,\n    J22 >= (9/25)*(12/5-(117/20)*(3/8)) > 37/500,\n    0<=J12 <= (27/200)*(1-9/25)/2=27/625<11/250.\n\nAll factors being bounded below are positive. Thus\n\n    det J > (27/1000)*(37/500)-(11/250)^2\n          =31/500000>0.                                 (6.3)\n\nSecond pair. Here Lc>=3+(1+12/5)*(2/5)=109/25, and\n\n    J11 >= (9/25)*(12/5-(117/20)*(37/100)) >21/250,\n    J22 >= (33/50)*(109/25-(117/20)*(67/100)) >29/100,\n    0<=J12 <= (37/100)*(29/12)*(17/50)/2 <153/1000.\n\nIt follows that\n\n    det J > (21/250)*(29/100)-(153/1000)^2\n          =951/1000000>0.                               (6.4)\n\nThird pair. The first-pair J11 bound and second-pair J22 bound apply.\nMoreover J12< (27/200)*(17/50)/2=459/20000<23/1000. Therefore\n\n    det J > (27/1000)*(29/100)-(23/1000)^2\n          =7301/1000000>0.                              (6.5)\n\nPositive first diagonal entry and positive determinant prove positive\ndefiniteness of each real2x2 matrix. Restoring X multiplies J by X and\nits determinant by X^2. The proof holds for all real gradient vectors\nand their actual PSD Gram, without imposing any geometric pattern.\n\nFor Family E, t=T and 2D=1+2S_m. Equations(4.6)--(4.7) and (6.3)--(6.5)\ngive the upper half of (1.2). If either gradient change is nonzero,\nthis upper bound is strict. Positive queried heights have never been\nassumed away or treated as free resource: they canceled only through\nthe five explicitly used original inequalities.\n\n## 7. All legal prefix completions and the actual last leaf\n\nNow require only the queried gradients (1.1), allowing an arbitrary\nterminal gradient and any static function consistent with the complete\nprefix. No completion is being proposed as a new lower construction;\nwe prove an upper bound for EVERY such completion using its actual\nlast jet. It is unnecessary to select a conjugate active face or to\nsolve for an unspecified terminal Gram.\n\nThe last physical step is d0=1+a. The suffix has at least one query,\nand its last query is at Z_r-(T-d0)r with value\n\n    f(x_(n-1))=F_r-(T-d0)||r||^2.\n\nThis equality follows from all original equal-gradient samples. The\nactual next point is x_n=x_(n-1)-d0*r=Z_r-T*r. Smoothness along precisely\nthis original step, by integrating the Lipschitz gradient, gives\n\n    f(x_n) <= f(x_(n-1))-d0||r||^2+d0^2||r||^2/2\n             =F_r-(T-d0^2/2)||r||^2.                    (7.1)\n\nConsequently (4.1) holds for z=f(x_n), t=T-d0^2/2. This is a charged\ninequality, NOT an alteration of the physical tail mass or a reset.\nAll earlier positions, queried values, and every minimum remain those\nof the one actual function being bounded. The proof in Section6\napplies with delta=(1+d0^2)/(2X), and now\n\n    2D=1+2S_m-d0^2>0.\n\nIt proves the upper bound in (1.3). Because the argument began at ANY\nchosen actual minimizer, moving to the actual closest one does not\nevade it. Completing an old prefix can change the complete minimum\nset, but applying these inequalities to that very completed function\nand its new closest minimizer already pays that change. There is no\nassumption that a radius from a different function is inherited.\n\nAll original remote/interior/optimum rows remain valid for each\nfunction in this quantification. They are not deleted from a primal\noptimization to assert existence. The proof uses a necessary subset\nto obtain a bound on the larger family, then pays the original last\nleaf by (7.1). This also covers arbitrary affine conjugate offsets\nand all genuine3D nonparallel terminal choices compatible with the\nfull prefix.\n\n## 8. Explicit sharp member and exact original-B comparison\n\nLet S=S_m, kappa=1/(1+2S), and define the scalar function\n\n    f_H(x)=x^2/2                         if |x|<=kappa,\n           kappa*|x|-kappa^2/2           if |x|>=kappa.\n\nStart at x_0=1. Its unique minimizer is0, its derivative is continuous,\nnondecreasing and1-Lipschitz, and it is one fixed global function.\nAt actual cumulative mass s<=S,\n\n    x=1-kappa*s >=1-kappa*S>kappa,\n    grad f_H(x)=kappa,\n    f_H(x)=kappa*(1-kappa*s)-kappa^2/2.\n\nThus every actual physical entry, including the actual last leaf, is\nexecuted with the same gradient. This is a member of all three pair\nfamilies with p=q=r=kappa. Its queried proximal heights are\n\n    delta(s)=f_H(x)-kappa^2/2=kappa-kappa^2*(s+1)>0,\n\nsince S>0 and s<=S<2S. Its OWN closest radius is exactly1 and\n\n    2f_H(x_n)=2kappa-(2S+1)kappa^2=1/(1+2S).\n\nThis proves equality in (1.2) and the lower half of (1.3). Nonparallel\ngradients cannot attain equality in Family E by positive definiteness,\nalthough we do not need to decide their restricted supremum.\n\nThe exact original benchmark is retained throughout:\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nNo dyadic-only recurrence is substituted for these all-integer-split\nmaxima. Fact5fd396eba75651e7 gives exactly\n\n    B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S_m)<237/250,\n\nat our SAME tau=1/4 and m>=1536. That proves the first inequality in\n(1.4). Put Z=1+2S_m. Equations(5.2),(5.4), with the Family E charge,\ngive Z>(23/2)X>=23552. Since d0^2<9/4, we have\nd0^2/Z<1/1000. Hence, for Family Q,\n\n    B_n V <= B_n/(Z-d0^2)\n           < (237/250)*(1000/999)=316/333<19/20.\n\nThis is a finite, uniform, quantitatively separated statement at every\ndeclared m, at the ACTUAL horizon n=2^m-1. It is a witness-family\nobstruction. It is neither a certificate for unrestricted W_n(h) nor\na negative resolution of the original minimax problem.\n\n## 9. What the original-space lift does and does not buy\n\nThe seven-endpoint lift shows that one can safely reason in finitely\nmany real vectors and paid scalar heights for this three-sector model.\nIt retains all intermediate samples and gives a genuine global smooth\nconvex interpolant, attained minimum, exact terminal and complete\nminimum hull. A3x3 PSD gradient Gram alone cannot replace its endpoint\ninterpolation tests or the x_0 inner products.\n\nThe certificate then proves a bound over the WHOLE declared geometry,\nnot just over one explicit feasible parameter law. This is why no\nnew numerical or symbolic optimizer is needed. In particular, adding\na third coordinate, freeing the angle of r relative to q, introducing\nindependent affine offsets, or choosing x_0 nonradially cannot repair\nthe canonical top-two choice while the actual gradients remain in\nthe three sectors (1.1). The same applies to the other two pairs\nprinted in Section5 and to arbitrary completions of their entire\nqueried prefixes, even after paying their new closest minima.\n\nNothing here declares all real three-gradient geometry exhausted on\nALL possible checkpoint pairs. It is also not an obstruction to a\nprojection hull whose actual gradients move along an edge inside one\nof the displayed blocks: such a trajectory violates (1.1).\n\n## 10. Concrete failure of the certificate on another actual pair\n\nTo test a fragile all-pair extrapolation, choose the whole-word root\nas the first checkpoint and the immediately following physical leaf\nas the second. These are actual positions j=2^(m-1)-1 and k=j+1.\nThis pair is outside Pairs_top for m>=3. Write U=X_(m-1) and\nDroot=a*c^(m-1). Its full masses are\n\n    u=U, v=1, d=Droot, e=a,\n    T=U-2-a, w=U-3/2-a,\n    D=2U+Droot-1/2.                                    (10.1)\n\nHere the middle block is empty, and the suffix is the full right child\nafter its ACTUAL first leaf. Formula(10.1) keeps all of that suffix.\nAs m tends to infinity in the SAME-enclosing-m tau1/4 family,\n\n    Droot/U -> lambda-2=:t0, U->infinity,\n    (u,v,d,e,w,D)/U -> (1,0,t0,0,1,lambda).\n\nThis follows from the exact mass formula in Section5: c->lambda,\na->lambda-2, v_h->1 and (2/c)^(m-1)->0, since c>lambda>2.\nAll denominators in (4.4) have positive limits. The weights tend to\n\n    alpha -> (lambda-1)/(2lambda-1),\n    eta -> (lambda-1)/(lambda+1)=lambda-2=t0.\n\nSubstitute these limits in (4.5), using lambda^2=2lambda+1.\nThe normalized limiting matrix entries are\n\n    J11/U -> (lambda-1)*t0/(2lambda-1)^2,\n    J22/U -> t0^2,\n    J12/U -> t0/(2lambda-1).\n\nIts determinant is exactly\n\n    lim det(J)/U^2 = -t0^3/(2lambda-1)^2 <0.              (10.2)\n\nFor example, the last simplification uses\n(lambda-1)t0=3-lambda. Thus the matrix is indefinite for all sufficiently\nlarge m in this very physical hierarchy. This is a rigorous failure\nof THIS PARTICULAR five-row certificate, not a construction of legal\ndata violating Huber and not a claim about the optimum at that pair.\nAdditional remote rows may repair the proof. A claimed all-pair\nextension of Section6 without such work would be false.\n\nThis test was textual and symbolic; no determinant evaluation or\ntrajectory replay was executed. It pinpoints the remaining degree\nof freedom: a materially different checkpoint selection, potentially\nwith essential remote-row information, remains open even within the\nthree-gradient program. More gradient sectors or a nonconstant\ngradient inside a block are also outside the present obstruction.\n\n## 11. Retrieval, resources and remaining theorem-level gap\n\nApplied skills: query-memory for prior state and exact scope;\nobtain-immediate-conclusions for block-value equalities and mass\nnormalization; direct-proving for the explicit weighted certificate;\nsearch-math-results for interpolation/conjugacy background; and\nconstruct-counterexamples for the failed all-pair extension in Section10.\n\nMatlas was queried for smooth convex interpolation and constructive\nstrongly convex conjugate envelopes. Its returned two lemmas did not\nsupply this block certificate. Web retrieval identified the primary\npaper by Adrien B. Taylor, Julien M. Hendrickx and Francois Glineur,\n\"Smooth Strongly Convex Interpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance\nof First-order Methods\", arXiv:1502.05666. reproduced at exact scope in report.", "U,Droot,t0": "Root-then-next-leaf certificate test parameters U=X_(m-1), Droot=a*c^(m-1), t0=lambda-2 in Section10; unrelated to U_n^ZJ."}}

In E3={sum x_j=0}, let D=conv{sigma(e_j-1/3*1):j=1,2,3}, sigma>0, and F(x)=max_(g in D)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2). Its full minimum is0 uniquely and every ordered row follows from the projection normal. Take x0=(sigma/3)(3,1,-4), with true radius squared26sigma^2/9. Threshold theta0=3/2 gives three projection levels (5/2,1/2,0)/3, gradient(sigma/3)(3/2,-1/2,-1), and height sigma^2/2>0. On the ACTUAL first leaf h0=1+a of SAME-m tau1/4 H_m, every m>=1536 has1/3<a<1/2, and w1=(3/2-3a/2,3/2+a/2,-3+a). Thus Wd0=7 and Wd1=(9-a)/2, with (h0/(h0-1))*Wd1-Wd0=(9-6a-a^2)/(2a)>0. This strictly contradicts any universal Wd0>=(h0/(h0-1))*Wd1 claim. The scoped Cartesian law does not include this initial state.
