PSD must hold on ALL original vectors.\n\n## 2. Mechanism I: chronological storage with a last-leaf objective interface\n\nThis is a completely new original-index array; it does not use inherited\nLambda or Kbase. It starts from original optimum-sector rows and finishes\nwith a two-gradient objective interface on the last physical leaf.\n\nFor each physical i define the scalars and vector expression\n\n    kap80_i=max(1,h_i-1),\n    r80_i=2h_i                 if h_i<2,\n        2h_i(h_i-1)          if h_i>2,\n    s80_i=h_i*abs(h_i-2),\n    z80_i=g_i                 if h_i<2,\n        x_i-g_i             if h_i>2.\n\nThese definitions refer to the actual h_i; no gradient is identified with\nanother gradient. Let jlast=n-1, Dleaf=1+2b, qleaf=1/Dleaf, and\n\n    Ppre=product_(0<=i<jlast)kap80_i^2,\n    w80_i^I=qleaf*product_(i<t<jlast)kap80_t^2  (i<jlast),\n    q_I=qleaf*Ppre.\n\nEvery nonzero original multiplier of Law I is\n\n    lambda_(i,star)=lambda_(star,i)=w80_i^I*r80_i, i<jlast;\n    lambda_(jlast,star)=1;\n    lambda_(n,star)=1;\n    lambda_(n,jlast)=1.                                  (I.1)\n\nAll other rows have coefficient zero. The support has no incoming actual\nterminal edge. All signs are positive. Prefix sector pairs are cycles;\nthe three last rows cancel f_(jlast) and give terminal flow2, so every\nfinite flow is correct. The full initial point, both endpoint gradients,\nand the endpoint value are present in this law.\n\nThe original-row identities are\n\n    kap80_i^2||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2\n           +r80_i(Q_(i,star)+Q_(star,i))=s80_i||z80_i||^2,    (I.2)\n\nand, writing X=x_(jlast), u=g_(jlast), v=g_n,\n\n    qleaf||X||^2-2f_n+Q_(jlast,star)+Q_(n,star)+Q_(n,jlast)\n      =qleaf||X-Dleaf(u+v)/2||^2\n                      +(3-2b)||u-v||^2/4.              (I.3)\n\nFor (I.2), Q_(i,star)+Q_(star,i)=||g_i||^2-<x_i,g_i>.\nWhen h_i<2, direct expansion leaves h_i(2-h_i)||g_i||^2.\nWhen h_i>2, it leaves h_i(h_i-2)||x_i-g_i||^2.\nFor (I.3), expanding the three rows gives\n\n    qleaf||X||^2-<X,u+v>+||u||^2+(2b-1)<u,v>+||v||^2.\n\nExpansion of the displayed two squares has exactly these coefficients.\nSince b<3/2 both square coefficients are positive. Multiplying (I.2)\nby w80_i^I and adding (I.3) proves the ENTIRE identity\n\n    M_I(q_I)=sum_(i<jlast)w80_i^I*s80_i||z80_i||^2\n       +qleaf||x_(jlast)-Dleaf(g_(jlast)+g_n)/2||^2\n       +(3-2b)||g_(jlast)-g_n||^2/4.                    (I.4)\n\nThere is no omitted displacement or residual. This gives a full-function\nupper-certificate draft at q_I, not at the requested q=17/(16D).\n\nAt any real original cut r<s<=jlast, the same identity reads\n\n    [product_(r<=i<s)kap80_i^2]||x_r||^2-||x_s||^2\n      +sum_(r<=i<s)[product_(i<t<s)kap80_t^2]r80_i\n                              (Q_(i,star)+Q_(star,i))\n      =sum_(r<=i<s)[product_(i<t<s)kap80_t^2]s80_i||z80_i||^2. (I.5)\n\nTwo consecutive blocks compose by multiplying the first identity by the\nsecond block's product and adding, with its ACTUAL common x_s. At a\ndyadic parent, the actual one-step bridge is an additional such factor.\nThe child displacement is always sum h_i g_i and x_s=x_r-sum h_i g_i.\nThese are context-dependent suffix weights, not fictitious resets.\nThey solve all-depth positivity for this law analytically, but the\nproduct growth costs too much. Section7 excludes *any* useful coefficient\nchoice on a much richer support, without assuming scalar-only storage.\n\n## 3. Mechanism II: dense checkpoint circulation and full terminal storage\n\nThe second family permits real mixed boundary information and all enclosing\ncontext. Its coefficient support is much larger than Law I. Let\n\n    Leaf={0,2,...,n-1}, Check={1,3,...,n-2}.\n\nThere are Nleaf leaves and Nleaf-1 queried checkpoints. Allow these rows:\n\n  (a) both optimum rows for EVERY original finite index, including n;\n  (b) EVERY ordered pair of distinct checkpoints;\n  (c) EVERY leaf-to-checkpoint row Q_(ell,p), ell in Leaf, p in Check;\n  (d) checkpoint-to-leaf rows Q_(p,ell) when |p-ell|=1;\n  (e) EVERY outgoing actual-terminal row Q_(n,i), i<n.\n\nCall this support E80. It contains both directions of every adjacent\nqueried-prefix pair, complete checkpoint coupling, all outgoing leaf\nlinks to all checkpoints regardless of ancestors, and the whole terminal\nfan. It permits optimum-to-terminal. It excludes incoming queried-to-\nterminal rows and remote checkpoint-to-leaf / leaf-to-leaf rows.\n\nFor the support-level obstruction later, EVERY nonnegative coefficient\non E80 is free, with arbitrary dependence on m and full ancestor context.\nStorage may be any quadratic form in x_0, actual boundary positions and\nboundary gradients, with value terms and all real mixed inner products.\nIt may use arbitrarily many such boundary coordinates. The only restriction\nis that its telescoped original-row certificate uses E80. No reduction\nto a scalar metric is presumed.\n\nHere is an explicit FULL feasible coefficient law inside that family, as\na starting point. It proves existence of a genuine changed base and makes\nevery added cost auditable. Put\n\n    Pfull=product_(i<n)kap80_i^2,\n    w80_i=product_(i<t<n)kap80_t^2,\n    Kprod=product_(i<n)(1+h_i),\n    alpha80=epsilon/[(n+2)^2*Kprod^2],\n    eta80=alpha80/[4(S+2)(n+1)^2],\n    q_II=Pfull+1.\n\nStart with coefficients lambda_(i,star)=lambda_(star,i)=w80_i*r80_i for i<n,\nlambda_(n,star)=2, and every other entry zero. To these add eta80 on\neach directed edge of each of the following cycles, counting overlaps:\n\n    A_(ell,p): ell -> p -> star -> ell,\n                           for every ell in Leaf and p in Check;\n    B_(p,r):   p -> r -> p, for every unordered pair in Check;\n    C_(p,ell): p -> ell -> p, for every |p-ell|=1;\n    T_i:       n -> i -> star -> n, for every 0<=i<n.      (II.1)\n\nThe arrow denotes the ordered Q source and receiver, not a GD update.\nAll cycles use original indices. Formula (II.1) specifies EVERY entry\nwithout an optimization problem or unknown PSD block. More explicitly,\nwrite rcheck=Nleaf-1. The finite entries are\n\n    lambda_(p,r)=eta80                (distinct p,r in Check),\n    lambda_(ell,p)=eta80*(1+1_(|p-ell|=1)),\n    lambda_(p,ell)=eta80*1_(|p-ell|=1),\n    lambda_(n,i)=eta80                (0<=i<n).\n\nThe optimum entries, including EVERY incoming one, are\n\n    lambda_(ell,star)=w80_ell*r80_ell+eta80,\n    lambda_(star,ell)=w80_ell*r80_ell+rcheck*eta80,\n    lambda_(p,star)=w80_p*r80_p+(Nleaf+1)*eta80,\n    lambda_(star,p)=w80_p*r80_p,\n    lambda_(n,star)=2,\n    lambda_(star,n)=n*eta80.                              (II.2)\n\nAll unlisted entries, including diagonals, are zero. Every scalar shown\nis strictly positive where its indicator is one. Each cycle has zero\nvalue flow at each vertex, and the initial array has only terminal flow2.\nHence the full original value cancellation holds exactly. In particular\nthere is no implicit subtraction of an incoming optimum cost.\n\nLet Cyc be the list in (II.1), and write Q_C=sum_(i,j in cycle C)Q_ij.\nThe exact entire-word composition identity is\n\n    M_II(q_II)=||x_0||^2+sum_(i<n)w80_i*s80_i||z80_i||^2\n                  +||x_n-g_n||^2+eta80*sum_(C in Cyc)Q_C. (II.3)\n\nIt follows by (I.2) on ALL physical steps and\n||x_n||^2-2f_n+2Q_(n,star)=||x_n-g_n||^2.\nEvery Q_C is kept with its original mixed terms and both original\ndisplacements. Individual cycles are NOT asserted PSD.\n\nFor a cut or dyadic composition, apply (I.5) to each physical child and\nthe actual bridge, multiply by the enclosing suffix product, and then\nadd the cycles assigned to that parent. One unambiguous assignment is\nthe smallest dyadic node containing all finite vertices of a cycle;\nif that set is not contained in a proper node, assign it to the root.\nRows involving n are assigned to the root. There is no need to discard\ncrossing cycles: their exact Q_C polynomials form the boundary coupling.\nThis is a complete additive identity, not a claim of local child PSD.\n\nFor completeness, the all-depth PSD expenditure is explicit. Every\nw80_i>=1 and s80_i>=epsilon. At level1, s80_i=(2-epsilon)epsilon>=epsilon.\nAt level0 use b>1 and 2-b>1/2. At higher levels h_i>3, so s80_i>3.\nLet z=x_n-g_n and let Vnorm^2=||x_0||^2+sum_(i<=n)||g_i||^2.\nThe triangular change of coordinates from (x_0,z80_0,...,z80_(n-1),z)\nto the original vectors has the following elementary bound:\n\n    Vnorm^2 <= (n+2)^2*Kprod^2\n                   [||x_0||^2+sum_(i<n)||z80_i||^2+||z||^2]. (II.4)\n\nIndeed, for h_i<2 one has g_i=z80_i and x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i z80_i;\nfor h_i>2 one has g_i=x_i-z80_i and\nx_(i+1)=(1-h_i)x_i+h_i z80_i. Induction using factors1+h_i bounds\neach x_i and each g_i by Kprod times the sum of residual-vector norms\nappearing up to that coordinate. The last equality g_n=x_n-z obeys the\nsame bound including z. Cauchy--Schwarz for n+2 terms, followed by summing\nthe n+2 original squared norms, proves (II.4).\nThus the first three terms of (II.3) are at least alpha80*Vnorm^2.\n\nAfter deleting its value terms, every individual Q row has absolute\nquadratic value at most (S+2)*Vnorm^2. For two finite indices use\n||x_i-x_j||<=S*Vnorm and ||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=Vnorm^2.\nFor an outgoing optimum row use ||x_i||<=(1+S)Vnorm, and for an incoming\noptimum row its quadratic is ||g_i||^2/2. The value terms of each cycle\ncancel, so the row bound can be summed inside that cycle.\n\nThe number of row occurrences over all cycles is exactly\n\n    Lcyc=3Nleaf(Nleaf-1)+(Nleaf-1)(Nleaf-2)\n                          +4(Nleaf-1)+3n\n        =4Nleaf^2+4Nleaf-5 < 2(n+1)^2.\n\nConsequently the absolute sum of all added cycle quadratics is strictly\nless than alpha80*Vnorm^2/2. Equation (II.3) proves\n\n    M_II(q_II) >= alpha80*Vnorm^2/2.                     (II.5)\n\nThis pays ALL cross-cycle and initial costs once, on the full space, for\nevery m>=1536. It is a full certificate draft at the very poor q_II. It\ndoes not suggest that cycles may be added freely at the desired tiny q.\n\n## 4. Required original-space screens\n\nUse exactly the earlier H4 direction: x_0=0, g_0,...,g_3=1,\ng_8,...,g_11=-1, and every other original gradient, including g_n, zero.\nThe two active blocks have equal physical mass, so their displacement\ncancels exactly. For Law I, (I.4) has all nonnegative terms and its\nfirst term is w80_0^I*b(2-b)>0. For Law II, (II.5) is strictly positive\non this nonzero original vector. These conclusions use the FULL forms;\nthere is no projection to a primitive or constant-gradient subspace.\n\nFor the deep wave at every 2<=j<=m-3, let p=2^j-1, Hbridge=1+d_j,\nx_0=0, g_0=-Hbridge/b, g_p=1, and all other original gradients zero.\nAgain b*g_0+Hbridge*g_p=0, exactly. In Law I the first nonnegative\nterm is w80_0^I*b(2-b)*(Hbridge/b)^2>0. Law II is positive by (II.5).\nBoth screens therefore PASS for both replacement bases, even if their\ninitial coefficient q is replaced by17/(16D): x_0=0 on both tests, so\nthat replacement does not change either value. Passing does not imply\na useful objective level; Section7 supplies a different test.\n\n## 5. Full initial/common-gradient and actual-terminal accounts\n\nFor either law, and indeed for any array on E80 with the correct flow,\nput K_ij=lambda_ij for finite i!=j, mu_i=lambda_(i,star),\nnu_i=lambda_(star,i), and h_n=0 as NOTATION only. The full polynomial is\n\n    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-sum_i mu_i<x_0,g_i>\n                                  +sum_(i,j)C_ij<g_i,g_j>,\n    C_ii=mu_i+sum_v K_iv-1_(i=n)-h_i*sum_(v>i)K_iv,\n    2C_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j*sum_(v>j)K_iv\n                    +h_i[mu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j.    (5.1)\n\nTo check the diagonal, half the total incident multiplier mass equals\nmu_i+sum_v K_iv-1_(i=n), by flow including nu_i. Forward rows out of i\ncontribute the additional -h_i sum_(v>i)K_iv. For i<j, squared gradient\ndifferences give the first term, forward rows out of i crossing j give\nthe second, and rows out of j reaching index<=i or the optimum give\nthe third. Incoming optimum rows have no initial mixed term. Thus no\nnu_i or displacement contribution has been silently omitted.\n\nLet e be the all-ones gradient column and Ppos80_i=sum_(t<i)h_t. Then\n\n    U80=sum_i mu_i=2+sum_i nu_i,\n    V80=e^T C e=D+sum_i nu_i(1+Ppos80_i).                 (5.2)\n\nFor a direct check, set g_i=1 and f_i=X-Ppos80_i-1/2. All finite Q rows\nand outgoing optimum rows vanish, while Q_(star,i)=1-X+Ppos80_i.\nSubstitution yields qX^2-U80 X+V80, proving (5.2). The initial/common\nblock is B80=[[q,-U80/2],[-U80/2,V80]]. In particular neither old Huber\ncontact derivative nor the old D/16 slack may be assumed for these bases.\n\nFor any variation v of the gradients orthogonal to e, put\nu80=mu^T v and k80=e^T C v. If Delta80=q V80-U80^2/4>0, exact\nelimination of the ONE initial/common block charges\n\n    [V80*u80^2/4-U80*u80*k80/2+q*k80^2]/Delta80.       (5.3)\n\nThe remaining quadratic is v^T C v minus (5.3). This formula includes\nall initial and common cross terms in one global charge. At Delta80=0\none must instead impose the range condition and use the corresponding\nsingular Schur complement. At the explicit q_I and q_II, the square\nidentities (I.4) and (II.5) directly establish PSD without assuming away\nthat condition; (II.5) in particular makes the common block positive\ndefinite. At the desired q, Section7 directly contradicts full PSD.\n\nLaw I has outgoing terminal probabilities1/2 at star and jlast. Its\nactual terminal square, from expansion of its rows or fact1252(c), is\n\n    M_I(q)=||g_n-Vlast||^2+Rlast,\n    Vlast=x_n-(x_(jlast)-g_(jlast))/2,\n    Rlast=q||x_0||^2-[f_(jlast)-||g_(jlast)||^2/2]-||Vlast||^2\n           +sum_(i!=j in {star,0,...,n-1})lambda_ij Q_ij.  (5.4)\n\nThe negative square cost in that prefix remainder is retained by (I.4)\nand (5.1); no terminal gradient is fixed by a quotient assumption.\n\nLaw II has incoming optimum-terminal mass n*eta80. Define\n\n    Aterm=1+n*eta80,\n    Bterm=(1+n*eta80/2)x_n\n                       -(eta80/2)sum_(i<n)(x_i-g_i)\n         =x_0-sum_(t<n)[h_t(1+eta80(t+1)/2)-eta80/2]g_t,\n    delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.\n\nIts exact ACTUAL-terminal completion is\n\n    M_II(q)=Aterm||g_n-Bterm/Aterm||^2+Rprefix,\n    Rprefix=q||x_0||^2+sum_(i!=j in {star,0,...,n-1})lambda_ij Q_ij\n                       -eta80*sum_(i<n)delta_i-||Bterm||^2/Aterm. (5.5)\n\nExpanding 2Q_(n,star)+eta80 sum_(i<n)Q_(n,i)+n eta80 Q_(star,n)\nproves (5.5): the terminal gradient coefficient is1+n eta80, its\nlinear coefficient is -2Bterm, and every f_n term cancels -2f_n.\nThe original prefix flow cancels the remaining -eta80 sum f_i.\nBoth initial and terminal costs are present. The probability formula\nfor an incoming-terminal-free certificate is deliberately NOT applied\nto Law II's different chosen support.\n\n## 6. A complete restricted-row transcript at every physical index\n\nThis construction tests ALL coefficient choices on E80, not merely the\ntwo explicit laws. It also shows why full boundary metrics cannot fix\nmissing rows by themselves. Define\n\n    Rreset=a*c^2/Mleaf=beta*c/Mleaf,\n    sbit(k)=number of ones in the binary expansion of integer k>=0,\n    v80_k=(-1)^k*Rreset^k*c^(-sbit(k))/Mleaf,\n                                      0<=k<Nleaf.                   (6.1)\n\nHere sbit(0)=0. This is a closed formula, never an executed recurrence.\nIf r=v2(k+1), binary carry gives sbit(k+1)-sbit(k)=1-r. Therefore\n\n    v80_(k+1)/v80_k=-Rreset*c^(r-1)\n               =-a*c^(r+1)/Mleaf=-d_(1+r)/Mleaf.        (6.2)\n\nFor each leaf index2k prescribe the REAL scalar jets\n\n    x_(2k)=Mleaf*v80_k,  g_(2k)=v80_k,\n    f_(2k)=(b+1/2)*v80_k^2.\n\nFor each queried checkpoint2k+1<n prescribe\n\n    x_(2k+1)=v80_k, g_(2k+1)=v80_k, f_(2k+1)=v80_k^2/2.     (6.3)\n\nThe leaf update gives Mleaf*v80_k-b*v80_k=v80_k. The following bridge update\ngives v80_k-(1+d_(1+v2(k+1)))*v80_k=Mleaf*v80_(k+1), by (6.2).\nThus these are the EXACT original physical GD relations. In particular\nx_0=Mleaf*v80_0=1; there is no freedom to change the radius in the proof.\n\nAfter the final physical leaf the actual terminal position is\n\n    t80=x_n=v80_(Nleaf-1)\n       =-Rreset^(Nleaf-1)/(Mleaf*c^(m-1)).               (6.4)\n\nThe sign is negative because Nleaf-1 is odd. Its binary expansion has\nm-1 ones. This gives (6.4) directly from (6.1), including the last leaf.\nComplete that terminal with\n\n    u80term=g_n=2t80/(b+2),\n    f_n=t80^2/(b+2),\n    x_star=g_star=f_star=0.                            (6.5)\n\nThere is no assertion of a single function interpolating these jets.\nTheir scalar Gram matrix is PSD because all vectors are literal scalars.\n\nWe now check EVERY row type in E80, including orientations and the\nterminal; no numerical check or finite-horizon inference is used.\n\nAt a leaf with parameter v,\n\n    Q_(leaf,star)=0, Q_(star,leaf)=-b*v^2.\n\nAt a checkpoint with parameter w, x=g=w and f=w^2/2, so both optimum\nrows and all ordered checkpoint-checkpoint rows are zero. For EVERY\nleaf v and checkpoint w, regardless of order or distance,\n\n    Q_(leaf(v),check(w))=0,\n    Q_(check(w),leaf(v))=b*v*(w-v).                    (6.6)\n\nThese formulas follow by substituting (6.3). They also hold with w=0\nfor the optimum. A neighboring checkpoint AFTER a leaf has w=v,\nso both adjacent rows vanish. At a checkpoint BEFORE the next leaf,\nv=-d*w/Mleaf with d>0; hence\n\n    b*v*(w-v)=-b*(d/Mleaf)*(1+d/Mleaf)*w^2<0.          (6.7)\n\nThese are ALL adjacent prefix incidences, so all of them satisfy Q<=0.\n\nPut u=u80term. Equation (6.5) gives\n\n    f_n-u*t80+u^2/2=-b*u^2/4.\n\nFor each queried checkpoint, including the optimum, its outgoing\nterminal row is therefore -b*u^2/4. For ANY leaf parameter v the\nterminal row is exactly\n\n    Q_(n,leaf(v))=-b*(v-u/2)^2<=0.                    (6.8)\n\nIndeed its expression is f_n-u*t80+u^2/2+b*u*v-b*v^2.\nThe remaining incoming optimum-terminal row is\nQ_(star,n)=-f_n+u^2/2=-b*u^2/4<=0.\nThis checks all row types(a)--(e), every optimum row, every leaf,\nevery checkpoint, and the actual unqueried terminal.\n\n## 7. All-depth growth, exact lower obstruction for E80, and failed target\n\nThis section is entirely symbolic. Since\nMleaf=lambda-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],\n\n    Rreset-1\n      =epsilon*[2/(lambda(1+epsilon))-lambda*epsilon]/Mleaf.\n\nWith epsilon<1/8 and lambda<5/2 the bracket is greater than\n32/45-5/16=287/720>5/16. Also Mleaf<5/2. Thus\n\n    Rreset>1+epsilon/8=1+1/(32m).                     (7.1)\n\nThe integer inequality 2^(m-1)-1>=96m^2 holds for m>=16. At16 it is\n32767>=24576. If it holds at m, then\n2^m-1=2(2^(m-1)-1)+1>=192m^2+1>=96(m+1)^2,\nbecause 96m^2-192m-95>0 for m>=16. This proves the assertion by induction.\nAll our m are in that range. Consequently\n\n    Nleaf-1>=96m^2>32m(2m+1).\n\nBernoulli's binomial inequality gives (1+1/(32m))^(32m)>=2. Together\nwith (7.1), this yields Rreset^(Nleaf-1)>2^(2m+1). Since Mleaf<4 and\nc<4, one has Mleaf*c^(m-1)<2^(2m). Formula (6.4) now gives\n\n    |t80|>2,\n    2f_n=2t80^2/(b+2)>8/(b+2)>16/7>2.               (7.2)\n\nThe exact expression, stronger than this convenient coarse bound, is\n\n    Qrelax80(m)=2*Rreset^(2Nleaf-2)\n                      /[(b+2)*Mleaf^2*c^(2m-2)].       (7.3)\n\nFor ANY nonnegative array supported on E80 with the required original\nvalue flow, evaluate its full polynomial on (6.1)--(6.5). Its initial\nnorm is1 and EVERY selected interpolation row is nonpositive. Therefore\n\n    M_cert(q) <= q-Qrelax80(m).\n\nFull PSD forces\n\n    q>=Qrelax80(m)>2, for EVERY m>=1536.               (7.4)\n\nThis is a support-level impossibility proof attempt: arbitrary weights,\nboth optimum directions, arbitrary context, arbitrary matrix boundary\npotentials, complete checkpoint couplings and the full terminal fan do\nnot escape it if their final row support stays inside E80. A free\nnonnegative square added to a storage derivation does not change this\nargument: after telescoping, the original-row certificate itself must\nstill be PSD and is tested by the displayed original vectors and values.\n\nThe requested q=17/(16D) is less than1 since S>=b>1 and D>3.\nThus (7.4) excludes it by a large margin for both mechanisms and their\nwhole specified coefficient family. This is not merely a bad choice of\neta80 or of the context weights. It is also not a contradiction with\nthe genuine full certificates (I.4), (II.5): their explicit levels are\nnecessarily at least (7.3), and the same calculation forces that fact.\nNo eigenvalue computation or automated recurrence was used to reach it.\n\nAt epsilon=0, Rreset=1 and (6.4) gives t80=-lambda^(-m). The growth\nin (7.2) specifically comes from the small positive SAME-m deformation\nrepeated across all leaves; it is not an objection to the undeformed\nsilver schedule's full certificate.\n\n## 8. Exact omitted prefix row and the missing boundary inequality\n\nThe transcript is provably NOT a legal primal instance. Let\n\n    pbad=n-2=2(Nleaf-2)+1, ellbad=0,\n    vfirst=v80_0=1/Mleaf,\n    wbad=v80_(Nleaf-2)=-Mleaf*t80/d_1.\n\nHere pbad is an actual queried checkpoint, and ellbad is the actual\ninitial leaf. Since t80<-2, d_1=1-epsilon<1 and Mleaf>2,\nwbad>2Mleaf>vfirst. Formula (6.6) gives the explicit forbidden row\n\n    Q_(pbad,0)=b*vfirst*(wbad-vfirst)>0.                (8.1)\n\nThis is a remote queried-prefix row, neither an incoming-terminal row\nnor a duplicate-gradient constraint. There is no way to infer its sign\nfrom E80: the complete transcript just checked is a counterexample to\nthat inference. The reverse row Q_(0,pbad) is present and equals zero.\nThis directional distinction is essential.\n\nOne economical new support direction is the nonnegative symmetric pair\nQ_(pbad,0)+Q_(0,pbad). It has zero value flow and acts positively by\n(8.1) on this transcript. But it is indefinite on the original formal\nspace. For ANY queried u<v its full polynomial is\n\n    Q_uv+Q_vu=||g_u-g_v||^2\n                 -<g_u-g_v,sum_(u<=t<v)h_t g_t>.      (8.2)\n\nSetting only g_u nonzero gives (1-h_u)||g_u||^2, which is negative\nwhen h_u>1; setting only g_v nonzero gives ||g_v||^2>0. Thus adding\nthis row pair is not a free PSD repair and starts a genuinely new\nunallocated coefficient mechanism. No such third mechanism is attempted.\n\nThe precise unresolved boundary construction is as follows. On the SAME\nHword_m, find nonnegative original multipliers with full flow and genuine\nremote checkpoint-to-leaf or other excluded prefix rows such that, with\nC and mu formed by (5.1),\n\n    C - (4q)^(-1)mu*mu^T >= 0 on ALL gradient coordinates,\n    q=17/(16D),                                           (8.3)\n\nor any other rigorously sufficient original-benchmark-beating level.\nEvery actual terminal and initial cost must remain in C and mu. Formula\n(8.3) is the exact Schur condition for q>0. {"id": "c4679166c63cc384", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T18:12:37.254356+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "max81 original-space counterdirections, JOINT UNVERIFIED: Law I is negative at x0=0 with every queried gradient -r81_p/2 except root 1-r81_p/2 and terminal (b-1)r81_p/2. Law II is negative at x0=0 with opposite unit gradients at the two child roots and every other original gradient zero; their equal physical steps make displacement exactly zero. These refute only the advertised coefficient laws and are not interpolable primal functions or W lower bounds.", "evidence": "# Paid remote leaf consistency: two explicit laws and their full-space failures\n\nJOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report, worker max81. Original target OPEN.\nGoverning assignment: TASK.md and guidance60cc1ca7a8ad2d07. One invocation,\nat most two mechanisms, no automatic renewal. All calculations below form\none unverified proof attempt. No intermediate draft is used as accepted truth.\nNo mathematical program, numerical/arithmetic/trajectory/recurrence replay,\noptimizer, solver, formal call, fact_submit, installation, subagent or consult.\n\nTwo new original-index arrays are constructed, outside the old E80 support.\nBoth have nonnegative coefficients and exact objective value flow. Neither\nhas full PSD, at the requested level or even at an arbitrary initial-norm\ncoefficient. We give explicit original-vector counterdirections. A larger\ncontact-routed fan family is excluded by the same calculation. The second\nlaw also gives a quantitative necessary restriction on receiver distributions:\nmass sent across an earlier checkpoint must decrease on the scale of the\nreciprocal of that checkpoint's physical step, when the two checkpoint\ndegrees agree. These are scoped coefficient obstructions, not W bounds.\n\n## 1. Scope, accepted inputs and literal hierarchy\n\nFor every n>=0, L>0 and predetermined h in [0,infinity)^n, W_n(h) is the\nsupremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite\ndimension, convex differentiable L-smooth f with an attained minimizer,\nevery such x_star and x_0!=x_star, with all n actual updates\nx_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R. The original question concerns\nlim_n B_n inf_h W_n(h), with every nonnegative word, including zero and\narbitrarily large steps. No result below changes those quantifiers.\n\nThe benchmark is the original all-integer-split recurrence\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt(u^2+6uv+v^2))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290, read in full, supplies the original interpolation\norientation, full independent-vector certificate interpretation and exact\nactual-terminal completion. It deletes no queried-prefix inequalities.\nAccepted931b9653ce0f7201, read in full, supplies B_n/D<47/50 for the\nfollowing SAME-enclosing-m hierarchy. Its implication from W<=17/(16D)\nis conditional; that hypothesis is still unproved. No other accepted\nmathematical result is used here. Prior finding0073a83e08ff2e21 was read\nin full as awareness; the reset construction needed below is rederived.\n\nFix m>=1536. Define\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),\n    n=2^m-1, h=H_m, S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S,\n    z=n-1, b=1+a, M=1+b, N=2^(m-1), A=D-b^2,\n    P81_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, Tail81_i=S-b-P81_i (i<z),\n    K81_i=A-P81_i-1=2S-b^2-P81_i (i<z), qtar=17/(16D).\n\nAll children use this same m. Index z is the last queried leaf, not the\nunqueried terminal n. All these hierarchy symbols are local to this report.\nLet level(i)=v2(i+1), the exponent of 2 dividing i+1. Then\nh_i=1+a*c^level(i), leaves are the even indices, and checkpoints the odd\nindices. In particular h_z=b. Write Leaf={0,2,...,z}, Check={1,3,...,n-2}.\n\nElementary bounds used throughout are\n\n    1/3<a<1/2, 4/3<b<sqrt(2)<3/2,\n    12/5<c<5/2, beta>7/8,\n    d_1=beta, d_2=beta*c>21/10,\n    S>2^m-1, D>2^m, A>0, K81_i>Tail81_i>0.\n\nFor example a>1/3 follows from beta>7/8 and c<5/2. The bound on c\nfollows from lambda<17/7 and epsilon<=1/6144. Also\nb=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]. The other comparisons follow by\nsubstitution. K81_i-Tail81_i=S+b-b^2>0 since b<2 and S>=b. These observations\ninclude positivity of every denominator in the arrays below.\n\nDefine a dyadic cell of height j to be\n\n    I_(j,r)={r*2^j,...,(r+1)*2^j-2},\n    1<=j<=m, 0<=r<2^(m-j).\n\nFor distinct queried indices u,v, J(u,v) is the least height of a cell\ncontaining both. Such a cell exists and its height is unique. This defines\nthe exact least common dyadic ancestor in ORIGINAL indices. For i in the\nleft half, including the whole-word root, J(i,z)=m.\n\nNormalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0, and retain independent original\nvectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n. Positions are always\nx_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. For distinct labels put\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nLegal samples have Q_ij<=0. An original coefficient array with\nout_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n) defines\n\n    M_cert(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij.\n\nAfter value cancellation, it must be PSD on ALL original vectors to be an\nupper certificate. A negative formal vector is not a primal instance.\n\n## 2. Mechanism I: position-sensitive remote routing to the last leaf\n\nFirst construct the entire family with independently chosen 0<=theta_i<=1\nfor each i<z. Set\n\n    r81_i=2theta_i*h_i/K81_i,\n    w81_i=2(1-theta_i)*h_i/Tail81_i.\n\nThe frozen LCA-sensitive law tested in Sections 6--8 is\n\n    eta=1/(4D), theta_i=1-eta/J(i,z).                    (I.1)\n\nEvery remote pair then has positive weight. The literal nonzero array is\n\n    lambda_(n,z)=2,\n    lambda_(z,star)=2-sum_(i<z)r81_i,\n    lambda_(i,star)=r81_i,\n    lambda_(z,i)=r81_i+w81_i, lambda_(i,z)=w81_i, i<z.       (I.2)\n\nAll unlisted rows are zero, including all incoming terminal and optimum\nrows. This array includes checkpoint-to-last-leaf rows at arbitrary\ndistance and leaf-to-last-leaf rows. Its support is not contained in E80.\n\nAll signs can be checked without an unknown feasibility condition. Put\nKbase=S+b-b^2>0. Since K81_i=Kbase+Tail81_i, and Tail81_i decreases by h_i as i\nincreases, integration of the decreasing function 1/(Kbase+t) gives\n\n    sum_(i<z) h_i/(Kbase+Tail81_i)\n      <= integral_0^(S-b) dt/(Kbase+t)\n      =log((2S-b^2)/(S+b-b^2)) <log 2<1.\n\nIndeed h_i/(Kbase+Tail81_i) is bounded by the integral over\n[Tail81_i-h_i,Tail81_i], and these intervals partition [0,S-b]. The ratio is below\n2 because b<2. Thus sum r81_i<2 for every allowed theta. Every coefficient\nin (I.2) is nonnegative, with strict outgoing optimum mass at z.\nAt i<z the two finite directions have net inflow r81_i, exactly discharged\nto star. At z the finite/optimum outgoing net is 2, supplied by (n,z).\nTerminal flow is 2. This proves all value cancellations.\n\nThis is a new source-routing base, not an addition to any inherited\ncertificate. Its terminal distribution is concentrated at the actual last\nleaf; its entire optimum distribution is given in (I.2).\n\n### 2.1 Full polynomial and the paid contact equation\n\nWrite g=g_z, v_i=g_i-g for i<z and u=sum r81_i v_i. Put\n\n    B(v)=sum_(i<z)[r81_i+w81_i(1-h_i)]||v_i||^2\n         +sum_(i<t<z)(h_i*r81_t-w81_i*h_t)<v_i,v_t>,\n    Jrem(v)=B(v)-A||u||^2/4.\n\nThe exact full identity, for EVERY real q, is\n\n    M_I(q)=||g_n+(b-1)g||^2\n       +q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,g>+A||g||^2\n       -<x_0,u>+A<g,u>+B(v).                         (I.3)\n\nHere is a direct derivation. Starting with rows 2Q_(n,z)+2Q_(z,star),\nreplace r81_i units of Q_(z,star) by Q_(z,i)+Q_(i,star), and add w81_i\nunits of Q_(i,z)+Q_(z,i). The two added expressions, after GD, are\n\n    Q_(z,i)+Q_(i,star)-Q_(z,star)\n       =-<v_i,x_0>+(P81_i+1)<v_i,g>\n                          +||v_i||^2+sum_(t<i)h_t<v_i,v_t>,\n    Q_(i,z)+Q_(z,i)\n       =||v_i||^2-Tail81_i<v_i,g>-sum_(i<=t<z)h_t<v_i,v_t>.\n\nThe original base has terminal square ||g_n+(b-1)g||^2 and remainder\nq||x_0||^2-2<x_0,g>+A||g||^2+2sum h_i<v_i,g>.\nThe defining relation\n\n    w81_i*Tail81_i=2h_i-K81_i*r81_i                              (I.4)\n\nchanges each last cross coefficient to A*r81_i, giving (I.3). In particular,\nremote symmetric pairs were NOT treated as PSD. Their negative diagonal\nand mixed displacement terms appear in B.\n\nAt q=1/A, (I.3) is equivalently\n\n    M_I(1/A)=||g_n+(b-1)g||^2\n      +||x_0-A*g-(A/2)u||^2/A+Jrem(v).               (I.5)\n\nEquation (I.4) is an intentional design restriction of this family. It is\nnot claimed necessary for a general certificate. No zero-contact rule is\nimposed on an unrestricted optimum. The term -A||u||^2/4 is the ONE\nglobal initial/common-gradient charge for this deliberately balanced law.\n\n### 2.2 Full-space failure for every member of the contact family\n\nTake p=2^(m-1)-1, H=h_p>4. Only v_p is nonzero, with v_p=1. The coefficient\nof Jrem on this vector is\n\n    E_p=r81_p-w81_p(H-1)-A*r81_p^2/4.                       (I.6)\n\nIf theta_p<=1/2, then r81_p<=H/K81_p and w81_p>=H/Tail81_p. Thus\nr81_p-w81_p(H-1)<=H/K81_p-H(H-1)/Tail81_p<0 because K81_p>Tail81_p and H>2.\nIf theta_p>=1/2, then r81_p>=H/K81_p>H/A>4/A, and\nr81_p-A*r81_p^2/4<0; subtracting w81_p(H-1)>=0 preserves negativity.\nSo E_p<0 for EVERY choice of the whole profile theta.\n\nThe complete scalar original-vector counterdirection is\n\n    x_0=0,\n    g_i=-r81_p/2 for every queried i!=p,\n    g_p=1-r81_p/2,\n    g_n=(b-1)r81_p/2.                                  (I.7)\n\nIt sets g=-r81_p/2, u=r81_p, makes the terminal square and the square in\n(I.5) zero, and gives M_I(q)=E_p<0 for every real q. The change from\n1/A to q multiplies ||x_0||^2=0. Hence even unlimited initial-norm slack\ncannot repair this coefficient family. This is stronger than rejection\nof the frozen small-eta profile, but only for family (I.2)--(I.4).\n\n## 3. Mechanism II: dense earlier-leaf transport with paid reverse cycles\n\nPut Tc=sum_(p in Check)h_p and t_p=h_p/Tc, so sum t_p=1. For EVERY\ncheckpoint p and earlier leaf ell<p define\n\n    raw_(p,ell)=[1+(p-ell)/(p+1)]/J(p,ell),\n    Z_p=sum_(ell in Leaf,ell<p)raw_(p,ell),\n    omega_(p,ell)=raw_(p,ell)/Z_p,\n    mu_ell=sum_(p in Check,p>ell)t_p*omega_(p,ell).    (II.1)\n\nThere is at least one earlier leaf for every checkpoint, so Z_p>0.\nThe weights depend on the exact least common ancestor and position within\nthe enclosing chronological range. They are not uniform fan averages.\nThe formula for mu_ell is used only for ell<z. The new optimum distribution is mu_ell at\nearlier leaves, zero at every checkpoint, and mass 1 at z.\n\nFor a global reverse-cycle amount kappa>=0, the array is\n\n    lambda_(n,z)=2, lambda_(z,star)=1,\n    lambda_(z,p)=t_p, p in Check,\n    lambda_(p,ell)=(1+kappa)t_p*omega_(p,ell),\n    lambda_(ell,p)=kappa*t_p*omega_(p,ell), ell<p,\n    lambda_(ell,star)=mu_ell, ell in Leaf, ell<z.      (II.2)\n\nEvery unlisted entry is zero. The frozen law is kappa=1. All signs are\nnonnegative. One unit flows z->star and one flows z->p->ell->star.\nEvery reverse cycle has zero net flow. Thus (II.2) has exactly terminal\nflow 2, with no incoming terminal or optimum edge. In particular it\ncontains Q_(n-2,0) with a strictly positive coefficient.\n\nThe complete original-row expression is\n\n    M_II(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+2Q_(n,z)+Q_(z,star)\n      +sum_p t_p[Q_(z,p)+sum_(ell<p)omega_(p,ell)\n                                      (Q_(p,ell)+Q_(ell,star))]\n      +kappa*sum_(p,ell<p)t_p*omega_(p,ell)\n                                      (Q_(p,ell)+Q_(ell,p)). (II.3)\n\nThere are no omitted charges in this expression. Each finite row is its\noriginal ordered interpolation row. Equations (II.1)--(II.3) are a\ncoefficient law, not an instruction to solve for unknown PSD blocks.\n\n### 3.1 Complete quadratic, including all independent prefix gradients\n\nSet W_(p,ell)=t_p*omega_(p,ell). Eliminating only g_n gives\nM_II(q)=||g_n+(b-1)g_z||^2+R_II, where\n\n    R_II=q||x_0||^2-<x_0,g_z+sum_(ell<z)mu_ell*g_ell>\n       +(2-(b-1)^2)||g_z||^2+sum_p t_p||g_p||^2\n       +sum_(ell<z)mu_ell||g_ell||^2\n       -sum_p t_p<g_z,g_p>-sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)<g_p,g_ell>\n       +<g_z,sum_(i<z)h_i*g_i>\n       +sum_p t_p<g_z,sum_(p<=i<z)h_i*g_i>\n       +sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)<g_p,sum_(ell<=i<p)h_i*g_i>\n       +sum_(ell<z)mu_ell<g_ell,sum_(i<ell)h_i*g_i>\n       +kappa*sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)\n          [||g_ell-g_p||^2\n                  -<g_ell-g_p,sum_(ell<=i<p)h_i*g_i>]. (II.4)\n\nThe formula follows by expanding the four types of prefix rows in\n(II.3). For example, the two half-squared-difference contributions at p\ngive t_p||g_p||^2; the two at each leaf give mu_ell||g_ell||^2.\nBoth original displacements of each reverse cycle remain in the last line.\nNo common-gradient, terminal or initial-point relation is assumed.\n\n### 3.2 A two-checkpoint zero-displacement counterdirection\n\nFor a queried checkpoint r<p put\nOmega_p(r)=sum_(ell in Leaf,ell<=r)omega_(p,ell). On the full original\nsubspace x_0=0, every leaf gradient zero and g_n=0, (II.4) becomes\n\n    (1+kappa)[sum_p t_p||g_p||^2\n       +sum_(r<p in Check)t_p*h_r*Omega_p(r)<g_r,g_p>]. (II.5)\n\nThis is a necessary test of the full matrix, not a claim that checkpoint\ncompression is lossless. All omitted formal coordinates are explicitly\nset to zero only to exhibit a negative direction.\n\nChoose r=2^(m-2)-1 and p=3*2^(m-2)-1, the roots of the two children of\nthe whole word. Both physical steps equal H=1+a*c^(m-2), and t_r=t_p.\nThere are 2^(m-3) earlier leaves at or before r and 3*2^(m-3) before p.\nEvery raw weight is at least 1/m and less than 2, so\n\n    Omega_p(r)>1/(6m).\n\nAlso H>2^(m-2)/3>12m. For the latter comparison, 2^(m-2)>36m at\nm=16 and doubling preserves it thereafter. Consequently H*Omega_p(r)>2.\nSet the complete original vector to\n\n    x_0=0, g_r=1, g_p=-1, all other g_i=0, including g_n. (II.6)\n\nIts physical displacement is H-H=0. Equation (II.5) gives\n\n    M_II(q)=(1+kappa)t_p[2-H*Omega_p(r)]<0.           (II.7)\n\nThis holds for EVERY real q and EVERY kappa>=0. Paying a larger common\nreverse-cycle factor cannot repair the law.\n\nMore generally, for arbitrary positive checkpoint masses summing to 1 and receiver\nprobabilities with the same routing support and one common kappa, the\ntwo-coordinate determinant in (II.5) requires\n\n    h_r*Omega_p(r) <= 2*sqrt(t_r/t_p), r<p.          (II.8)\n\nIndeed its diagonal entries are (1+kappa)t_r and (1+kappa)t_p, and its\noff-diagonal entry is (1+kappa)t_p*h_r*Omega_p(r)/2. Thus comparable\ncheckpoint degrees need receiver leakage at most a constant divided by\nthe earlier physical step. The displayed raw kernel has only an inverse-m\nlower bound on that leakage, which is too large. This requirement alone\ndoes not certify any more localized law; all other directions remain.\n\n## 4. One global initial/common charge and the exact terminal account\n\nBoth laws have no incoming terminal row and lambda_(n,z)=2. Directly,\n\n    -2f_n+2Q_(n,z)\n       =||g_n+(b-1)g_z||^2-2f_z+[1-(b-1)^2]||g_z||^2. (4.1)\n\nThus the true terminal is free, with center -(b-1)g_z. Nothing identifies\nit with the last queried gradient. The entire original prefix remains.\n\nFor either law let mu_i=lambda_(i,star), finite K_ij=lambda_ij, and h_n=0\nonly for notation. There are no incoming optimum rows and sum mu_i=2.\nThe complete polynomial is q||x_0||^2-sum mu_i<x_0,g_i>+g^T C g, with\n\n    C_ii=mu_i+sum_v K_iv-1_(i=n)-h_i*sum_(v>i)K_iv,\n    2C_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j*sum_(v>j)K_iv\n                      +h_i[mu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. (4.2)\n\nTo check this formula, the incident half-squared-gradient terms contribute\nmu_i+sum_v K_iv-1_(i=n), by flow; each forward row out of i contributes\n-h_i on that diagonal. For i<j the squared differences give the first\nterm, forward rows from i crossing j the second, and rows from j reaching\nindex <=i or star the third. This accounts for every original coordinate.\n\nEliminate g_n using (4.1), obtaining the explicit prefix matrix\nCpre=C_(0:z,0:z)-(b-1)^2 e_z e_z^T, where e_z selects coordinate z.\nLet g_i=G+v_i on the queried prefix, with v_z=0. Define\n\n    u=sum_(i<=z)mu_i*v_i, k=e^T Cpre v, T=v^T Cpre v,\n\nwhere e is the all-ones prefix column. The coefficients Cpre,mu are\nentirely specified by the two literal arrays and (4.2). Substitution of\nall prefix gradients G gives e^T Cpre e=A. Therefore the full prefix form\nis exactly\n\n    q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,G>+A||G||^2-<x_0,u>+2<G,k>+T. (4.3)\n\nAt q=qtar the initial/common determinant Delta=qA-1 is positive, because\nD>17b^2. Eliminating this ONE two-coordinate block charges\n\n    Charge(u,k)=[A||u||^2/4-<u,k>+q||k||^2]/(qA-1). (4.4)\n\nThis follows by inversion of [[q,-1],[-1,A]] with cross column\n(-u/2,k). No separate local charge is substituted for it. Full PSD is\nequivalent to T>=Charge for every original prefix variation v_z=0.\nAt a singular determinant a range condition would additionally be needed;\nthe advertised qtar is not singular. For Law I, k=A*u/2 by (I.3), and\n(4.4) is exactly A||u||^2/4. Its minimizing initial/common values are\nx_0=0,G=-u/2, explaining why increasing q cannot repair (I.7).\nFor Law II, (4.2) and (II.4) retain k explicitly; (II.6) already gives\na negative value before any attempted elimination. No PSD claim is left\nto an unspecified matrix problem: both full matrices have been refuted.\n\n## 5. Complete reconstruction of the E80 reset transcript\n\nFor reuse as a SCREEN only, define Rreset=a*c^2/M=beta*c/M and sbit(k)\nto be the number of ones in the binary expansion of integer k>=0. Put\n\n    v_k=(-1)^k Rreset^k c^(-sbit(k))/M, 0<=k<N.\n\nAt leaf 2k prescribe x=M v_k,g=v_k,f=(b+1/2)v_k^2. At checkpoint\n2k+1<n prescribe x=g=v_k,f=v_k^2/2. Binary carry gives\nsbit(k+1)-sbit(k)=1-v2(k+1), hence\nv_(k+1)/v_k=-a*c^(1+v2(k+1))/M. Thus the leaf step takes M v_k to v_k\nand the following actual checkpoint step takes v_k to M v_(k+1).\nEvery physical update is satisfied, x_0=1, and the final position is\n\n    t=x_n=v_(N-1)=-Rreset^(N-1)/(M*c^(m-1)).\n\nComplete the actual terminal by g_n=u=2t/(b+2), f_n=t^2/(b+2), and set\nthe optimum jet to zero. This is a scalar Gram transcript; no function is\nasserted to interpolate it. Substitution gives the full row table:\n\n    Q_(leaf(v),star)=0, Q_(star,leaf(v))=-b*v^2;\n    both optimum rows at check(w) are 0;\n    Q_(check(w),check(w'))=0;\n    Q_(leaf(v),check(w))=0;\n    Q_(check(w),leaf(v))=b*v*(w-v);\n    Q_(leaf(v),leaf(w))=b*w*(v-w);\n    Q_(n,check(w))=Q_(n,star)=Q_(star,n)=-b*u^2/4;\n    Q_(n,leaf(v))=-b*(v-u/2)^2.                       (5.1)\n\nIn particular both adjacent queried directions are nonpositive: following\na leaf has w=v, while at the preceding checkpoint the next leaf parameter\nis -d*w/M with d>0. Every E80 row is accounted for by this table. E80 means\nboth optimum orientations at every finite index, all checkpoint pairs,\nall leaf-to-checkpoint rows, adjacent checkpoint-to-leaf rows, and all\noutgoing actual-terminal rows. No leaf-to-leaf or remote checkpoint-to-\nleaf rows belonged to E80.\n\nThe growth estimate is included, rather than imported from the old draft.\nSince M=lambda-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],\n\n    Rreset-1=epsilon[2/(lambda(1+epsilon))-lambda*epsilon]/M\n              >epsilon/8=1/(32m).\n\nFor the strict bound, epsilon<1/8 and lambda<5/2 make the bracket exceed\n32/45-5/16>5/16, while M<5/2. For m>=16, induction gives\n2^(m-1)-1>=96m^2: the base is 32767>=24576 and the induction difference\n96m^2-192m-95 is positive. Hence N-1>32m(2m+1).\nThe binomial inequality (1+1/(32m))^(32m)>=2 yields\nRreset^(N-1)>2^(2m+1). Because M*c^(m-1)<2^(2m), we have t<-2.\n\nThis transcript is explicitly not interpolable. At pbad=n-2 the checkpoint\nparameter w=v_(N-2)=-M*t/beta>2M, whereas v_0=1/M. Consequently\n\n    Q_(pbad,0)=b*v_0*(w-v_0)>0, Q_(0,pbad)=0.        (5.2)\n\nLaw I omits this row. Law II includes it with positive weight. The new\nfailure below is insufficient paid action even though that violated row\nis now present. Neither (5.1) nor (5.2) is a lower bound on unrestricted W.\n\n### 5.1 A symbolic tail bound; no trajectory or recurrence was executed\n\nFor k=N-1-j, binary complementation gives exactly\n\n    |v_k/t|=Rreset^(-j)c^sbit(j), 1<=j<N.\n\nSince an integer with r binary ones is at least 2^r-1,\nc^(2sbit(j))<=(j+1)^4. Put rreset=Rreset^(-2). The previous lower bound\non Rreset gives 1-rreset>=1/(64m): with zeta=1/(32m)<1, use\n1-(1+zeta)^(-2)>=zeta/2.\nFor every integer d>=1,\nj^d<=d!*binom(j+d-1,d), and summing the geometric-series identity gives\nsum_(j>=1)j^d r^j<=d!/(1-r)^(d+1), 0<r<1. This identity follows by\nmultiplying d+1 geometric series and counting compositions, or by d\nsuccessive differentiations of their convergent series. Therefore\n\n    sum_(k<N-1)v_k^2/t^2 <=768(64m)^5=:Moment5,\n    sum_(p=2k+1 in Check)h_p*v_k^2/t^2\n                                  <=92160(64m)^7=:Moment7. (5.3)\n\nFor the second bound, v2(k+1)=v2(j), so\nh_p=1+beta*c^v2(j)<=1+j^2<=2j^2. Apply the d=6 inequality to\n2j^2(j+1)^4<=128j^6. For the first use (j+1)^4<=16j^4; the displayed\nconstant 768 is a harmless enlargement of 16*4!.\n\nAll coarse exponential comparisons used below follow from\n\n    2^(m-1)>2^24(64m)^7, m>=1536.                    (5.4)\n\nAt m=1536, m<2^11 makes the right side less than 2^143, whereas the\nleft side is 2^1535. The ratio of the left side to m^7 increases at each\ninteger increment, since (1+1/m)^7<sum_(j>=0)(7/m)^j<2 for m>=1536.\nThis proves (5.4) for all relevant m by induction. This is symbolic\nreasoning, not a generated arithmetic table or executed recurrence.\n\n## 6. Complete reset screen for both frozen laws\n\nOn (5.1), Law I has exact value\n\n    M_I(q)=q-2t^2/(b+2)-2b*(t-u/2)^2\n      +b*sum_(earlier leaves ell=2k)\n           [r81_ell*v_k*(t-v_k)-w81_ell*(t-v_k)^2]\n      +b*sum_(checkpoints p=2k+1)w81_p*t*(v_k-t).      (6.1)\n\nEvery selected original row appears here: outgoing optimum rows vanish,\nlast-leaf-to-checkpoint rows vanish, leaf pairs and both other orientations\nare evaluated by (5.1). For the frozen eta=1/(4D), w81_p<=2eta. Use\nv(t-v)<=t^2/4 and t(v-t)<=v^2/4, and sum r81_ell<2. Formula (5.3) and\n(5.4) give\n\n    last sum in (6.1) <=(b*eta/2)Moment5*t^2 <t^2/8.\n\nIndeed its coefficient is at most 96b(64m)^5/D, which is less than 1/8\nby b<3/2, D>2^m and (5.4). The leaf relay contribution is at most\nb*t^2/2 and its symmetric-pair part is nonpositive. Finally\n\n    2/(b+2)+2b*(b+1)^2/(b+2)^2-b/2\n       >4/7+7b/18 >121/126.\n\nHere (b+1)/(b+2)>2/3 and b>1. Thus\n\n    M_I(qtar)<qtar-(3/4)t^2<0.                       (6.2)\n\nThe inequality 121/126-1/8>3/4 and t^2>4 justify the displayed margin.\nThis reset conclusion is for the frozen eta law. The broader arbitrary-\ntheta contact family is excluded instead by (I.7), without a claim that\nevery such theta has the same sign on the reset transcript.\n\nFor Law II with kappa=1, the EXACT corresponding expression is\n\n    M_II(q)=q-2t^2/(b+2)-2b*(t-u/2)^2\n       +2b*sum_(p=2k+1)t_p*sum_(ell=2s<p)\n                         omega_(p,ell)*v_s*(v_k-v_s). (6.3)\n\nThe reverse leaf-to-checkpoint rows are zero, as are the source-to-\ncheckpoint and outgoing optimum rows. The displayed 2 is the actual\n1+kappa forward weight. Since v_s(v_k-v_s)<=v_k^2/4, the last sum is\nat most (b/2)sum_p t_p*v_k^2. The total checkpoint mass satisfies\nTc>=h_(2^(m-1)-1)>2^(m-1)/3. Equations (5.3)--(5.4) prove\n\n    sum_p t_p*v_k^2/t^2 <=Moment7/Tc<1/4.\n\nTherefore\n\n    M_II(qtar)<qtar-[2/(b+2)-b/8]*t^2\n                       <qtar-t^2/4<0.              (6.4)\n\nWe used 2/(b+2)-b/8>4/7-3/16>1/4 and qtar<1. The actual negative\nterminal-row cost was retained in (6.3) and only dropped for this upper\nbound. The included remote rows are too weak under this global degree\nnormalization. Their mere presence does not pay the required reset cost.\n\n## 7. The older two-gradient deep waves\n\nFor 2<=j<=m-3 set p=2^j-1, H=h_p, x_0=0, g_0=-H/b, g_p=1, and every\nother original gradient, including g_n, zero. The physical displacement\nis exactly zero. This is a formal full-space screen only.\n\nFor the frozen Law I, direct substitution in (I.3) gives\n\n    M_I=(r81_0+w81_0)H^2/b^2-(H-1)(r81_p+w81_p).           (7.1)\n\nBoth J(0,z) and J(p,z) equal m. Define, only for this calculation,\n\n    F(P)=(2-2eta/m)/(2S-b^2-P)+(2eta/m)/(S-b-P).\n\nThen r81_i+w81_i=h_i F(P81_i) for i=0,p, and F is strictly increasing.\nFor j>=3, H>4 and b/(b-1)<4; using F(P81_p)>F(0) in (7.1) gives\n\n    M_I<F(0)H[H/b-(H-1)]<0.                        (7.2)\n\nAt j=2, H=1+beta*c and P81_p=2b+1+beta<6. Now\nb-(b-1)H=1-beta^2>epsilon. Also\n\n    [F(P81_p)-F(0)]/F(0) <= P81_p/(S-b-P81_p).\n\nIt follows from (7.1) that M_I>0 if\n15/(S-8)<1/(6m), which follows from S>90m+8. This last inequality\nholds since S>2^m-1 and m>=1536 (the ratio 2^m/m increases for m>1,\nand the asserted comparison already holds at m=16).\nThus Law I passes exactly this j=2 screen and fails all j>=3 in its range.\n\nLaw II with kappa=1 passes every one of these old deep waves. Write\n\n    L_le=sum_(r in Check,r<=p)t_r*omega_(r,0),\n    L_gt=sum_(r in Check,r>p)t_r*omega_(r,0).\n\nIts exact value is\n\n    M_II=[(2-b)L_le+2L_gt]*H^2/b^2\n                 +2t_p-t_p*omega_(p,0)*(2b-3)*H/b>0. (7.3)\n\nTo derive it, the routed base contributes\n(L_le+L_gt)H^2/b^2+t_p-t_p*omega_(p,0)(b-1)H/b.\nFor a reverse cycle ending before p the interval displacement is b*g_0;\nfor one ending after p it is zero. The cycle at p contributes the\nremaining mixed term with coefficient b-2 and its checkpoint diagonal.\nAdding these gives (7.3). Its signs follow from b<3/2 and t_p>0.\nThe new equal-scale wave (II.6) still refutes its full matrix.\n\n## 8. The older H4 positive/negative block direction\n\nSet x_0=0, g_i=1 at i=0,1,2,3, g_i=-1 at i=8,9,10,11, and all other\noriginal gradients zero, including g_n. The two active four-query blocks\nhave identical physical mass, so displacement is zero. Put\n\n    H1=1+beta, H2=1+beta*c,\n    P2=b+H1, B4=2b+H1+H2, C4=b+H2.\n\nHere P2<7/2, B4<9, C4>4. All active original prefix masses P81_i are\nbelow 100, their total active step mass is 2B4<18 (in fact <17), and\nall J(i,z)=m.\n\nFor Law I put k0=(2-2eta/m)/(2S-b^2) and l0=(2eta/m)/(S-b).\nFreeze r81_i=k0 h_i,w81_i=l0 h_i only within this calculation. Zero physical\ndisplacement makes the exact frozen polynomial\n\n    (k0+l0)/2 * sum_active h_i(2-h_i).\n\nThe sum is twice\n2b(2-b)+(1+beta)(1-beta)+(1+beta*c)(1-beta*c), which is less than -1:\nthe first term is <=2, the second <1/4, and the third <1-(21/10)^2.\nThus the full sum is <-2 and the frozen value is <-(k0+l0).\n\nThe actual coefficients differ by at most\n|r81_i-k0 h_i|<=200 k0 h_i/S and\n|w81_i-l0 h_i|<=200 l0 h_i/S for each active i, since P81_i<100 and S>400.\nIn the polynomial (I.3) with g_z=x_0=0, the factor multiplying each\nr81_i or w81_i has absolute value at most 18: |g_i|<=1 and the total\nactive step mass is <17. Thus the total perturbation is at most\n61200(k0+l0)/S<(k0+l0)/2, since S>2^18. Consequently M_I<0.\n\nFor Law II use its exact routed expression (II.3), setting all values\nf_i=0 after their cancellation. Checkpoints p<=13 contribute at most\n4000/Tc in absolute value. To verify this bound, there are seven such\ncheckpoints, h_p<8, every formal position has absolute value <9,\neach finite row has absolute quadratic value <=20, and each optimum\nrow <=19/2. The bracket\nQ_(z,p)+sum omega[2Q_(p,ell)+Q_(ell,p)+Q_(ell,star)]\nhas absolute value <=70. Summing t_p<=56/Tc gives less than 4000/Tc.\n\nFor p>=15, g_p=0 and its ancestor height J(p,ell) is the SAME for all\nfour active leaves ell=0,2,8,10. Every such p is outside the cell\n{0,...,14} containing them. The full bracket equals\n\n    2*sum_active_leaves omega_(p,ell)*L_ell,\n    (L_0,L_2,L_8,L_10)=(1,1+P2,1-B4,1-B4+P2).\n\nSince raw_(p,ell)=(2p+1-ell)/[(p+1)J], its numerator before the positive\ndenominator (p+1)J Z_p is\n\n    N_p=8p-16+6P2+(16-4p)C4 <69-8p <=-3(p+1), p>=15.\n\nThis uses C4>4 and P2<7/2. Hence every such checkpoint contributes\nnegatively. At the whole root p0=2^(m-1)-1, J=m and Z_p0<=p0+1,\nso its contribution is at most\n\n    -6h_p0/[Tc*m*(p0+1)].\n\nThis one term already dominates 4000/Tc. Indeed\nh_p0/[m(p0+1)]>(7/16)(6/5)^(m-2)/m>4000.\nAt m=1536 use (6/5)^4>2 and 383 blocks of four powers, which give a\nlower bound exceeding 2^383 before division by m and multiplication by\n7/16. Thereafter (6/5)^(m-2)/m increases since m>5. Therefore M_II<0.\nThis is an all-depth analytical sign proof for the actual first-block\nembedding, not a finite numeric H4 experiment or a statement about all\nfour-block embeddings.\n\n## 9. What is excluded, omitted rows, and the remaining interface\n\nLaw I fails for every independent theta_i in [0,1] satisfying its\ndefinition, even though the exact optimum distribution and all remote\nfan weights are allowed to vary. The obstruction uses its deliberately\nbalanced contact equation (I.4). It does not exclude a different contact\nrelation, a different terminal distribution, or added independent rows.\nOne omitted potentially relevant row is Q_(n-2,0), explicitly positive\non the reset transcript. Adding it would define a different law, not\nrepair (I.2) within its parameters.\n\nLaw II fails for the literal LCA/position kernel (II.1), every common\nkappa>=0, and degrees t_p proportional to h_p. The necessary condition\n(II.8) applies to the specified routed support with arbitrary degrees\nand receiver probabilities, but is only one necessary two-coordinate\ntest. It is not an enlarged-support impossibility theorem. For example,\nthe omitted direct checkpoint pair Q_(p,r), with r,p from (II.6), and\nits reverse are not in (II.2). Either could alter that two-coordinate\nmatrix; neither is presumed to be a free PSD repair. Non-last leaf-to-\nleaf rows and nonuniform reverse-cycle amounts are also outside this law.\n\nThe concrete failure mechanisms are different. In Law I, exact contact\nbalance combines a large optimum charge at a long-step coordinate with\nnegative forward-pair diagonal costs. In Law II, a low-decay ancestor\ntail creates a mixed term of size h_r Omega_p(r) between equally weighted\ncheckpoint coordinates. Increasing a common reverse-cycle coefficient\nscales the offending matrix. Its normalization by the whole checkpoint\nmass also underpays the reset transcript despite including its known\nviolated row. Passing the older two-gradient waves does not address this.\n\nAn unresolved full-function construction must pay the reset while\ncontrolling all such ancestor-crossing mixed terms, and must also prove\nthe complete remainder after the single charge (4.4) and actual terminal\nsquare (4.1). Merely requiring unknown blocks to be PSD restates the\nmissing theorem. We give no third coefficient law or automatic next round.\n\nNo positive primal candidate is asserted. Consequently no static function,\nminimum set, closest radius or attained W lower bound is attributed to any\nof the formal negative directions or to the incompatible reset transcript.\nThe original unrestricted exact-profile minimax question remains OPEN.\n\n## 10. Matlas was\nqueried once for long-step interpolation/PSD certificates; the returned\nbounded-step and unrelated results do not supply the missing mechanism.\nOne web call located primary background. {"id": "8e4a0d40a049c892", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T18:36:45.852024+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "JOINT UNVERIFIED scope example in max2r72: four unequal positive heights on a full regular rank2 Walsh cone simplex give a legal actual first-leaf edge-to-three-generator-face transition, unequal gradient norms, moving vertical coordinate, and strict outgoing-optimum slack at the true closest initial minimizer. Thus none of those features by itself escapes the conic whole-word product obstruction.", "evidence": "This is a component of the SINGLE joint unverified report21d26350c9141917, not an independently verified premise. Its body is F_D(X)=max_(g in D)(<X,g>-||g||^2/2), D=conv(0,M u_s), u_s=(((-1)^(t dot s))_(t!=0),k_s), in real R^4. M=1+2||ell0||r0 product_i max(1,h_i-1), with ell0 the unique vector satisfying <ell0,u_s>=1 and r0=(3-delta)/sqrt(3+delta^2). This explicit dilation preserves the cone and puts each actual conic projection strictly below the finite cap by the distance argument supplied in the full joint report. The complete minimum is {X:<X,u_s><=0 all s}. Direct body-normal proofs of smoothness and the full-word scale guard are included in that joint evidence.\n\n## 10. A genuine changing-face member with strict closest-optimum slack\n\nThis is an explicit member of Law II, used to test the scope of its\nobstruction. It is not a third law and is not advertised as a useful\nwhole-word witness. It refutes the shortcuts that Law II freezes its\ndirection or silently makes all outgoing optimum rows tight.\n\nTake r=2,N=4,b=R=A=1. Label the four vertices0,1,2,3 and put\n\n    delta=a/100,\n    (k_0,k_1,k_2,k_3)=(delta,2delta,3delta,4delta).\n\nAll dependent character weights equal1 and all four heights differ.\nOne has0<a<1/2 because a<1/lambda<1/2, so0<delta<1/200.\nUse the actual cap scale M prescribed in (7.4), with these parameters.\n\nThe initial cone gradient and the first ACTUAL leaf update are\n\n    theta=(3-delta)/(3+delta^2)>0,\n    g_0=theta u_0,\n    h_0=1+a,\n    X_1=X_0-(1+a)theta u_0.\n\nFor J={1,2,3}, put k=(2,3,4)^T delta and define\n\n    T=(1+a)theta-1,\n    U=1+(1+a)theta delta,\n    v=(11,12,13)^T delta/4,\n    C=55 delta^2/2.\n\nThe vector of scores U_J^T X_1 is T 1-U k. The exact face Gram is\n\n    Gamma_J=4I-11^T+kk^T.\n\nThe inverse of 4I-11^T is (I+11^T)/4 (multiply the matrices to check),\nso its product with k is v and k^T v=C. Substitution into Gamma_J alpha\n=T1-U k yields the explicit solution\n\n    alpha=T 1-v (U+9delta T)/(1+C).                      (10.1)\n\nThis identity can be checked by multiplying the displayed matrices;\nno numerical system solve or unverified inverse formula is required.\nWe now check the signs analytically. Since\n\n    T=a-(1+a)delta(1+delta)/(3+delta^2),\n\none has a/2<T<a. Also0<U<2 and U+9delta T<3, using a<1/2 and\ndelta<1/200. Each component subtracted in (10.1) is less than\n39delta/4=39a/400<a/4. Thus every alpha_s>a/4>0.\n\nLet g_1=U_J alpha and r_1=X_1-g_1. The three J-normal equations are\nr_1 dot u_s=0. Its vertical coordinate is strictly negative, because\nX_1 has vertical coordinate -1-(1+a)theta delta<0 and the vertical\ncoordinate of U_J alpha is positive. The sum of ALL four base columns is\n(0,10delta), hence\n\n    <r_1,u_0>=10delta (r_1)_vertical<0.                  (10.2)\n\nThis is the sole remaining remote cone guard; together with alpha>0\nit proves g_1 is the exact projection. The already proved whole-word\nscale guard gives coefficient sum/M<1/2 for both g_0 and g_1. Thus g_0\nis in the interior of the edge from0 to M u_0, whereas g_1 is in the\nrelative interior of the face conv(0,M u_1,M u_2,M u_3). Neither query\nis a vertex. Their directions differ because the four base columns are\nlinearly independent. Their norms differ since0<||g_1||<=a||g_0||<||g_0||.\n\nThe closest initial minimizer of (7.1) has\n\n    <X_star,u_j>=-gamma0 delta(1+3(j+1))<0, j=1,2,3.\n\nConsequently <X_star,g_1><0. The outgoing optimum row is strictly slack\nabout the TRUE closest minimum, not only about the more distant (0,-1).\nThe vertical coordinate moved during the first ordinary step. Nevertheless\nthe actual ENTIRE trajectory still satisfies (9.3)--(9.4).\n\nThis example shows why strict outgoing-optimum slack plus a legal local\nnoncommuting or changing-face transition is not itself a terminal gain.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"proof_attempt": "21d26350c9141917", "status": "JOINT UNVERIFIED; not a verified fact", "mechanism": "member of Law II, not a third law"}, "glossary": {"F_D": "The single convex smooth projection potential defined in Section2.", "D": "The actual finite cone simplex conv(0,v_s); distinct from scalar D_m.", "K": "The closed cone generated by the body's nonzero vertices; distinct from K_n.", "Z": "The complete minimum set K^polar with nonpositive inner-product convention.", "delta_i": "Normal height <X_i-g_i,g_i>; distinct from outgoing optimum slack.", "X_star": "The explicitly proved closest initial minimizer for the respective law.", "E": "Actual terminal score 2 F_D(X_n)/dist(X_0,Z)^2.", "k_s": "Nonnegative vertical height at label s; actual scaled height M k_s in Law II.", "chi_t": "Real Walsh character s -> (-1)^(t dot s).", "ell": "The real linear coefficient-sum functional vector from Fourier inversion.", "ell0": "The coefficient-sum vector for Law II base generators.", "Gamma": "Literal real vertex Gram; Gamma^0 denotes the base Gram.", "r0": "Law II exact initial closest distance.", "M": "Law II explicit common dilation of all generator vectors.", "Q": "The unevaluated whole-word product of max(1,h_i-1).", "pi_i": "The product of h_t-1 over the actual prefix t<i.", "F_ray_n": "The complete accepted alternating-ray lower-bound channel from fact 1fca3e5dd555cb4a.", "J_i": "The unique positive generator support of the actual cone projection at X_i."}}
{"id": "3eeb3aa0eb6773bf", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T18:37:29.594818+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "JOINT UNVERIFIED actual signed-energy obstruction: |x_n+S_m*g_n|<=|x_0| fails for every SAME-m hierarchy m>=1536 on a static asymmetric unit-core clipped function, even though its actual terminal objective score remains below1/D_m.", "evidence": "All claims in this counterexample are part of joint draft72141426b777f11c, not accepted facts.\n\n## 2. Same-enclosing-m word and all parameter guards\n\nFix any integer m>=1536 ONCE. Define\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    d_j=a*c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),\n    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, D_j=1+2*S_j=2*A_j-1.\n\nAll children retain this exact m,epsilon,beta,c,a. H_j has2^j-1 steps.\nThe largest PHYSICAL bridge parameter is d_*=d_(m-1); d_m is only\nan auxiliary ruler. Let ell=1+a be the first and last physical step,\nand b=a*beta. Every step is greater than1, and every bridge of level\nat least1 is immediately preceded and followed by a leaf ell. This\nlast property follows inductively from the literal concatenation;\nthere are no adjacent nonleaf bridges.\n\nHere epsilon<=1/6144<1/100, beta>99/100, and\n\n    2<c<(49/20)*(101/100)<5/2,\n    1/3<99/250<a<1/lambda<1/2,\n    0<b<1/2, c*b=beta^2<1.\n\nThe c bound uses sqrt(2)<29/20. Set eta=a/(c-2). Since\nlambda*(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda, one has c*(c-2)>1 and0<eta<1.\nSumming the literal word gives\n\n    A_(j+1)=2*A_j+d_j,\n    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j,\n    d_j/A_j <= c-2 <1/2,                            (2.1)\n    A_j<c^j for j>=1.\n\nThe closed expression has initial value1 and the correct update because\n2*eta+a=c*eta. The ratio in(2.1) is strictly increasing in j: its\nreciprocal is\n\n    A_j/d_j = 1/(c-2)+((1-eta)/a)*(2/c)^j.            (2.2)\n\nTwo placement guards will be used repeatedly. First,\n\n    A_j-d_j>ell for every j>=1.                      (2.3)\n\nIndeed A_j-d_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*(3-c)*c^j. Both coefficients\nare positive and both powers increase with j. At j=1 its value is\n2+a-beta>1+a=ell. In particular a*d_j<A_j-ell.\n\nSecond, for every2<=j<=m,\n\n    d_j>A_(j-1),\n    (c-1)*d_(j-1)-A_(j-1)<0.                        (2.4)\n\nAt j=2,\n\n    c*(d_2-A_1)=2*epsilon-lambda^2*epsilon^2*(1+epsilon)>0,\n\nsince lambda^2<6 and6*epsilon*(1+epsilon)<2. If d_j>A_(j-1),\nthen d_(j+1)-A_j=(c-1/c)*d_j-2*A_(j-1)>0, because\nc-1/c>lambda-1/lambda=2. The second inequality in(2.4) follows\nfrom(2.1) and(c-1)*(c-2)<(3/2)*(1/2)<1.\n\nAt the final level,\n\n    d_m^2>D_m.                                      (2.5)\n\nTo see this, a>1/3 and A_m<c^m imply d_m^2>c^(2m)/9, while\nD_m<2*c^m. Since c^m>2^m>=32>18 for m>=5, the first quantity\nexceeds the second. All advertised m satisfy this.\n\nFinally Gamma_m>d_*>1. The latter follows from d_2=beta*c>1 and\nm-1>=2. With A=A_(m-1), d=d_*, one has d<A/2 and\nD_m=4*A+2*d-1>4*A, since d>1. Thus\n2*a*D_m>(8/3)*A>d, proving Gamma_m^2>d^2. These estimates justify\nevery use of an inactive positive clipping threshold under(T).\n\n## 3. Static functions, complete queries, minima, and every row\n\nFor u,v>0 let\n\n    g(x)=clip(x,[-v,u]), f(x)=x*g(x)-g(x)^2/2.\n\nEquivalently f is x^2/2 on[-v,u], is u*x-u^2/2 on x>=u,\nand is -v*x-v^2/2 on x<=-v. Its derivative g is continuous,\nnondecreasing and1-Lipschitz. Partitioning an interval at the two\nthresholds proves the Lipschitz assertion directly. Consequently f\nis finite, convex, differentiable, globally1-smooth and has the\ncomplete minimum set{0}, with f(0)=0. For general L set F=L*f.\nThe original updates with steps h_i/L then coincide with\n\n    x_0=s, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g(x_i),\n    g_i=g(x_i), f_i=f(x_i), 0<=i<=n.                  (3.1)\n\nThese specify ALL physical queries and the original terminal on one\nfixed function. The initial radius about the complete minimum is\nexactly |s|. All theorem statements cover this full literal trajectory,\nnot an auxiliary trajectory with freely chosen gradients.\n\nLet y_i=x_i-g_i. Direct substitution of the displayed f gives every\nordered interpolation residual, including terminal and optimum,\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2\n        =y_j*(g_i-g_j)<=0.                          (3.2)\n\nIf y_j>0, then g_j=u and g_i<=u. If y_j<0, then g_j=-v and\ng_i>=-v. If y_j=0 the residual is zero. This proves both orders,\nall remote rows, threshold equalities and repeated points. At the\noptimum x=g=f=0 the same formula applies. The complete Gram matrix\nof the initial displacement and all gradients is an ordinary scalar\nEuclidean Gram: every quadratic form is the square of a linear\ncombination of these real numbers, hence is nonnegative. Scaling by\nL gives the original L-smooth residuals without changing the score.\n\nThe one-sided comparison function is\n\n    g_infty(x)=max(x,-v),\n    f_infty(x)=x^2/2 for x>=-v,\n               -v*x-v^2/2 for x<=-v.                (3.3)\n\nIt has the same regularity, complete minimum{0}, true radius |s|,\nphysical query definition(3.1), and all residuals(3.2). For this\nfunction y_j is either0 or negative, so the proof of(3.2) is unchanged.\nIts positive curvature is1, not0. All its definitions are explicit;\nno limit interchange u->infinity is invoked.\n\nFor either function f(x)<=x^2/2 everywhere. On a clipped ray the\ndifference is (|x|-b_side)^2/2. Reflection swaps u and v, changes the\nsign of every iterate, and preserves the score. We may therefore\nassume u>=v when proving(T), and reflect the complete conclusion.\n\nFor reference retain BOTH signed endpoints. For a finite-clipping\nstep1+d, the exact image of[-p,q], p,q>=0, is[-p',q'], with\n\n    p'=max(d*min(q,u), p-(1+d)*v),\n    q'=max(d*min(p,v), q-(1+d)*u).                   (3.4)\n\nThe first terms are nonnegative. The opposite-sign extremum occurs\nat the included threshold or endpoint on each ray; the remaining\nsame-sign extremum occurs at the outer endpoint. Continuity fills\nthe interval. Thus this formula includes all threshold equalities.\nIf Psi_j denotes the whole H_j map and Phi_j its signed interval\nmap, then Phi_0 is the identity and\n\n    Phi_(j+1)=Phi_j o E_(d_j) o Phi_j.               (3.5)\n\nEach Phi_j is coordinatewise nondecreasing by interval inclusion.\nAll histories in(3.5) are compatible images of one continuous map;\nthere is no independent choice of child maxima. For(3.3), the exact\none-step formula becomes\n\n    p'=max(d*q,p-(1+d)*v), q'=d*min(p,v).             (3.6)\n\nThese formulas are used as symbolic identities only. No trajectory\nor recurrence was run as a program.\n\n## 4. Mechanism1: a genuine signed-energy obstruction\n\nOne tempting ratio-free primitive envelope is\n\n    |Psi_m(s)+S_m*g(Psi_m(s))| <= |s|.               (4.1)\n\nFor this clipped function its squared left side equals the signed\nobjective/gradient expression\n\n    x_n^2+2*S_m*f_n+S_m*(S_m+1)*g_n^2,\n\nbecause f_n=x_n*g_n-g_n^2/2. Thus(4.1) retains the actual clipped\nobjective weight and is stronger than a bare radius claim. It would\nimply the desired objective bound: on either ray, maximizing\n2f(x)/(x+S_m*g(x))^2 gives1/D_m by completing the saturated-ray\nsquare, while the core ratio is1/A_m^2<=1/D_m.\n\nNevertheless(4.1) is false for every advertised m. Put\n\n    A=A_(m-1), d=d_(m-1), S=A-1,\n    choose u>0, v=d*u/A, s=A*u.                     (4.2)\n\nHere0<v<u since d/A<1/2. Every query of the first child is\nx=u*(A-P), where P is its actual prefix mass, and that child ends\nat u. The root sends u to-d*u=-A*v. Every query of the second\nchild is x=-v*(A-Q), where Q is its actual prefix mass. The ACTUAL\nterminal after its last leaf is -v. All queried saturated guards hold\nbecause both children end at their threshold and all earlier prefix\nmasses are smaller. The function, rows, complete minimum and radius\nare those of Section3, with own radius A*u.\n\nTo check the strict violation, first prove d/A>1/lambda. At level2,\n\n    lambda*d_2-A_2\n      =epsilon*(1+4/[lambda*(1+epsilon)]-lambda^2*epsilon)>0.\n\nHere lambda^2*epsilon<6/6144<1. The ratios d_j/A_j increase by(2.2),\nso the same strict comparison holds at m-1>=2. If t=d/A, then\n\n    |x_n+S_m*g_n|/|s|=A_m*d/A^2=t*(2+t)>1,           (4.3)\n\nbecause (1/lambda)*(2+1/lambda)=1. This includes the small-side\nclipping weight exactly. It is not a claim that the objective itself\nincreases beyond the target: this trajectory has score d^2/A^4, and\n\n    D_m*d^2/A^4 < (5*A-1)/(4*A^2)<1                 (4.4)\n\nsince d/A<1/2, D_m<5*A-1 and A>2. The latter inequality follows\nfrom4*A^2-5*A+1=(4*A-1)*(A-1)>0 for A>2.\n\nA second direct energy-only closure also loses necessary history.\nIf one replaces a child's actual image by the entire sublevel\n2f<=R^2/D_j, then on a saturated ray with threshold1 the permissible\nradius is (R^2/D_j+1)/2. Passing this enlarged symmetric interval\nthrough a bridge and another child objective bound yields, for large\nR, a bound with leading growth R^4/(4*D_j^3). The desired parent\nbound grows only as R^2/D_(j+1). For sufficiently large R the former\ncannot prove the latter. This is an obstruction to that enlargement,\nnot an actual trajectory counterexample: the omitted affine prefix\nhistory is precisely the missing information.\n\nMechanism1 is therefore not promoted to a uniform invariant. The next\nmechanism keeps threshold-conditioned affine exterior branches and\nexact signed interval capture, rather than assuming(4.1).\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"joint_report": "72141426b777f11c", "target_claim": "Ratio-free primitive energy (x_n+S_m*g_n)^2<=x_0^2", "status": "refuted_in_joint_unverified_draft", "formal_calls": 0, "original_target": "OPEN"}, "glossary": {"H_j": "Local SAME-enclosing-m physical hierarchy H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j);
